(\zeta_{2}\otimes 1)u_{2}^{*}\in M_{2}\overline{\otimes}M_{3}
and
(M_{1}\overline{\otimes}M_{3})\cap(M_{2}\overline{\otimes}M_{3})=\mathbb{C}1\overline{\otimes}M_{3}
, it follows that we can find
\zeta_{3}\in M_{3}
such that

(\zeta_{1}^{*}\otimes 1)u_{1}=(\zeta_{2}\otimes 1)u_{2}^{*}=1\otimes\zeta_{3}.

In conclusion,
u_{1}=\zeta_{1}\otimes\zeta_{3}
and
u_{2}=\zeta_{2}\otimes\zeta_{3}^{*}
, which finishes the proof.
∎

2.6.
Graphs

A graph
\Gamma
is called
simple
if it is undirected and contains no loops and no multiple edges between any pair of vertices.
We write
v\in\Gamma
to mean that
v
is a
vertex
of
\Gamma
. We say that two vertices
v,w\in\Gamma
are
adjacent
if they share an edge.
We write
\Lambda\subset\Gamma
to mean that
\Lambda
is a
full subgraph
of
\Gamma
: the vertices of
\Lambda
are a subset of the vertices of
\Gamma
and two vertices in
\Lambda
are adjacent if and only if they are adjacent in
\Gamma
.

The
link
and
star
of a vertex
v\in\Gamma
are defined by

\text{lk}(v)=\{w\in\Gamma\mid v
and
w
are adjacent
\}
and
\text{st}(v)=\{v\}\cup\text{lk}(v)
.

For a full subgraph
\Lambda\subset\Gamma
, we define

\text{$\Lambda^{\perp}=\bigcap_{v\in\Lambda}\text{lk}(v)=\{w\in\Gamma\mid w$ is adjacent to every vertex $v\in\Lambda\}$}.

We make the convention that
\emptyset^{\perp}=\Gamma
and note that
\text{lk}(v)=\{v\}^{\perp}
, for every vertex
v\in\Gamma
.

A
join decomposition

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}\circ\Gamma_{2}
of a graph
\Gamma
means that
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\subset\Gamma
are disjoint full subgraphs such that
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}\cup\Gamma_{2}
and
\Gamma_{2}=\Gamma_{1}^{\perp}
.
We say that
\Gamma
is a
join graph
if it admits a join decomposition
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}\circ\Gamma_{2}
, with
\Gamma_{1}
and
\Gamma_{2}
nonempty. We say that
\Gamma
is
irreducible
if it is not a join graph.
A graph
\Gamma
is called a
clique
if it is a complete graph.
The
maximal clique factor
of
\Gamma
is the clique
\Gamma_{0}\subset\Gamma
whose vertices are
those
v\in\Gamma
such that
\Gamma=\text{st}(v)
. Note that
\Gamma=\Gamma_{0}\circ\Gamma_{0}^{\perp}
and
\Gamma_{0}
is the maximal clique with this property (which may be empty).
Moreover,
\Gamma_{0}^{\perp}
has an empty maximal clique factor, and decomposes as
\Gamma_{0}^{\perp}=\Gamma_{1}\circ\cdots\circ\Gamma_{n}
, where
\Gamma_{i}
is an irreducible graph with at least two vertices, for every
1\leq i\leq n
.

Following
[
EH24
, Definition 6.1]
, we say that
\Lambda
is a
collapsible
full subgraph of
\Gamma
if
\text{st}(v)\cap(\Gamma\setminus\Lambda)=\Lambda^{\perp}
, for every
v\in\Lambda
.
A graph
\Gamma
is called
strongly reduced
if it contains no proper collapsible full subgraph
\Lambda\subsetneq\Gamma
on at least two vertices. It is
clique-reduced
if it contains no proper collapsible complete full subgraph on at least two vertices
[
EH24
, Section 9.1]
.

A vertex
v\in\Gamma
is
untransvectable
if there is no vertex
w\neq v
in
\Gamma
such that
\mathrm{lk}(v)\subset\mathrm{st}(w)
. A finite simple graph is
transvection-free
if all its vertices are untransvectable. In general, we denote by
\Gamma^{u}
the full subgraph of
\Gamma
whose vertices are the untransvectable vertices of
\Gamma
.

Remark 2.12
.

In this remark, we justify two basic graph-theoretic claims made in the introduction.

(1)

Let
\Gamma
be a finite simple graph with girth at least
5
and no vertices of valence less than
2
.
Then
\Gamma
is transvection-free. Assume by contradiction that
\text{lk}(v)\subset\text{st}(v^{\prime})
, for
v,v^{\prime}\in\Gamma
with
v\not=v^{\prime}
.
Since
|\text{lk}(v)|\geq 2
, we can choose distinct vertices
v_{1},v_{2}\in\text{lk}(v)
. Then
v_{1},v_{2}\in\text{st}(v^{\prime})
.
If
v^{\prime}
is equal to
v_{1}
or
v_{2}
,
then
v,v_{1},v_{2}
is a triangle. Otherwise, the distinct vertices
v,v_{1},v^{\prime},v_{2}
are connected in this order. Either case contradicts the fact that
\Gamma
has girth at least
5
.

(2)

Let
\Gamma
be a finite simple graph which is transvection-free, contains no squares.
Then every connected component of
\Gamma
is strongly reduced. To see this, consider a component
\Gamma_{0}
of
\Gamma
and a collapsible full subgraph
\Lambda\subset\Gamma_{0}
with
|\Lambda|\geq 2
.
Note that
\Lambda
is not a clique, for otherwise we would have that
\text{st}(v)=\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}
, for every
v\in\Lambda
, which contradicts the assumption that
\Gamma
is transvection-free since
|\Lambda|\geq 2
.

If
\Lambda^{\perp}=\emptyset
, then since
\Lambda
is collapsible, we get that
\Lambda
is disconnected from
\Gamma_{0}\setminus\Lambda
. Since
\Gamma_{0}
is connected, we deduce that
\Lambda=\Gamma_{0}
, as desired.
Thus, it remains to argue that we cannot have
\Lambda^{\perp}\not=\emptyset
. If
\Lambda^{\perp}\not=\emptyset
, then
\Lambda^{\perp}
is not a clique, for otherwise we could have that
\text{st}(v)\subset\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}\subset\text{st}(w)
, for every
v\in\Lambda
and
w\in\Lambda^{\perp}
, which again contradicts the assumption that
\Gamma
is transvection-free.
Finally, since both
\Lambda
and
\Lambda^{\perp}
are not cliques we can find non-adjacent vertices
v_{1},v_{2}\in\Lambda
and
w_{1},w_{2}\in\Lambda^{\perp}
. But then the vertices
v_{1},w_{1},v_{2},w_{2}
determine a square, which is a contradiction.

We record the following basic fact about collapsible subgraphs.

Lemma 2.13
.

Let
\Lambda
be a finite simple graph, and assume that every connected component of
\Lambda
is strongly reduced. Let
\Delta\subset\Lambda
be a collapsible subgraph with
|\Delta|\geq 2
.

Then
\Delta
is a union of connected components of
\Lambda
.

Proof.

Let
\Omega
be a connected component of
\Delta
, and assume towards a contradiction that
\Omega\cap\Delta\neq\emptyset
and
\Omega\cap\Delta\neq\Omega
. Then
\Omega\cap\Delta
and
\Omega\setminus(\Delta\cap\Omega)
are two non-empty subgraphs of the connected graph
\Omega
, so there must be an edge joining
\Omega\cap\Delta
to
\Omega\setminus(\Delta\cap\Omega)
. In other words, we can find
w\in\Omega\cap\Delta
such that
\mathrm{lk}(w)\cap(\Omega\setminus\Delta)\neq\emptyset
. We have

\mathrm{lk}(w)\cap(\Omega\setminus(\Delta\cap\Omega))=\mathrm{lk}(w)\cap(\Lambda\setminus\Delta)=\Delta^{\perp},

where the first equality comes from the fact that
\Omega
is the connected component of
\Lambda
containing
w
, and the second from the fact that
\Delta
is a collapsible subgraph of
\Lambda
. It follows that
\Delta^{\perp}\neq\emptyset
, which forces
\Delta
to be contained in
\Omega
. It follows that
\Delta
is a proper collapsible subgraph of
\Omega
with
|\Delta|\geq 2
, contradicting the fact that
\Omega
is strongly reduced.
∎

2.7.
Graph product von Neumann algebras

Let
\Gamma
be a finite simple graph and
(M_{v},\tau_{v})_{v\in\Gamma}
be a collection of tracial von Neumann algebras.
In order to recall the definition of the graph product von Neumann algebra
M_{\Gamma}

[
Ml04
,
CF17
]
we label (following
[
BC24
,
BCC24
]
) alternating words in
(M_{v})_{v\in\Gamma}
using elements of the right-angled Coxeter group
\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}
associated to
\Gamma
.

Recall that
\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}
is the group generated by the vertex set of
\Gamma
, subject to the relations
v^{2}=e
, for every
v\in\Gamma
, and
vw=wv
, for every adjacent vertices
v,w\in\Gamma
.
Equivalently,
\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}
is the graph product group over
\Gamma
where each vertex group is equal to
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
. For a full subgraph
\Lambda\subset\Gamma
, we consider the natural embedding
\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda}\subset\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}
.
For
{\bf v}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}
, let
|{\bf v}|
be the length of
{\bf v}
with respect to the set of generators
\Gamma\subset\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}
. A word
{\bf v}=v_{1}\cdots v_{n}
, with
v_{1},\dots,v_{n}\in\Gamma
, is said to be
reduced
if
|{\bf v}|=n
. We say that
{\bf v}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}

starts
(respectively,
ends
) with a letter
a\in\Gamma
if
|a{\bf v}|<|{\bf v}|
(respectively,
|{\bf v}a|<|{\bf v}|
). For
v\in\Gamma
, let
\mathring{M}_{v}=\{x\in M_{v}\mid\tau_{v}(x)=0\}
.

The
graph product von Neumann algebra

(M_{\Gamma},\tau)=*_{v\in\Gamma}(M_{v},\tau_{v})
is the unique (up to isomorphism) tracial von Neumann algebra with the following properties:

(a)

M_{\Gamma}
is generated by copies of
(M_{v})_{v\in\Gamma}
such that
\tau_{|M_{v}}=\tau_{v}
, for every
v\in\Gamma
;

(b)

M_{v}
and
M_{w}
commute, whenever
v,w\in\Gamma
are adjacent vertices;

(c)

\tau(x_{1}\cdots x_{n})=0
, for every
n\geq 1
and every
x_{1}\in\mathring{M}_{v_{1}},\dots,x_{n}\in\mathring{M}_{v_{n}}
, where
v_{1},\dots,v_{n}\in\Gamma
are any vertices such that
v_{1}\cdots v_{n}
is a reduced word in
\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}
.

The reader is referred to
[
CF17
, Proposition 2.22]
for the equivalence of this definition with the other usual one given as an operator algebra on a graph product of Hilbert spaces.

Remark 2.14
.

We record here some properties of
M_{\Gamma}
(see
[
CF17
, Remark 3.23 and Theorem 3.26]
):

(1)

Whenever
\Lambda\subset\Gamma
is a full subgraph, we have a natural embedding
M_{\Lambda}\subset M_{\Gamma}
, given by identifying
M_{\Lambda}
with the von Neumann subalgebra of
M_{\Gamma}
generated by
\{M_{v}\}_{v\in\Lambda}
.

(2)

If
v,w\in\Gamma
are distinct vertices then the von Neumann algebra
M_{v}\vee M_{w}
is canonically isomorphic to
M_{v}\overline{\otimes}M_{w}
, if
v
and
w
are adjacent, and to
M_{v}*M_{w}
, otherwise.

(3)

If
v\in\Gamma
, then
M_{\Gamma}=M_{\Gamma\setminus\{v\}}*_{M_{\text{lk}(v)}}M_{\text{st}(v)}
and
M_{\text{st}(v)}=M_{\text{lk}(v)}\overline{\otimes}M_{v}.

(4)

The graph product constructions for groups and von Neumann algebras are compatible. For a collection
(G_{v})_{v\in\Gamma}
of discrete groups, let
G_{\Gamma}=*_{v,\Gamma}G_{v}
the associated graph product group. If
M_{v}=\text{L}(G_{v})
, for every
v\in\Gamma
, then we have a canonical isomorphism
\text{L}(G_{\Gamma})\cong M_{\Gamma}
.

Next, we recall a useful decomposition of
\text{L}^{2}(M_{\Gamma})
.
Let
\mathring{M}_{e}=\mathbb{C}1
, where
e\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}
is the identity element. For
{\bf v}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}
, let
{\bf v}=v_{1}\cdots v_{n}
be a reduced decomposition, for
v_{1},\dots,v_{n}\in\Gamma
. We define

\mathring{M}_{\bf{v}}:=\mathring{M}_{v_{1}}\cdots\mathring{M}_{v_{n}}=\{x_{1}\cdots x_{n}\mid x_{1}\in\mathring{M}_{v_{1}},\dots,x_{n}\in\mathring{M}_{v_{n}}\}.

Then
\mathring{M}_{\bf v}
does not depend on the choice of the reduced decomposition of
{\bf v}
. Indeed, by the normal form theorem for graph products
[
Gr90
]
,
any other reduced decomposition of
{\bf v}
is obtained by
repeating finitely many times the operation of swapping
v_{i}
and
v_{i+1}
, for some
i
with
v_{i}\in\text{lk}(v_{i+1})
, in which case
M_{v_{i}}
and
M_{v_{i+1}}
commute by (b).
Moreover,
\mathring{M}_{\bf u}\perp\mathring{M}_{\bf v}
, for distinct
{\bf u},{\bf v}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}
and
\bigoplus_{{\bf v}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}}\mathring{M}_{\bf v}

is
\|\cdot\|_{2}
-dense in
\text{L}^{2}(M_{\Gamma})
, as a consequence of (c) and (a), respectively. In other words, if
{e}_{\bf v}:\text{L}^{2}(M_{\Gamma})\rightarrow\overline{\mathring{M}_{\bf v}}^{\|\cdot\|_{2}}
denotes the orthogonal projection, for every
{\bf v}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}
, then
\sum_{{\bf v}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}}{e}_{\bf v}=1.

The rest of this subsection is devoted to basic results about graph product von Neumann algebras.

Lemma 2.15
.

Assume the above setting. Let
n\geq 1
and
\Gamma_{0},\Gamma_{1},\dots,\Gamma_{n}\subset\Gamma
be full subgraphs.
For
S\subset M_{\Gamma}
, we denote by
\emph{sp}(S)
the linear span of
S
and by
\overline{\emph{sp}}(S)
its
\|\cdot\|_{2}
-closure.
Then we have

(1)

\emph{sp}(M_{v_{1}}\cdots M_{v_{n}})=\emph{sp}(\{\mathring{M}_{\bf v}\mid{\bf v}\in\mathcal{W}_{v_{1}}\cdots\mathcal{W}_{v_{n}}\})
, for every
v_{1},\dots,v_{n}\in\Gamma
.

(2)

\overline{\emph{sp}}(M_{\Gamma_{1}}\cdots M_{\Gamma_{n}})=\overline{\emph{sp}}(\{\mathring{M}_{\bf v}\mid{\bf v}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{1}}\cdots\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{n}}\})
.

(3)

\emph{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{0}}}(\overline{\emph{sp}}(M_{\Gamma_{1}}\cdots M_{\Gamma_{n}}))=\overline{\emph{sp}}(\{\mathring{M}_{\bf v}\mid{\bf v}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{0}}\cap\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{1}}\cdots\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{n}}\})
.

Proof.

We will first prove by induction that for every
n\geq 1
we have

(2.5)

\mathring{M}_{v_{1}}\cdots\mathring{M}_{v_{n}}\subset\text{sp}(\{\mathring{M}_{\bf v}\mid{\bf v}\in\mathcal{W}_{v_{1}}\cdots\mathcal{W}_{v_{n}}\})
, for every
v_{1},\dots,v_{n}\in\Gamma
.

For
n=1
, this is obvious since
v_{1}\in\mathcal{W}_{v_{1}}
. Assume that (
2.5
) holds for some
n\geq 1
and let
v_{1},\dots,v_{n},v_{n+1}\in\Gamma
. If
{\bf v}=v_{1}\cdots v_{n+1}
is a reduced word in
\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}
, then
\mathring{M}_{v_{1}}\cdots\mathring{M}_{v_{n+1}}=\mathring{M}_{\bf v}
by the definition of
\mathring{M}_{\bf v}
. Since
{\bf v}\in\mathcal{W}_{v_{1}}\cdots\mathcal{W}_{v_{n+1}}
, (
2.5
) holds in this case. We can therefore assume that
{\bf v}
is not a reduced word. This means that we can find
1\leq i<j\leq n+1
such that
v_{i}=v_{j}
and
v_{k}\in\text{lk}(v_{i})
, for every
i<k<j
. Thus,
\mathring{M}_{v_{i}}=\mathring{M}_{v_{j}}
commutes with
\mathring{M}_{v_{k}}
, for every
i<k<j
. Using this fact, that
\mathring{M}_{v_{i}}\mathring{M}_{v_{i}}\subset M_{v_{i}}
and
M_{v_{i}}=\mathring{M}_{v_{i}}+\mathbb{C}1
, we derive that

(2.6)

\mathring{M}_{v_{1}}\cdots\mathring{M}_{v_{n+1}}\subset\mathring{M}_{v_{1}}\cdots\mathring{M}_{v_{i-1}}M_{v_{i}}\mathring{M}_{v_{i+1}}\cdots\mathring{M}_{v_{j-1}}\mathring{M}_{v_{j+1}}\cdots\mathring{M}_{v_{n}}\subset S_{1}+S_{2},

with

\begin{array}[]{l}S_{1}=\mathring{M}_{v_{1}}\cdots\mathring{M}_{v_{i+1}}\cdots\mathring{M}_{v_{j-1}}\mathring{M}_{v_{j+1}}\cdots\mathring{M}_{v_{n+1}},\\
S_{2}=\mathring{M}_{v_{1}}\cdots\mathring{M}_{v_{i-1}}\mathring{M}_{v_{i+1}}\cdots\mathring{M}_{v_{j-1}}\mathring{M}_{v_{j+1}}\cdots\mathring{M}_{v_{n+1}}.\end{array}

By the induction hypothesis, we get that
S_{1}\subset\text{sp}(\{\mathring{M}_{\bf v}\mid{\bf v}\in\mathcal{V}_{1}\})
and
S_{2}\subset\text{sp}(\{\mathring{M}_{\bf v}\mid{\bf v}\in\mathcal{V}_{2}\})
, where
\mathcal{V}_{1}=\mathcal{W}_{v_{1}}\cdots\mathcal{W}_{v_{j-1}}\mathcal{W}_{v_{j+1}}\cdots\mathcal{W}_{v_{n+1}}
and
\mathcal{V}_{2}=\mathcal{W}_{v_{1}}\cdots\mathcal{W}_{v_{i-1}}\mathcal{W}_{v_{i+1}}\cdots\mathcal{W}_{v_{j-1}}\mathcal{W}_{v_{j+1}}\cdots\mathcal{W}_{v_{n+1}}.

Since
\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathcal{V}_{2}\subset\mathcal{W}_{v_{1}}\cdots\mathcal{W}_{v_{n+1}}
, we get that
S_{1}+S_{2}\subset\text{sp}(\{\mathring{M}_{\bf v}\mid{\bf v}\in\mathcal{W}_{v_{1}}\cdots\mathcal{W}_{v_{n+1}}\})
, which by (
2.6
) implies that
\mathring{M}_{v_{1}}\cdots\mathring{M}_{v_{n+1}}\subset\text{sp}(\{\mathring{M}_{\bf v}\mid{\bf v}\in\mathcal{W}_{v_{1}}\cdots\mathcal{W}_{v_{n+1}}\})
. This finishes the proof of (
2.5
).

We are now ready to prove assertions (1)-(3).

(1) Let
v_{1},\dots,v_{n}\in\Gamma
.
If
{\bf v}\in\mathcal{W}_{v_{1}}\cdots\mathcal{W}_{v_{n}}\setminus\{e\}
, then it follows by induction on
n
that
{\bf v}
admits a reduced decomposition
{\bf v}=v_{i_{1}}\cdots v_{i_{k}}
, for some
1\leq k\leq n
and some
1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<\dots<i_{k}\leq n
. Therefore,
\mathring{M}_{\bf v}=\mathring{M}_{v_{i_{1}}}\cdots\mathring{M}_{v_{i_{k}}}\subset M_{v_{1}}\cdots M_{v_{n}}
, which justifies the inclusion
\supset
from (1).

For the opposite inclusion, note that

(2.7)

\text{sp}(M_{v_{1}}\cdots M_{v_{n}})=\mathbb{C}1+\text{sp}(\{\mathring{M}_{v_{i_{1}}}\cdots\mathring{M}_{v_{i_{k}}}\mid 1\leq k\leq n,1\leq i_{1}<\dots<i_{k}\leq n\}).

By (
2.5
) we get that
\mathring{M}_{v_{i_{1}}}\cdots\mathring{M}_{v_{i_{k}}}\subset\text{sp}(\{\mathring{M}_{\bf v}\mid{\bf v}\in\mathcal{W}_{v_{i_{1}}}\cdots\mathcal{W}_{v_{i_{k}}}\})\subset\text{sp}(\{\mathring{M}_{\bf v}\mid{\bf v}\in\mathcal{W}_{v_{1}}\cdots\mathcal{W}_{v_{n}}\})
. In combination with (
2.7
), the inclusion
\subset
from (1) follows.

(2) Let
{\bf v}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{1}}\cdots\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{n}}
. Write
{\bf v}=v_{1,1}\cdots v_{1,k_{1}}\cdots v_{n,1}\cdots v_{n,k_{n}}
, where
v_{i,1},\dots,v_{i,k_{i}}\in\Gamma_{i}
, for every
1\leq i\leq n
. The proof of (1) gives that
\mathring{M}_{\bf v}\subset M_{v_{1,1}}\cdots M_{v_{1,k_{1}}}\cdots M_{v_{n,1}}\cdots M_{v_{n,k_{n}}}
. Since
M_{v_{i,1}}\cdots M_{v_{i,k_{i}}}\subset M_{\Gamma_{i}}
, for every
1\leq i\leq n
, we get that
\mathring{M}_{\bf v}\subset M_{\Gamma_{1}}\cdots M_{\Gamma_{n}}
. This implies the inclusion
\supset
from (2).

To justify the opposite inclusion, for
1\leq i\leq n
, let
v_{i,1},\dots,v_{i,k_{i}}\in\Gamma_{i}
.
By (1), we have

M_{v_{1,1}}\cdots M_{v_{1,k_{1}}}\cdots M_{v_{n,1}}\cdots M_{v_{n,k_{n}}}\subset\text{sp}(\{\mathring{M}_{\bf v}\mid{\bf v}\in\mathcal{W}_{v_{1,1}}\cdots\mathcal{W}_{v_{1,k_{1}}}\cdots\mathcal{W}_{v_{n,1}}\cdots\mathcal{W}_{v_{n,k_{n}}}\}).

Since
\mathcal{W}_{v_{i,1}}\cdots\mathcal{W}_{v_{i,k_{i}}}\subset\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{i}}
, for every
1\leq i\leq n
, we derive that

M_{v_{1,1}}\cdots M_{v_{1,k_{1}}}\cdots M_{v_{n,1}}\cdots M_{v_{n,k_{n}}}\subset\text{sp}(\{{\mathring{M}}_{\bf v}\mid{\bf v}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{1}}\cdots\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{n}}\}).

Since the span of
M_{v_{1,1}}\cdots M_{v_{1,k_{1}}}\cdots M_{v_{n,1}}\cdots M_{v_{n,k_{n}}}
, ranging over all
v_{i,1},\dots,v_{i,k_{i}}\in\Gamma_{i}
, for every
1\leq i\leq n
, is
\|\cdot\|_{2}
-dense in
\text{sp}(M_{\Gamma_{1}}\cdots M_{\Gamma_{n}})
, the inclusion
\subset
from (2) follows.

(3) This assertion follows readily by using (2) and the fact that if
{\bf v}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}
, then
\text{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{0}}}(\mathring{M}_{\bf v})
is equal to
\mathring{M}_{\bf v}
, if
{\bf v}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{0}}
, and to
\{0\}
, if
{\bf v}\not\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{0}}
.
∎

Lemma 2.16
.

Assume the above setting and additionally that
M_{v}
is diffuse, for every
v\in\Gamma
. Let
\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}\subset\Gamma
be full subgraphs.
Then the following hold:

(1)

M_{\Lambda}^{\prime}\cap M_{\Gamma}=\mathcal{Z}(M_{\Lambda})\overline{\otimes}M_{\Lambda^{\perp}}
and
\mathcal{Z}(M_{\Lambda})=\overline{\otimes}_{v\in C}\mathcal{Z}(M_{v})
, where
C
is the maximal clique factor of
\Lambda
.
Moreover, if
M_{v}
is a II
1
factor, for every
v\in\Lambda
, then
M_{\Lambda}
is a II
1
factor and
M_{\Lambda}^{\prime}\cap M_{\Gamma}=M_{\Lambda^{\perp}}
.

(2)

M_{\Lambda}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
if and only if
\Lambda\subset\Lambda^{\prime}
.

(3)

If
\Lambda
is not a clique, then
M_{\Lambda}
has no amenable direct summand.

Proof.

For part (1), see
[
CF17
, Corollaries 3.28 and 3.29]
. Part (2) is a consequence of the following fact: if
v\in\Gamma
and
M_{v}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
, then
v\in\Lambda^{\prime}
. For a proof of this fact, see
[
BCC24
, Proposition 5.9]
. This fact can be proved directly as follows. Assume that
v\not\in\Lambda^{\prime}
. Since
M_{v}
is diffuse and
M_{\text{st}(v)}=M_{\text{lk}(v)}\overline{\otimes}M_{v}
, we clearly have that
M_{v}\nprec_{M_{\text{st}(v)}}M_{\text{lk}(v)}
. Since
M_{\Gamma}=M_{\Gamma\setminus\{v\}}*_{M_{\text{lk}(v)}}M_{\text{st}(v)}
,
[
IPP08
, Theorem 1.1]
implies that
M_{v}\nprec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Gamma\setminus\{v\}}
and hence
M_{v}\nprec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
.
For part (3), note that as
\Lambda
is not a clique, it contains two vertices
w,w^{\prime}
which are not adjacent. Hence,
M_{w}*M_{w^{\prime}}\subset M_{\Lambda}
. Since
M_{w}
and
M_{w^{\prime}}
are diffuse,
M_{w}*M_{w^{\prime}}
is a nonamenable II
1
factor and thus
M_{\Lambda}
has no amenable direct summand.
∎

Note that part (2) is also a consequence of a more general result (Proposition
3.2
) that we prove in the next section.

For further reference, we also recall the following key calculation of conditional expectations in graph product von Neumann algebras.
For a reduced word
{\bf v}=v_{1}\cdots v_{n}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}
, we define, abusing notation,
\text{lk}({\bf v})=\bigcap_{i=1}^{n}\text{lk}(v_{i}).

Proposition 2.17
.

Assume the above setting. Let
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\subset\Gamma
be full subgraphs,
{\bf u},{\bf v}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}
,
a\in\mathring{M}_{\bf u}
and
b\in\mathring{M}_{\bf v}
.
Consider reduced decompositions
{\bf u}={\bf u}_{l}{\bf u}_{c}{\bf u}_{r}
and
{\bf v}={\bf v}_{l}{\bf v}_{c}{\bf v}_{r}
, where
{\bf u}_{l},{\bf v}_{l}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{1}}
,
{\bf u}_{r},{\bf v}_{r}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{2}}
, and
{\bf u}_{c},{\bf v}_{c}
do not start with a letter from
\Gamma_{1}
and do not end with a letter from
\Gamma_{2}
.
Write
a=a_{l}a_{c}a_{r}
,
b=b_{l}b_{c}b_{r}
, where
a_{l}\in\mathring{M}_{{\bf u}_{l}},a_{c}\in\mathring{M}_{{\bf u}_{c}},a_{r}\in\mathring{M}_{{\bf u}_{r}}
and
b_{l}\in\mathring{M}_{{\bf v}_{l}},b_{c}\in\mathring{M}_{{\bf v}_{c}},b_{r}\in\mathring{M}_{{\bf v}_{r}}
.

Then for every
x\in M_{\Gamma}
we have that

\emph{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{2}}}(a^{*}\emph{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{1}}}(x)b)=\tau(a_{c}^{*}b_{c})a_{r}^{*}\emph{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{1}\cap\Gamma_{2}\cap\emph{lk}({\bf u}_{c})}}(a_{l}^{*}xb_{l})b_{r}.

Proposition
2.17
was proved in
[
BC24
, Lemma 3.4]
when
M_{v}=\text{L}(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})
for every
v\in\Gamma
. It was established in general in
[
CdSHJKEN24
, Lemma 3.17]
, where it is stated in an equivalent form. The above formulation of Proposition
2.17
is given in
[
BCC24
, Proposition 5.1]
.

In the next section, we establish several intertwining results for graph product von Neumann algebras. We derive a first such result here as an immediate consequence of Proposition
2.17
.

Lemma 2.18
.

Assume the above setting.
Let
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\subset\Gamma
be full subgraphs such that
\Gamma_{2}\subset\Gamma_{1}
.
Let
P\subset pM_{\Gamma_{1}}p
be a von Neumann subalgebra. Assume that
P\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\Gamma_{2}}
.

Then
P\prec_{M_{\Gamma_{1}}}^{s}M_{\Gamma_{2}}
.

Proof.

Assume by contradiction that the conclusion fails. Then we can find a nonzero projection
p^{\prime}\in P^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma_{1}}p
such that
Pp^{\prime}\nprec_{M_{\Gamma_{1}}}M_{\Gamma_{2}}
. Hence we can find a net
(u_{n})\subset\mathcal{U}(Pp^{\prime})
such that
\|\text{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{2}}}(a^{*}u_{n}b)\|_{2}\rightarrow 0
, for every
a,b\in M_{\Gamma_{1}}
. We claim that
\|\text{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{2}}}(a^{*}u_{n}b)\|_{2}\rightarrow 0
, for every
a,b\in M_{\Gamma}
. Assuming the claim, we get that
Pp^{\prime}\nprec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Gamma_{2}}
, which contradicts the fact that
P\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\Gamma_{2}}
.

In order to prove the claim, we may assume that
a\in\mathring{M}_{\bf u}
and
b\in\mathring{M}_{\bf v}
, for some
{\bf u},{\bf v}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}
. Proposition
2.17
gives
a_{l},b_{l}\in M_{\Gamma_{1}}
and
C>0
such that
\|\text{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{2}}}(a^{*}\text{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{1}}}(x)b)\|_{2}\leq C\|\text{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{2}}}(a_{l}^{*}xb_{l})\|_{2}
, for every
x\in M_{\Gamma}
. Since
a_{l},b_{l}\in M_{\Gamma_{1}}
we get that
\|\text{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{2}}}(a_{l}^{*}u_{n}b_{l})\|_{2}\rightarrow 0
. Since all
u_{n}
belong to
Pp^{\prime}
, which is contained in
M_{\Gamma_{1}}
, we derive that
\|\text{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{2}}}(a^{*}u_{n}b)\|_{2}=\|\text{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{2}}}(a^{*}\text{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{1}}}(u_{n})b)\|_{2}\rightarrow 0
, which proves the claim.
∎

3.
Intertwining results for graph product von Neumann algebras and hulls

This section is devoted to several intertwining results for graph product von Neumann algebras, and introduces the central notion of the
hull
of a subalgebra in a graph product von Neumann algebra. Several of the results obtained in this section develop and expand results from
[
BCC24
, Section 5]
in a more systematic way.
We first introduce some notation that we use throughout this section.

Notation 3.1
.

Let
\Gamma
be a finite simple graph and
(M_{v},\tau_{v})_{v\in\Gamma}
a collection of tracial von Neumann algebras. Let
M_{\Gamma}=*_{v,\Gamma}M_{v}
the associated graph product von Neumann algebra and
p\in M_{\Gamma}
a projection.

3.1.
A general intertwining result

We start with the following crucial result from which we will deduce a number of corollaries.

Proposition 3.2
.

Assume the setting from Notation
3.1
. Let
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\subset\Gamma
be full subgraphs, and assume that
p\in M_{\Gamma_{1}}
. Let
P\subset pM_{\Gamma_{1}}p
be a von Neumann subalgebra such that
P\nprec_{M_{\Gamma_{1}}}M_{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}
, for every full subgraph
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\subsetneq\Gamma_{1}
.

(1)

If
P\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Gamma_{2}}
, then
\Gamma_{1}\subset\Gamma_{2}
.

(2)

Additionally, if
v\in pM_{\Gamma}\setminus\{0\}
is such that
Pv\subset\sum_{i=1}^{k}v_{i}M_{\Gamma_{2}}
for some
v_{1},\dots,v_{k}\in M_{\Gamma}
, then
v
belongs to the
\|\cdot\|_{2}
-closure of the linear span of
M_{\Gamma_{1}^{\perp}}M_{\Gamma_{2}}
.

In order to motivate Proposition
3.2
, we note that it is a von Neumann algebraic analogue of the following group-theoretic fact. Let
G_{\Gamma}=*_{v,\Gamma}G_{v}
be the graph product group associated to a collection
(G_{v})_{v\in\Gamma}
of discrete groups. Let
H\subset G_{\Gamma_{1}}
be a subgroup which is not contained in any subgroup of
G_{\Gamma_{1}}
of the form
hG_{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}h^{-1}
, for some proper full subgraph
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\subsetneq\Gamma_{1}
and
h\in G_{\Gamma_{1}}
. If
H
is contained in
gG_{\Gamma_{2}}g^{-1}
, for some
g\in G_{\Gamma}
, then
\Gamma_{1}\subset\Gamma_{2}
and
g
belongs to
G_{\Gamma_{1}^{\perp}}G_{\Gamma_{2}}
. This group-theoretic fact can be deduced for instance from the results in
[
AM15
, Section 3]
.

Proof.

In order to prove the conclusion, it suffices to show that
if there exists
v\in pM_{\Gamma}\setminus\{0\}
such that
Pv\subset\sum_{i=1}^{k}v_{i}M_{\Gamma_{2}}
, for some
v_{1},\dots,v_{k}\in M_{\Gamma}
, then
\Gamma_{1}\subset\Gamma_{2}
and
v\in\overline{\text{sp}}(M_{\Gamma_{1}^{\perp}}M_{\Gamma_{2}})
. By using Proposition
2.6
we may assume that
v
satisfies the stronger condition that
(P)_{1}v\subset\sum_{i=1}^{k}v_{i}(M_{\Gamma_{2}})_{1}
. By assumption we have
P\nprec_{M_{\Gamma_{1}}}M_{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}
, for every full subgraph
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\subsetneq\Gamma_{1}
. Therefore, Proposition
2.4
(5) gives a net
(u_{n})\subset\mathcal{U}(P)
such that for every
x,y\in pM_{\Gamma_{1}}
and every full subgraph
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\subsetneq\Gamma_{1}
, one has
\|\mathrm{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}}(x^{*}u_{n}y)\|_{2}\rightarrow 0
.

Claim 3.3
.

The following hold.

(1)

If
\Gamma_{1}\not\subset\Gamma_{2}
, then
\|\emph{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{2}}}(a^{*}u_{n}b)\|_{2}\rightarrow 0
, for every
a,b\in M_{\Gamma}
.

(2)

Let
{\bf u}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}
and consider a reduced decomposition
{\bf u}={\bf u}_{l}{\bf u}_{c}{\bf u}_{r}
, where
{\bf u}_{l}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{1}}
,
{\bf u}_{r}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{2}}
, and
{\bf u}_{c}
does not start with a letter from
\Gamma_{1}
and does not end with a letter from
\Gamma_{2}
.
If
{\bf u}_{c}\not\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{1}^{\perp}}
, then
\|\emph{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{2}}}(a^{*}u_{n}b)\|_{2}\rightarrow 0
, for every
a\in\mathring{M}_{\bf u}
and
b\in M_{\Gamma}
.

Proof of Claim
3.3
.

We prove both assertions at the same time. The linear span of
\{\mathring{M}_{\bf v}\mid{\bf v}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}\}
is
\|\cdot\|_{2}
-dense in
M_{\Gamma}
, so we may assume that
b\in\mathring{M}_{\bf v}
, for some
{\bf v}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}
. When proving (1), we may also assume that
a\in\mathring{M}_{\bf u}
for some
{\bf u}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}
(when proving (2) the element
\bf u
is given). Consider reduced decompositions
{\bf u}={\bf u}_{l}{\bf u}_{c}{\bf u}_{r}
and
{\bf v}={\bf v}_{l}{\bf v}_{c}{\bf v}_{r}
, where
{\bf u}_{l},{\bf v}_{l}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{1}}
,
{\bf u}_{r},{\bf v}_{r}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{2}}
, and
{\bf u}_{c},{\bf v}_{c}
do not start with a letter from
\Gamma_{1}
and do not end with a letter from
\Gamma_{2}
. Assuming either that
\Gamma_{1}\not\subset\Gamma_{2}
(for (1)) or that
{\bf u}_{c}\not\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{1}^{\perp}}
(for (2)), our aim is to prove that
\|\mathrm{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{2}}}(a^{*}u_{n}b)\|_{2}\rightarrow 0
, for every
a\in\mathring{M}_{\bf u}
and every
b\in\mathring{M}_{\bf v}
.

Write
a=a_{l}a_{c}a_{r}
,
b=b_{l}b_{c}b_{r}
, where
a_{l}\in\mathring{M}_{{\bf u}_{l}},a_{c}\in\mathring{M}_{{\bf u}_{c}},a_{r}\in\mathring{M}_{{\bf u}_{r}}
and
b_{l}\in\mathring{M}_{{\bf v}_{l}},b_{c}\in\mathring{M}_{{\bf v}_{c}},b_{r}\in\mathring{M}_{{\bf v}_{r}}
. Let
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}=\Gamma_{1}\cap\Gamma_{2}\cap\text{lk}({\bf u}_{c})
. Since
u_{n}\in M_{\Gamma_{1}}
, Proposition
2.17
gives

(3.1)

\text{$\text{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{2}}}(a^{*}u_{n}b)=\tau(a_{c}^{*}b_{c})a_{r}^{*}\text{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}}(a_{l}^{*}u_{n}b_{l})b_{r}$, for every $n$}.

If
\Gamma_{1}\not\subset\Gamma_{2}
, then
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\subsetneq\Gamma_{1}
. If
{\bf u}_{c}\not\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{1}^{\perp}}
, then
\Gamma_{1}\not\subset\text{lk}({\bf u}_{c})
and thus
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\subsetneq\Gamma_{1}
. So in either case, we have
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\subsetneq\Gamma_{1}
. Since
a_{l},b_{l}\in M_{\Gamma_{1}}
, we get that
\|\text{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}}(a_{l}^{*}u_{n}b_{l})\|_{2}=\|\text{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}}((pa_{l})^{*}u_{n}(pb_{l}))\|_{2}\rightarrow 0
. Together with
(
3.1
) we deduce that
\|\text{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{2}}}(a^{*}u_{n}b)\|_{2}\rightarrow 0
, which finishes the proof of the claim.
∎

We are now ready to prove the conclusion.
First, assume by contradiction that
\Gamma_{1}\not\subset\Gamma_{2}
.
If
u\in\mathcal{U}(P)
, then
uv=\sum_{i=1}^{k}v_{i}x_{i}
, for some
x_{1},\dots,x_{k}\in(M_{\Gamma_{2}})_{1}
.
Since for every
1\leq i\leq k
we have
\langle uv,v_{i}x_{i}\rangle=\tau(x_{i}^{*}v_{i}^{*}uv)=\tau(\text{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{2}}}(x_{i}^{*}v_{i}^{*}uv))=\tau(x_{i}^{*}\text{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{2}}}(v_{i}^{*}uv))
, we deduce that

(3.2)

\|v\|_{2}^{2}=\langle uv,uv\rangle=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\Re\langle uv,v_{i}x_{i}\rangle=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\Re\tau(x_{i}^{*}\text{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{2}}}(v_{i}^{*}uv))\leq\sum_{i=1}^{k}\|\text{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{2}}}(v_{i}^{*}uv)\|_{2}.

As
\Gamma_{1}\not\subset\Gamma_{2}
, Claim
3.3
(1) gives
\|\text{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{2}}}(a^{*}u_{n}b)\|_{2}\rightarrow 0
, for every
a,b\in M_{\Gamma}
. So,
\|\text{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{2}}}(v_{i}^{*}u_{n}v)\|_{2}\rightarrow 0
, for every
1\leq i\leq k
. Since
v\not=0
and
(u_{n})\subset\mathcal{U}(P)
, this contradicts (
3.2
) and proves that
\Gamma_{1}\subset\Gamma_{2}
.

Second, let
\mathcal{H}_{0}
be the linear span of
\{\mathring{M}_{\bf u}\mid{\bf u}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{1}^{\perp}}\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{2}}\}
, and put
\mathcal{H}=\overline{\mathcal{H}_{0}}^{\|\cdot\|_{2}}
. We claim that

(3.3)

\|\text{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{2}}}(a^{*}u_{n}b)\|_{2}\rightarrow 0
, for every
a\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
and
b\in M_{\Gamma}
.

To prove this, we may assume that
a\in\mathring{M}_{\bf u}
, for some
{\bf u}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{1}^{\perp}}\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{2}}
. Consider a reduced decomposition
{\bf u}={\bf u}_{l}{\bf u}_{c}{\bf u}_{r}
, where
{\bf u}_{l}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{1}}
,
{\bf u}_{r}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{2}}
and
{\bf u}_{c}
does not start with a letter from
\Gamma_{1}
and does not end with a letter from
\Gamma_{2}
. Since
{\bf u}\not\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{1}^{\perp}}\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{2}}
and
\Gamma_{1}\subset\Gamma_{2}
, we get that
{\bf u}_{c}\not\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{1}^{\perp}}
.
Thus, (
3.3
) follows from Claim
3.3
(2).

Finally, (
3.3
) gives that
\|\text{E}_{M_{\Gamma_{2}}}(a^{*}u_{n}b)\|_{2}\rightarrow 0
, for every
a\in p\mathcal{H}_{0}
and
b\in M_{\Gamma}
.
Since
\mathcal{H}
is an
M_{\Gamma_{1}}
-
M_{\Gamma_{2}}
-bimodule,
p\mathcal{H}=\overline{p\mathcal{H}_{0}}^{\|\cdot\|_{2}}
is a
P
-
M_{\Gamma_{2}}
-bimodule, so Corollary
2.7
implies that
v\in\text{L}^{2}(M_{\Gamma})\ominus p\mathcal{H}
. Since
v\in pM_{\Gamma}
, we have
v\in[(1-p)\mathcal{H}]^{\perp}
, hence we conclude that
v\in\text{L}^{2}(M_{\Gamma})\ominus\mathcal{H}
.
Noticing that
\text{L}^{2}(M_{\Gamma})\ominus\mathcal{H}
is equal to the
\|\cdot\|_{2}
-closure of the linear span of
\{\mathring{M}_{\bf u}\mid{\bf u}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{1}^{\perp}}\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma_{2}}\}
and thus to the
\|\cdot\|_{2}
-closure of the linear span of

M_{\Gamma_{1}^{\perp}}M_{\Gamma_{2}}
by Lemma
2.15
(2), this finishes the proof.
∎

3.2.
The hull of a subalgebra

The following definition, central to our work, is inspired by Zimmer’s notion of the algebraic hull of a cocycle with values into an algebraic group (see
[
Zi84
, Section 9.2]
);
see also
[
HH22
, Section 3.3]
, where a related notion of parabolic support was introduced for measured groupoids equipped with cocycles towards right-angled Artin groups.

Definition 3.4
.

Assume the setting from Notation
3.1
.
We say that
M_{\Lambda}
, for a full subgraph
\Lambda\subset\Gamma
, is the (von Neumann algebraic)
hull
of a von Neumann subalgebra
P\subset pM_{\Gamma}p
if
P\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\Lambda}
and
P\nprec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
, for every full subgraph
\Lambda^{\prime}\subsetneq\Lambda
.

We now address the existence and uniqueness of the hull of a von Neumann subalgebra of
pM_{\Gamma}p
. The uniqueness of the hull is a consequence of Corollary
3.5
below. In general, a von Neumann subalgebra
P\subset pM_{\Gamma}p
need not have a hull. However, Proposition
3.7
below shows that the hull exists provided that
P^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p
is a factor. In general, there is a partition
\{p_{\Lambda}\}_{\Lambda}
of
p
in
\mathcal{Z}(P^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p)
such that
Pp_{\Lambda}
has hull
M_{\Lambda}
.

We start with the following consequence of Proposition
3.2
, which justifies the uniqueness of the hull.

Corollary 3.5
.

Assume the setting from Notation
3.1
.
Let
P\subset pM_{\Gamma}p
be a von Neumann subalgebra.
Let
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\subset\Gamma
be full subgraphs.

If
P\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\Gamma_{1}}
and
P\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\Gamma_{2}}
, then
P\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\Gamma_{1}\cap\Gamma_{2}}
.

Corollary
3.5
generalizes
[
BCC24
, Proposition 5.9]
which implies the case
\Gamma_{1}=\{v\}
, for
v\in\Gamma
.

Proof.

We will prove the following claim.

Claim 3.6
.

Let
Q\subset pM_{\Gamma}p
be a von Neumann subalgebra, and let
\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}\subset\Gamma
be two full subgraphs. Assume that
Q\prec^{s}_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{1}}
and
Q\prec^{s}_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{2}}
, and that for every proper full subgraph
\Lambda^{\prime}_{1}\subsetneq\Lambda_{1}
, one has
Q\not\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}_{1}}
.
Then
\Lambda_{1}\subset\Lambda_{2}
.

Before proving the claim, let us first explain how the corollary follows. Let
P\subset pM_{\Gamma}p
be as in the statement, and let
p^{\prime}\in P^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p
be a nonzero projection. Let
\Lambda_{1}\subset\Gamma_{1}
be a minimal subgraph such that
Pp^{\prime}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{1}}
(so that
Pp^{\prime}\not\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}_{1}}
for every proper full subgraph
\Lambda^{\prime}_{1}\subsetneq\Lambda_{1}
).
Then we can find a nonzero projection
p^{\prime\prime}\in P^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p
such that

p^{\prime\prime}\leq p^{\prime}
and
Pp^{\prime\prime}\prec^{s}_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{1}}
.
We also have
Pp^{\prime\prime}\prec^{s}_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Gamma_{2}}
. Applying the claim to
Q=Pp^{\prime\prime}
(with
\Lambda_{2}=\Gamma_{2}
), we deduce that
\Lambda_{1}\subset\Gamma_{2}
, so
\Lambda_{1}\subset\Gamma_{1}\cap\Gamma_{2}
. We thus have
Pp^{\prime\prime}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Gamma_{1}\cap\Gamma_{2}}
, in particular
Pp^{\prime}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Gamma_{1}\cap\Gamma_{2}}
. Since this is true for any
p^{\prime}\in P^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p
, we have proved that
P\prec^{s}_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Gamma_{1}\cap\Gamma_{2}}
, as desired.

We now prove the above claim. Since
Q\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{2}}
, we have projections
q_{2}\in Q,r_{2}\in M_{\Lambda_{2}}
, a nonzero partial isometry
v_{2}\in r_{2}M_{\Gamma}q_{2}
and a
*
-homomorphism
\theta_{2}:q_{2}Qq_{2}\rightarrow r_{2}M_{\Lambda_{2}}r_{2}
such that

(1)

\theta_{2}(y)v_{2}=v_{2}y
, for every
y\in q_{2}Qq_{2}
.

Since
v_{2}^{*}v_{2}\in(q_{2}Qq_{2})^{\prime}\cap q_{2}M_{\Gamma}q_{2}=(Q^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p)q_{2}
, we can find a projection
q^{\prime}\in Q^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p
such that
v_{2}^{*}v_{2}=q_{2}q^{\prime}
. Let
r=v_{2}v_{2}^{*}
. Then
r\in\theta_{2}(q_{2}Qq_{2})^{\prime}\cap r_{2}M_{\Gamma}r_{2}
and
r\leq r_{2}
.

Since
q_{2}Qq_{2}q^{\prime}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{1}}
and
q_{2}Qq_{2}q^{\prime}\nprec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{1}^{\prime}}
, for every full subgraph
\Lambda_{1}^{\prime}\subsetneq\Lambda_{1}
, by Proposition
2.5
(2) we can find projections
q_{1}\in q_{2}Qq_{2}
,
r_{1}\in M_{\Lambda_{1}}
, a nonzero partial isometry
v_{1}\in r_{1}M_{\Gamma}q_{1}q^{\prime}
and a
*
-homomorphism
\theta_{1}:q_{1}Qq_{1}\rightarrow r_{1}M_{\Lambda_{1}}r_{1}
such that

(2)

\theta_{1}(x)v_{1}=v_{1}x
, for every
x\in q_{1}Qq_{1}
, and

(3)

\theta_{1}(q_{1}Qq_{1})\nprec_{M_{\Lambda_{1}}}M_{\Lambda_{1}^{\prime}}
, for every proper full subgraph
\Lambda_{1}^{\prime}\subsetneq\Lambda_{1}
.

Letting
v=v_{1}v_{2}^{*}
and using (1) and (2), we get that

(3.4)

\theta_{1}(x)v=v\theta_{2}(x)
, for every
x\in q_{1}Qq_{1}
.

Since
vv^{*}=v_{1}(v_{2}^{*}v_{2})v_{1}^{*}=v_{1}(q_{2}q^{\prime})v_{1}^{*}=v_{1}v_{1}^{*}\not=0
, we get that
v\not=0
. Let
R:=\theta_{1}(q_{1}Qq_{1})\subset r_{1}M_{\Lambda_{1}}r_{1}
. Then
Rv\subset vM_{\Lambda_{2}}
and
R\nprec_{M_{\Lambda_{1}}}M_{\Lambda_{1}^{\prime}}
, for every full subgraph
\Lambda_{1}^{\prime}\subsetneq\Lambda_{1}
, by (3). Proposition
3.2
thus implies that
\Lambda_{1}\subset\Lambda_{2}
, as desired.
∎

As explained above, we now address the existence of the hull.

Proposition 3.7
.

Assume the setting from Notation
3.1
.
Let
P\subset pM_{\Gamma}p
be a von Neumann subalgebra.
For every full subgraph
\Lambda\subset\Gamma
, let
p_{\Lambda}\in\mathcal{N}_{pM_{\Gamma}p}(P)^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p
be the maximal projection such that
Pp_{\Lambda}\prec^{s}_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda}
and
Pp_{\Lambda}\nprec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
, for every full subgraph
\Lambda^{\prime}\subsetneq\Lambda
.

Then
\sum_{\text{$\Lambda$}}p_{\Lambda}=p
.

Moreover, if
\Lambda\subset\Gamma
is a full subgraph, then
Pp_{\Lambda}\nprec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
, for every full subgraph
\Lambda^{\prime}\subset\Gamma
with
\Lambda\not\subset\Lambda^{\prime}
.

The moreover part of the statement ensures that if
P
admits a hull,
of the form
M_{\Lambda}
for some full subgraph
\Lambda
of
\Gamma
,
and
P\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
, for a full subgraph
\Lambda^{\prime}\subset\Gamma
, then
\Lambda\subset\Lambda^{\prime}
.

Before proving this result, we justify in the following remark that the projection
p_{\Lambda}
is well-defined.

Remark 3.8
.

Let
(M,\tau)
be a tracial von Neumann algebra and
P\subset pMp,Q,R_{1},\dots,R_{k}\subset qMq
be von Neumann subalgebras. By Zorn’s lemma we can find a maximal, with respect to inclusion, family of pairwise orthogonal projections
\{z_{i}\}_{i\in I}\subset\mathcal{N}_{pMp}(P)^{\prime}\cap pMp
such that for every
i\in I
we have
Pz_{i}\prec_{M}^{s}Q
and
Pz_{i}\nprec_{M}R_{j}
, for every
1\leq j\leq k
.
Put
z=\sum_{i\in I}z_{i}
. Then
z\in\mathcal{N}_{pMp}(P)^{\prime}\cap pMp
,
Pz\prec_{M}^{s}Q
and
Pz\nprec_{M}R_{j}
, for every
1\leq j\leq k
. Using that
\mathcal{N}_{pMp}(P)^{\prime}\cap pMp
is abelian as it is contained in
\mathcal{Z}(P^{\prime}\cap pMp)
, it follows that
z
is the maximal projection with these properties.

Proof of Proposition
3.7
.

We first claim that if
\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}\subset\Gamma
are distinct full subgraphs, then
p_{\Lambda_{1}}p_{\Lambda_{2}}=0
. Assume by contradiction that
p^{\prime}=p_{\Lambda_{1}}p_{\Lambda_{2}}\not=0
. Then
Pp^{\prime}\prec^{s}_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{1}}
and

Pp^{\prime}\prec^{s}_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{2}}
.
Corollary
3.5
implies that
Pp^{\prime}\prec^{s}_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda_{2}}
. Hence
Pp_{\Lambda_{1}}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda_{2}}
and
Pp_{\Lambda_{2}}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda_{2}}
. The definitions of
p_{\Lambda_{1}}
and
p_{\Lambda_{2}}
imply that
\Lambda_{1}=\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda_{2}=\Lambda_{2}
,
which gives a contradiction and proves the claim.

Next, we claim that
\sum_{\text{$\Lambda$}}p_{\Lambda}=p
. Assume by contradiction that

q=p-\sum_{\Lambda}p_{\Lambda}\in\mathcal{N}_{pM_{\Gamma}p}(P)^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p
is nonzero.
Let
\Lambda\subset\Gamma
be a full subgraph of minimal cardinality with
Pq\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda}
. Proposition
2.4
(3) gives a nonzero projection
r\in\mathcal{N}_{qM_{\Gamma}q}(Pq)^{\prime}\cap qM_{\Gamma}q
such that
Pr\prec_{M}^{s}M_{\Lambda}
. If
u\in\mathcal{N}_{pM_{\Gamma}p}(P)
, then
uq\in\mathcal{N}_{qM_{\Gamma}q}(Pq)
and so
ur=(uq)r=r(uq)=r(qu)=ru
. This shows that
r\in\mathcal{N}_{pM_{\Gamma}p}(P)^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p
. As
Pr\nprec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
, for every full subgraph
\Lambda^{\prime}\subsetneq\Lambda
, and
r\leq p-p_{\Lambda}
, this contradicts the maximality of
p_{\Lambda}
.

Finally, to prove the moreover assertion assume that
Pp_{\Lambda}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
, for a full subgraph
\Lambda^{\prime}\subset\Gamma
.
As above, Proposition
2.4
(3) gives a nonzero projection
s\in\mathcal{N}_{pM_{\Gamma}p}(P)^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p
with
s\leq p_{\Lambda}
and
Ps\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
. Since
s\leq p_{\Lambda}
, we have
Ps\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\Lambda}
. Thus, Proposition
3.5
gives that
Ps\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\Lambda\cap\Lambda^{\prime}}
and hence
Pp_{\Lambda}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda\cap\Lambda^{\prime}}
. The definition of
p_{\Lambda}
implies that
\Lambda\cap\Lambda^{\prime}=\Lambda
and hence
\Lambda\subset\Lambda^{\prime}
, as desired.
∎

3.3.
Normalizers

We continue with consequences of Proposition
3.2
concerning normalizers inside graph product von Neumann algebras.
The first assertion of the following proposition is a direct consequence of Proposition
3.2
in the case where
\Gamma_{1}=\Lambda
and
\Gamma_{2}=\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}
. Both assertions are also consequences of
[
BCC24
, Proposition 5.8(2)]
– we omit the proof of the second, as we will prove a stronger version in Proposition
3.10
below.

Proposition 3.9

(​​
[
BCC24
, Proposition 5.8]
)
.

Assume the setting from Notation
3.1
.
Let
P\subset pM_{\Gamma}p
be a von Neumann subalgebra and
\Lambda\subset\Gamma
a full subgraph.
Then we have:

(1)

Assume that
p\in M_{\Lambda}
,
P\subset pM_{\Lambda}p
and
P\nprec_{M_{\Lambda}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
, for every proper full subgraph
\Lambda^{\prime}\subsetneq\Lambda
. If
v\in pM_{\Gamma}
satisfies
Pv\subset\sum_{i=1}^{k}v_{i}M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}
, for some
k\geq 1
and
v_{1},\dots,v_{k}\in M_{\Gamma}
, then
v\in M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}
. In particular,

(\mathcal{Q}\mathcal{N}_{pM_{\Gamma}p}(P))^{\prime\prime}\subset M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}
.

(2)

Assume that
P\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\Lambda}
and that
P\nprec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
, for every full subgraph
\Lambda^{\prime}\subsetneq\Gamma
. Then
\mathcal{N}_{pM_{\Gamma}p}(P)^{\prime\prime}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}
.

For further use, we establish the following strengthening of Proposition
3.9
(2).

Proposition 3.10
.

Assume the setting from Notation
3.1
.
Let
P\subset pM_{\Gamma}p
be a von Neumann subalgebra,
\Lambda\subset\Gamma
a full subgraph and
e\in M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}^{\prime}\cap M_{\Gamma}
a nonzero projection. Assume that
P\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\Lambda}e
and
P\nprec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
, for every proper full subgraph
\Lambda^{\prime}\subsetneq\Lambda
.

Then
\mathcal{N}_{pM_{\Gamma}p}(P)^{\prime\prime}\prec^{s}_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}e
.

Proof.

We adapt the proof of
[
BCC24
, Proposition 5.8(2)]
, following closely the proofs of
[
Po06b
, Lemma 3.5]
and
[
Io15
, Lemma 9.4]
.
Let
p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{pM_{\Gamma}p}(P)^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p
be a nonzero projection. Since
Pp^{\prime}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda}e
and
P\nprec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
, for every
\Lambda^{\prime}\subsetneq\Lambda
, by Proposition
2.5
(2), we can find projections
q\in P
,
r\in M_{\Lambda}
, a nonzero partial isometry
v\in reM_{\Gamma}qp^{\prime}
and a
*
-homomorphism
\theta:qPq\rightarrow rM_{\Lambda}r
such that

•

\theta(x)v=vx
, for every
x\in qPq
, and

•

\theta(qPq)\nprec_{M_{\Lambda}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
, for every proper full subgraph
\Lambda^{\prime}\subsetneq\Lambda
.

Let
u\in\mathcal{N}_{pM_{\Gamma}p}(P)
.
Let
z\in\mathcal{Z}(P)
be a projection such that
z=\sum_{i=1}^{m}w_{i}w_{i}^{*}
, for some partial isometries
w_{i}\in P
satisfying
w_{i}^{*}w_{i}\leq q
, for every
1\leq i\leq m
. For
m\geq 1
, this condition is equivalent to having
z\leq m\text{E}_{\mathcal{Z}(P)}(q)
and thus
holds for
z={\bf 1}_{[\frac{1}{m},\infty)}(\text{E}_{\mathcal{Z}(P)}(q))
.
Then

qzuqz(qPq)\subset qzuP=qzPu=qPzu\subset\sum_{i=1}^{m}(qPw_{i})(w_{i}^{*}u)\subset\sum_{i=1}^{m}qPq(w_{i}^{*}u),

and similarly
(qPq)qzuqz\subset\sum_{i=1}^{m}uw_{i}(qPq)
.
Thus, we have that
qzuqz\in\mathcal{Q}\mathcal{N}_{qM_{\Gamma}q}(qPq)
.

Next, if
x\in\mathcal{Q}\mathcal{N}_{qM_{\Gamma}q}(qPq)
, then
vxv^{*}\in\mathcal{Q}\mathcal{N}_{rM_{\Gamma}r}(\theta(qPq))
.
Hence
v(qzuqz)v^{*}\in\mathcal{Q}\mathcal{N}_{rM_{\Gamma}r}(\theta(qPq))
.
On the other hand, by Proposition
3.9
(1), we have that
\mathcal{Q}\mathcal{N}_{rM_{\Gamma}r}(\theta(qPq))\subset M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}
. Thus, we derive that
v(qzuqz)v^{*}\in M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}
. Since the projection
z
can be taken to approximate arbitrarily well the central support of
q
in
P
, we conclude that
vuv^{*}=v(quq)v^{*}\in M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}
. Since this holds for every
u\in\mathcal{N}_{pM_{\Gamma}p}(P)
, we deduce that
t:=vv^{*}\in M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}
and
v\mathcal{N}_{pM_{\Gamma}p}(P)^{\prime\prime}v^{*}\subset tM_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}t
. Since
p_{1}:=v^{*}v\in(qPq)^{\prime}\cap qM_{\Gamma}q
and
p_{1}\leq p^{\prime}
, we have
p_{1}\in\mathcal{N}_{pM_{\Gamma}p}(P)^{\prime\prime}p^{\prime}
.
Therefore, the map
\rho:p_{1}(\mathcal{N}_{pM_{\Gamma}p}(P)^{\prime\prime}p^{\prime})p_{1}\to tM_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}t
sending
x
to
vxv^{*}

is a
*
-homomorphism which satisfies
\rho(x)v=vx
, for every
x\in p_{1}(\mathcal{N}_{pM_{\Gamma}p}(P)^{\prime\prime}p^{\prime})p_{1}
, and we have
vv^{*}=t
and
v^{*}v=p_{1}
. As
t\leq re\leq e
, we also have
t=te\in M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}e
.
Hence,
\mathcal{N}_{pM_{\Gamma}p}(P)^{\prime\prime}p^{\prime}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}e
, which finishes the proof.
∎

3.4.
Additional consequences of Proposition
3.2

In the proofs of Theorems
A
and
B
, given a von Neumann subalgebra
P\subset pM_{\Gamma}p
, we will need to analyze not only the full subgraphs
\Lambda\subset\Gamma
such that
P\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda}
, but also the projections
s\in M_{\Lambda}^{\prime}\cap M_{\Gamma}
such that
P\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda}s
. We continue with two corollaries of Proposition
3.2
dealing with such projections.

Corollary 3.11
.

Assume the setting from Notation
3.1
.
Let
P\subset pM_{\Gamma}p
be a von Neumann subalgebra,
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\subset\Gamma
full subgraphs with
\Gamma_{1}\subset\Gamma_{2}
, and
e\in M_{\Gamma_{2}}^{\prime}\cap M_{\Gamma}
a projection. Assume that
P\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\Gamma_{1}}
and
P\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\Gamma_{2}}e
.

Then
P\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\Gamma_{1}}e
.

The proof of Corollary
3.11
elaborates on the proof of Corollary
3.5
that it extends.

Proof.

We will establish the following claim.

Claim 3.12
.

Let
Q\subset pM_{\Gamma}p
be a von Neumann subalgebra, and let
\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}\subset\Gamma
be two full subgraphs. Let
e\in M^{\prime}_{\Lambda_{2}}\cap M_{\Gamma}
be a projection. Assume that
Q\prec^{s}_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{1}}
and
Q\prec^{s}_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{2}}e
, and that for every proper full subgraph
\Lambda^{\prime}_{1}\subsetneq\Lambda_{1}
, one has
Q\not\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}_{1}}
.

Then
\Lambda_{1}\subset\Lambda_{2}
and
Q\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{1}}e
.

Before proving the claim, let us first explain how the corollary follows. Let
P\subset pM_{\Gamma}p
be as in the statement, and let
p^{\prime}\in P^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p
. Let
\Lambda_{1}\subset\Gamma_{1}
be a minimal subgraph such that
Pp^{\prime}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{1}}
. Then we can find a non-zero projection
p^{\prime\prime}\in P^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p
with
p^{\prime\prime}\leq p^{\prime}
such that
Pp^{\prime\prime}\prec^{s}_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{1}}
. We also have
Pp^{\prime\prime}\prec^{s}_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Gamma_{2}}e
. By choice of
\Lambda_{1}
, for every proper full subgraph
\Lambda^{\prime}_{1}\subsetneq\Lambda_{1}
, we have
Pp^{\prime\prime}\not\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}_{1}}
. Applying the claim to
Q=Pp^{\prime\prime}
(with
\Lambda_{2}=\Gamma_{2}
), we deduce that
Pp^{\prime\prime}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{1}}e
, in particular
Pp^{\prime}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{1}}e
. Since this is true for any
p^{\prime}\in P^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p
, we have proved that
P\prec^{s}_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{1}}e
, in particular
P\prec^{s}_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Gamma_{1}}e
, as desired.

We now prove the above claim.
Since
Q\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{2}}e
, Proposition
2.5
(1) provides projections
q_{2}\in Q,r_{2}\in M_{\Lambda_{2}}
, a nonzero partial isometry
v_{2}\in r_{2}eM_{\Gamma}q_{2}
and a
*
-homomorphism
\theta_{2}:q_{2}Qq_{2}\rightarrow r_{2}M_{\Lambda_{2}}r_{2}
such that

(1)

\theta_{2}(y)v_{2}=v_{2}y
, for every
y\in q_{2}Qq_{2}
.

Since
v_{2}^{*}v_{2}\in(q_{2}Qq_{2})^{\prime}\cap q_{2}M_{\Gamma}q_{2}=(Q^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p)q_{2}
, we can find a projection
q^{\prime}\in Q^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p
such that
v_{2}^{*}v_{2}=q_{2}q^{\prime}
. Let
r=v_{2}v_{2}^{*}
. Then
r\in\theta_{2}(q_{2}Qq_{2})^{\prime}\cap r_{2}M_{\Gamma}r_{2}
and
r\leq r_{2}e
.

We have
q_{2}Qq_{2}q^{\prime}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{1}}
and
q_{2}Qq_{2}q^{\prime}\nprec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{1}^{\prime}}
, for every full subgraph
\Lambda_{1}^{\prime}\subsetneq\Lambda_{1}
. Therefore, Proposition
2.5
(2) yields projections
q_{1}\in q_{2}Qq_{2}
,
r_{1}\in M_{\Lambda_{1}}
, a nonzero partial isometry
v_{1}\in r_{1}M_{\Gamma}q_{1}q^{\prime}
and a
*
-homomorphism
\theta_{1}:q_{1}Qq_{1}\rightarrow r_{1}M_{\Lambda_{1}}r_{1}
such that

(2)

\theta_{1}(x)v_{1}=v_{1}x
, for every
x\in q_{1}Qq_{1}
, and

(3)

\theta_{1}(q_{1}Qq_{1})\nprec_{M_{\Lambda_{1}}}M_{\Lambda_{1}^{\prime}}
, for every full subgraph
\Lambda_{1}^{\prime}\subsetneq\Lambda_{1}
.

Let
v=v_{1}v_{2}^{*}
. Using (1) and (2), we get that

(3.5)

\theta_{1}(x)v=v\theta_{2}(x)
, for every
x\in q_{1}Qq_{1}
.

Since
vv^{*}=v_{1}(v_{2}^{*}v_{2})v_{1}^{*}=v_{1}(q_{2}q^{\prime})v_{1}^{*}=v_{1}v_{1}^{*}\not=0
, we get that
v\not=0
. Let
R:=\theta_{1}(q_{1}Qq_{1})\subset r_{1}M_{\Lambda_{1}}r_{1}
. Then
Rv\subset vM_{\Lambda_{2}}
and
R\nprec_{M_{\Lambda_{1}}}M_{\Lambda_{1}^{\prime}}
, for every full subgraph
\Lambda_{1}^{\prime}\subsetneq\Lambda_{1}
, by (3). Proposition
3.2
thus implies that
\Lambda_{1}\subset\Lambda_{2}
, and that
v
lies in the
\|\cdot\|_{2}
-closure of the linear span of
M_{\Lambda_{1}^{\perp}}M_{\Lambda_{2}}
.
Since every element of
M_{\Lambda_{1}^{\perp}}
is a sum of four unitaries, it follows that
v
belongs to the
\|\cdot\|_{2}
-closure of the linear span of
\{uM_{\Lambda_{2}}\mid u\in\mathcal{U}(M_{\Lambda_{1}^{\perp}})\}
.

For
u\in\mathcal{U}(M_{\Lambda_{1}^{\perp}})
, define
\xi_{u}=\text{E}_{M_{\Lambda_{2}}}(u^{*}v)r
.
Let
x\in q_{1}Qq_{1}
. By using (
3.5
) and that
u\in M_{\Lambda_{1}}^{\prime}\cap M_{\Gamma}
we get that
\theta_{1}(x)(u^{*}v)=(u^{*}v)\theta_{2}(x)
. Applying
\text{E}_{M_{\Lambda_{2}}}
and using that
\Lambda_{1}\subset\Lambda_{2}
, hence
M_{\Lambda_{1}}\subset M_{\Lambda_{2}}
, gives that
\theta_{1}(x)\text{E}_{M_{\Lambda_{2}}}(u^{*}v)=\text{E}_{M_{\Lambda_{2}}}(u^{*}v)\theta_{2}(x)
.
Since
r\in\theta_{2}(q_{2}Qq_{2})^{\prime}\cap r_{2}M_{\Gamma}r_{2}
, we deduce that

(3.6)

\theta_{1}(x)\xi_{u}=\xi_{u}\theta_{2}(x)
, for every
x\in q_{1}Qq_{1}
and
u\in\mathcal{U}(M_{\Lambda_{1}^{\perp}})
.

We next claim that there exists
u\in\mathcal{U}(M_{\Lambda_{1}^{\perp}})
such that
\xi_{u}\not=0
.
Since
v\not=0
and
v
belongs to the
\|\cdot\|_{2}
-closure of the linear span of
\{uM_{\Lambda_{2}}\mid u\in\mathcal{U}(M_{\Lambda_{1}^{\perp}})\}
, we can find
u\in\mathcal{U}(M_{\Lambda_{1}^{\perp}})
such that
\text{E}_{M_{\Lambda_{2}}}(u^{*}v)\not=0
. Since
v^{*}v=v_{2}(v_{1}^{*}v_{1})v_{2}^{*}\leq v_{2}v_{2}^{*}=r
, we get that
vr=v
and so
rv^{*}=v^{*}
. Thus,

\tau(\xi_{u}v^{*}u)=\tau(\text{E}_{M_{\Lambda_{2}}}(u^{*}v)rv^{*}u)=\tau(\text{E}_{M_{\Lambda_{2}}}(u^{*}v)v^{*}u)=\|\text{E}_{M_{\Lambda_{2}}}(u^{*}v)\|_{2}^{2}\not=0,

and therefore
\xi_{u}\not=0
.

Let
u\in\mathcal{U}(M_{\Lambda_{1}^{\perp}})
such that
\xi_{u}\not=0
.
Since
e\in M_{\Lambda_{2}}^{\prime}\cap M_{\Gamma}
and
r\leq r_{2}e\leq e
, we get that
e\xi_{u}=\xi_{u}
.
By using this fact, (
3.6
) and (1), we derive that

(3.7)

(\theta_{1}(x)e)(\xi_{u}v_{2})=(\theta_{1}(x)\xi_{u})v_{2}=(\xi_{u}\theta_{2}(x))v_{2}=(\xi_{u}v_{2})x
, for every
x\in q_{1}Qq_{1}
.

Let
w=\xi_{u}v_{2}
and define a
*
-homomorphism
\rho:q_{1}Qq_{1}\rightarrow r_{1}M_{\Lambda_{1}}r_{1}e
by
\rho(x)=\theta_{1}(x)e
. Then (
3.7
) implies that
\rho(x)w=wx
, for every
x\in q_{1}Qq_{1}
. Thus, in order to conclude that
Q\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{1}}e
and finish the proof, it remains to argue that
\rho(q_{1})wq_{1}\not=0
, i.e. that
\rho(q_{1})w\not=0
.

Note that since
vv^{*}=v_{1}v_{1}^{*}\leq r_{1}
, we have
r_{1}v=v
.
Since
r_{1}\in M_{\Lambda_{1}}\subset M_{\Lambda_{2}}
,
u\in M_{\Lambda_{1}^{\perp}}
and
r_{1}v=v
, we get that
r_{1}\xi_{u}=r_{1}\text{E}_{M_{\Lambda_{2}}}(u^{*}v)r=\text{E}_{M_{\Lambda_{2}}}(r_{1}u^{*}v)r=\text{E}_{M_{\Lambda_{2}}}(u^{*}r_{1}v)r=\xi_{u}
.
By using this fact we get that
\rho(q_{1})w=\theta_{1}(q_{1})(e\xi_{u})v_{2}=r_{1}\xi_{u}v_{2}=\xi_{u}v_{2}
.
Finally, since
\xi_{u}r=\xi_{u}
and
v_{2}v_{2}^{*}=r
, we get that
(\xi_{u}v_{2})(\xi_{u}v_{2})^{*}=\xi_{u}(v_{2}v_{2}^{*})\xi_{u}^{*}=\xi_{u}r\xi_{u}^{*}=\xi_{u}\xi_{u}^{*}
. Since
\xi_{u}\not=0
, we derive that
\xi_{u}v_{2}\not=0
and thus
\rho(q_{1})w\not=0
, which finishes the proof.
∎

Corollary 3.13
.

Assume the setting from Notation
3.1
.
Let
P\subset pM_{\Gamma}p
be a von Neumann subalgebra,
\Lambda\subset\Gamma
be a full subgraph and
z\in M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}^{\prime}\cap M_{\Gamma}
be a nonzero projection.
Assume that
P\prec^{s}_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda}z
and
P\nprec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
, for every full subgraph
\Lambda^{\prime}\subsetneq\Lambda
.

Then
P\nprec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda}(1-z)
.

Proof.

Assume by contradiction that
P\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda}(1-z)
. By Proposition
2.5
(1) we find projections
p_{2}\in P,q_{2}\in M_{\Lambda}
, a nonzero partial isometry
v_{2}\in q_{2}(1-z)M_{\Gamma}p_{2}
and a
*
-homomorphism
\theta_{2}:p_{2}Pp_{2}\rightarrow q_{2}M_{\Lambda}q_{2}
such that
\theta_{2}(x)v_{2}=v_{2}x
, for every
x\in p_{2}Pp_{2}
.
Since
v_{2}^{*}v_{2}\in(p_{2}Pp_{2})^{\prime}\cap p_{2}M_{\Gamma}p_{2}=(P^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p)p_{2}
, we can find a projection
q^{\prime}\in P^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p
such that
v_{2}^{*}v_{2}=p_{2}q^{\prime}
.

As
P\prec^{s}_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda}z
and
P\nprec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
,
for every full subgraph
\Lambda^{\prime}\subsetneq\Lambda
, we have
p_{2}Pp_{2}q^{\prime}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda}z
and
p_{2}Pp_{2}q^{\prime}\nprec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
,
for every full subgraph
\Lambda^{\prime}\subsetneq\Lambda
.
Proposition
2.5
(2) gives projections
p_{1}\in p_{2}Pp_{2}
,
q_{1}\in M_{\Lambda}
, a nonzero partial isometry
v_{1}\in q_{1}zMp_{1}q^{\prime}
and a
*
-homomorphism
\theta_{1}:p_{1}Pp_{1}\rightarrow q_{1}M_{\Lambda}q_{1}
satisfying
\theta_{1}(x)v_{1}=v_{1}x
, for every
x\in p_{1}Pp_{1}
, and
Q:=\theta_{1}(p_{1}Pp_{1})\nprec_{M_{\Lambda}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
, for every full subgraph
\Lambda^{\prime}\subsetneq\Lambda
.

Let
v=v_{1}v_{2}^{*}
. then
\theta_{1}(x)v=v\theta_{2}(x)
, for every
x\in p_{1}Pp_{1}
. Hence, we have
Qv\subset vM_{\Lambda}
. Since
vv^{*}=v_{1}(v_{2}^{*}v_{2})v_{1}^{*}=v_{1}(p_{2}q^{\prime})v_{1}^{*}=v_{1}v_{1}^{*}\not=0
, we get that
v\not=0
.
Since
Q\nprec_{M_{\Lambda}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
, for every full subgraph
\Lambda^{\prime}\subsetneq\Lambda
, by applying Proposition
3.9
(1) we conclude that
v\in M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}
.
On the other hand, we have
vv^{*}=v_{1}v_{1}^{*}\leq z
and
v^{*}v\leq v_{2}v_{2}^{*}\leq 1-z
. Since
z\in M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}^{\prime}\cap M_{\Gamma}
,
v
and
z
commute and thus
v=zv(1-z)=z(1-z)v=0
, which gives the desired contradiction.
∎

3.5.
A unitary conjugacy result

We continue with a result improving
[
BCC24
, Theorem 5.11]
.

Theorem 3.14
.

Assume the setting from Notation
3.1
.
Let
\Lambda\subset\Gamma
be a full subgraph and
P\subset p(\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{\Gamma})p
be a von Neumann subalgebra, for some
n\geq 1
. Assume that
P\prec_{\mathbb{M}_{n}({\mathbb{C}})\otimes M_{\Gamma}}^{s}\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{\Lambda}
and
P\nprec_{\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{\Gamma}}\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
, for every full subgraph
\Lambda^{\prime}\subsetneq\Lambda
. Assume also that
M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}
is a II
1
factor.

Then there exists a unitary
u\in\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{\Gamma}
such that

•

uPu^{*}\subset\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}
;

•

uPu^{*}\prec^{s}_{\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}}\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{\Lambda}
;

•

if in addition
P
and
P^{\prime}\cap p(\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{\Gamma})p
are factors, then there are a unitary
u^{\prime}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}
and a decomposition
M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}=M_{\Lambda}^{t}\overline{\otimes}M_{\Lambda^{\perp}}^{1/t}
, for some
t>0
, such that
u^{\prime}uPu^{*}u^{\prime*}\subset\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{\Lambda}^{t}
.

In the proof of Theorem
3.14
, we will assume that
n=1
. The fact that one can reduce to this case is a consequence of the following remark.

Remark 3.15
.

Let
(N,\tau)
be any tracial von Neumann algebra. Then the results proved so far in this section apply to von Neumann subalgebras
P\subset p(M_{\Gamma}\overline{\otimes}N)p
, where in all of the statements we replace
M_{\Gamma^{\prime}}
by
M_{\Gamma^{\prime}}\overline{\otimes}N
, for every full subgraph
\Gamma^{\prime}\subset\Gamma
. This is because we can realize
M_{\Gamma}\overline{\otimes}N
as the graph product von Neumann algebra
M_{\widetilde{\Gamma}}
, where
\widetilde{\Gamma}=\Gamma\circ\{v\}
, for a vertex
v
with
M_{v}=N
.

In particular, the above results apply when
P\subset p(M_{\Gamma}\otimes\mathbb{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C}))p
, for some
k\geq 1
. Note that if
\Gamma^{\prime}\subset\Gamma
is a full subgraph, then
P\prec_{M_{\Gamma}\otimes\mathbb{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C})}M_{\Gamma^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbb{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C})
if and only if
P\prec_{M_{\Gamma}\otimes\mathbb{M}_{k}(\mathbb{C})}M_{\Gamma^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbb{C}1
.

To prove Theorem
3.14
, we will use the following fact. The proof of this fact is standard and is left to the reader (see, e.g., the proof of
[
IPP08
, Theorem 5.1]
).

Fact 3.16
.

Let
(M,\tau)
be a tracial von Neumann algebra,
P\subset pMp
a von Neumann subalgebra and
Q\subset M
be a von Neumann subalgebra which is a II
1
factor.
Let
\mathcal{F}
be the family of projections
p^{\prime}\in P^{\prime}\cap pMp
for which there is a unitary
u\in M
such that
uPp^{\prime}u^{*}\subset Q
.
Then the following hold:

(1)

If
p_{0}\in P
is a projection for which there is a unitary
v\in M
such that
vp_{0}Pp_{0}v^{*}\subset Q
, then the central support of
p_{0}
in
P
belongs to
\mathcal{F}
.

(2)

If
p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}
, then the central support of
p^{\prime}
in
P^{\prime}\cap pMp
belongs to
\mathcal{F}
.

(3)

If
\{p_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}\subset\mathcal{F}
is a family of pairwise orthogonal projections, then
\sum_{i\in I}p_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}
.

Proof of Theorem
3.14
.

We assume that
n=1
, so that
P\subset pM_{\Gamma}p
.
Since
M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}
is a II
1
factor, there is a maximal projection
r\in\mathcal{Z}(P^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p)
for which we can find a unitary
u\in M_{\Gamma}
such that
uPru^{*}\subset M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}
. Indeed, using Fact
3.16
(3), we can take
r=\sum_{i\in I}r_{i}
, where
\{r_{i}\}_{i\in I}\subset\mathcal{Z}(P^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p)
is a maximal, with respect to inclusion, family of pairwise orthogonal projections such that for every
i\in I
there is a unitary
u_{i}\in M_{\Gamma}
with
u_{i}Pr_{i}u_{i}^{*}\subset M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}
.

Let
r^{\prime}=p-r\in\mathcal{Z}(P^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p)
and assume by contradiction that
r^{\prime}\not=0
. Then
Pr^{\prime}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda}
and
Pr^{\prime}\nprec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
, for every full subgraph
\Lambda^{\prime}\subsetneq\Lambda
.

By Proposition
2.4
(6), there are projections
p_{1}\in P,q\in M_{\Lambda}
, a nonzero partial isometry
v\in qM_{\Gamma}p_{1}r^{\prime}
and a
*
-homomorphism
\theta:p_{1}Pp_{1}r^{\prime}\rightarrow qM_{\Lambda}q
such that

•

\theta(x)v=vx
, for every
x\in p_{1}Pp_{1}p^{\prime}
, and

•

\theta(p_{1}Pp_{1}r^{\prime})\nprec_{M_{\Lambda}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
, for every full subgraph
\Lambda^{\prime}\subsetneq\Lambda
.

Since
v^{*}v\in(p_{1}Pp_{1}r^{\prime})^{\prime}\cap p_{1}r^{\prime}M_{\Gamma}p_{1}r^{\prime}=(P^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p)r^{\prime}p_{1}
, we can find a projection
r^{\prime\prime}\in P^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p
such that
r^{\prime\prime}\leq r^{\prime}
and
v^{*}v=p_{1}r^{\prime\prime}
.
By Proposition
3.9
(1), we get that
\theta(p_{1}Pp_{1}r^{\prime})^{\prime}\cap qM_{\Gamma}q\subset qM_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}q
and thus
vv^{*}\in qM_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}q
.
Hence we conclude that

(3.8)

vp_{1}Pp_{1}r^{\prime\prime}v^{*}=\theta(p_{1}Pp_{1}r^{\prime})vv^{*}\subset M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}.

Let
z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}(Pr^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{Z}(P)r^{\prime\prime}
be the central support of
p_{1}r^{\prime\prime}
in
Pr^{\prime\prime}
. Then
z^{\prime}\in P^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p
. Let
z\in\mathcal{Z}(P^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p)
be the central support of
z^{\prime}
in
P^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p
.
We have
p_{1}r^{\prime\prime}\leq r^{\prime\prime}\leq r^{\prime}=p-r
. Since
z^{\prime}=\vee_{u\in\mathcal{U}(Pr^{\prime\prime})}up_{1}r^{\prime\prime}u^{\ast}
, and
up_{1}r^{\prime\prime}u^{*}\leq p-r
for every
u\in\mathcal{U}(Pr^{\prime\prime})
, we have
z^{\prime}\leq p-r
. Taking the central support in
P^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p
, we deduce that
z\leq p-r
.
Since
v^{*}v=p_{1}r^{\prime\prime}
and
M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}
is a factor,
using Fact
3.16
, (1) and (2), we find a unitary
w\in M_{\Gamma}
such that
wPzw^{*}\subset M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}
.
By using again that
M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}
is a factor and Fact
3.16
(3) we find a unitary
\xi\in M_{\Gamma}
such that
\xi P(r+z)\xi^{*}\subset M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}
. As
z\not=0
, this contradicts the maximality of
r
. Thus,
r^{\prime}=0
, hence,
r=p
and
uPu^{*}\subset qM_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}q
, where
q=upu^{*}
.

Put
Q=uPu^{*}
.
Since
Q\subset qM_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}q
and
Q\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\Lambda}
, Lemma
2.18
implies that

Q\prec^{s}_{M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}}M_{\Lambda}
.

To prove the last assertion of the theorem, assume that
P
and
P^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p
.
Thus,
Q
and
Q^{\prime}\cap qM_{\Gamma}q
are factors. Since
Q\nprec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
, for every full subgraph
\Lambda^{\prime}\subsetneq\Lambda
, by Proposition
3.9
(1) we deduce that
Q^{\prime}\cap qM_{\Gamma}q\subset M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}
. Thus,
Q^{\prime}\cap qM_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}q=Q^{\prime}\cap qM_{\Gamma}q
is a factor.
Since
M_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\perp}}=M_{\Lambda}\overline{\otimes}M_{\Lambda^{\perp}}
, Lemma
2.8
(1) gives the moreover assertion.
∎

3.6.
A criterion for collapsibility

We end this section with the following criterion for a subgraph to be collapsible
(see Section
2.6
for the definition). This criterion will be needed in the proofs of Theorems
B
and
C
.

Proposition 3.17
.

Let
\Gamma,\Lambda
be finite simple graphs. Let
(M_{v},\tau_{v})_{v\in\Gamma},(N_{w},\tau_{w})_{w\in\Lambda}
be families of diffuse tracial von Neumann algebras such that the associated graph product von Neumann algebras
M_{\Gamma}=*_{v,\Gamma}M_{v}
and
N_{\Lambda}=*_{w,\Lambda}N_{w}
are II
1
factors.
Let
v\in\Gamma
and
C\subset\Lambda
be a full subgraph.
Let
\theta:M_{\Gamma}\rightarrow rPr
be a
*
-isomorphism, where
P=\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes N_{\Lambda}
, for some
n\geq 1
, and
r\in P
is a projection.
Identify
M_{\Gamma}=rPr
, via
\theta
, and
N_{\Lambda}=sPs
, where
s=e_{1,1}\otimes 1
.

Assume that
M_{v}p\prec_{P}^{s}N_{C\cup C^{\perp}}q
and
N_{C}q\prec_{P}^{s}M_{v}p
, for some nonzero projections
p\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{v}^{\prime}\cap rPr)
and
q\in\mathcal{Z}(N_{C}^{\prime}\cap sPs)
.

Then
C
is a collapsible subgraph of
\Lambda
. Moreover,
M_{\emph{st}(v)}p\prec_{P}^{s}N_{C\cup C^{\perp}}q
.

Proof.

We will treat the case where
n=1
(so in particular
s=1
). The general case can be handled using Remark
3.15
; note that the argument from Remark
3.15
, working with the graph
\tilde{\Lambda}=\Lambda\circ\{\ast\}
, introduces a vertex group which is not diffuse, but the following proof can be checked to work in this setting.

We first note that
\mathcal{Z}(M_{v}^{\prime}\cap rPr)=\mathcal{Z}(M_{\text{st}(v)}^{\prime}\cap rPr)
. Indeed, since
\text{st}(v)^{\perp}=\emptyset
, Lemma
2.16
(1) implies that
\mathcal{Z}(M_{v}^{\prime}\cap rPr)=\mathcal{Z}(M_{\text{st}(v)}^{\prime}\cap rPr)=\mathcal{Z}(M_{v})\overline{\otimes}\mathcal{Z}(M_{B})
, where
B
is the maximal clique factor of
\text{lk}(v)
. In particular
p\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{\text{st}(v)}^{\prime}\cap rPr)
.

Next, we claim that

(3.9)

N_{C}q\prec_{P}M_{v}p^{\prime}
, for every nonzero projection
p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{\text{st}(v)}^{\prime}\cap rPr)p
.

Assume by contradiction that
N_{C}q\nprec_{P}M_{v}p^{\prime}
, for some nonzero projection
p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{\text{st}(v)}^{\prime}\cap rPr)p
. Since
N_{C}q\prec_{P}^{s}M_{v}p
, we get that
N_{C}q\prec_{P}^{s}M_{v}(p-p^{\prime})
.
Since
N_{C}
is diffuse, we have
N_{C}q\nprec M_{\emptyset}=\mathbb{C}1
.

We now observe that

(3.10)

N_{C\cup C^{\perp}}q\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\text{st}(v)}(p-p^{\prime})

To see this, let
r_{0}\in N_{C}
be any projection with
\tau(r_{0}q)\leq\tau(r)
. Since
P
is a II
1
factor, we can find a partial isometry
\delta\in P
with
\delta^{*}\delta=r_{0}q
and
\delta\delta^{*}\leq r
, such that
\delta r_{0}N_{C}r_{0}q\delta^{*}\subset rPr=M_{\Gamma}
.
Since
N_{C}q\prec_{P}^{s}M_{v}(p-p^{\prime})
, we get that
\delta r_{0}N_{C}r_{0}q\delta^{*}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{v}(p-p^{\prime})
.
Since
p-p^{\prime}\in M_{\text{st}(v)}^{\prime}\cap rPr
, Proposition
3.10
gives that
\delta r_{0}N_{C\cup C^{\perp}}r_{0}q\delta^{*}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\text{st}(v)}(p-p^{\prime})
. Since this holds for any projection
r_{0}\in N_{C}
satisfying
\tau(r_{0}q)\leq\tau(r)
, (
3.10
) follows.

Since
M_{v}p\prec_{P}^{s}N_{C\cup C^{\perp}}q
,
equation (
3.10
) combined with Proposition
2.4
(2) implies that
M_{v}p\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\text{st}(v)}(p-p^{\prime})
. Since obviously
M_{v}p\prec_{P}^{s}M_{v}
, by using Corollary
3.11
and that
p-p^{\prime}\in M_{\text{st}(v)}^{\prime}\cap rPr
, we get that
M_{v}p\prec_{P}^{s}M_{v}(p-p^{\prime})
. Thus,
M_{v}p^{\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{v}(r-p^{\prime})
,
which contradicts Corollary
3.13
as
p^{\prime}\in M_{\text{st}(v)}^{\prime}\cap rPr
is a nonzero projection. This finishes the proof of (
3.9
).

We continue by proving the moreover assertion of the proposition:

(3.11)

M_{\text{st}(v)}p\prec_{P}^{s}N_{C\cup C^{\perp}}q.

To this end, let
p_{0}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{\text{st}(v)}^{\prime}\cap rPr)p
be a nonzero projection.
Since
M_{v}p_{0}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{C\cup C^{\perp}}
, our existence result for hulls (Proposition
3.7
) ensures that we can find a nonzero projection
p_{1}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{\text{st}(v)}^{\prime}\cap P)p_{0}
and a full subgraph
D\subset\Gamma
such that
M_{v}p_{1}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{D}
and
M_{v}p_{1}\nprec_{P}N_{D^{\prime}}
, for every full subgraph
D^{\prime}\subsetneq D
. Corollary
3.5
then ensures that
D\subset C\cup C^{\perp}
.

Since
M_{v}p_{1}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{C\cup C^{\perp}}q
,
q\in\mathcal{Z}(N_{C}^{\prime}\cap P)\subset N_{C\cup C^{\perp}}^{\prime}\cap P
,
M_{v}p_{1}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{D}
and
D\subset C\cup C^{\perp}
, Corollary
3.11
implies that
M_{v}p_{1}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{D}q
.
As
N_{C}q\prec_{P}M_{v}p_{1}
by (
3.9
), Proposition
2.4
(2) gives that
N_{C}q\prec_{P}N_{D}q
. Hence,
N_{C}\prec_{P}N_{D}
, and Lemma
2.16
(2) gives that
C\subset D
, thus
D^{\perp}\subset C^{\perp}
. Since
D\subset C\cup C^{\perp}
, we get that
D\cup D^{\perp}\subset C\cup C^{\perp}
. As
q\in N_{C\cup C^{\perp}}^{\prime}\cap P
, we get that
q\in N_{D\cup D^{\perp}}^{\prime}\cap P
.

Next, since
M_{v}p_{1}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{D}q
and
M_{v}p_{1}\nprec_{P}N_{D^{\prime}}
, for every full subgraph
D^{\prime}\subsetneq D
, Proposition
3.10
implies that
M_{\text{st}(v)}p_{1}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{D\cup D^{\perp}}q
.
Using this fact and again that
D\cup D^{\perp}\subset C\cup C^{\perp}
, we get that
M_{\text{st}(v)}p_{1}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{C\cup C^{\perp}}q
. Since
p_{1}\leq p_{0}
, we get that
M_{\text{st}(v)}p_{0}\prec_{P}N_{C\cup C^{\perp}}q
. Since the latter holds for every nonzero projection
p_{0}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{\mathrm{st}(v)}^{\prime}\cap rPr)
, Remark
2.2
(5) ensures that (
3.11
) holds.

Finally, let
w\in C
. Since
N_{C}q\prec_{P}^{s}M_{v}p
, we get that
N_{w}q\prec_{P}^{s}M_{v}p
. By applying Proposition
3.10
(arguing as in the proof of Equation (
3.10
)) we derive that

N_{\text{st}(w)}q\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\text{st}(v)}p
. In combination with (
3.11
) and Proposition
2.4
(2) it follows that
N_{\text{st}{(w)}}q\prec_{P}N_{C\cup C^{\perp}}q
. By Lemma
2.16
(2) this gives that
\text{st}(w)\subset C\cup C^{\perp}
. Hence, we have that
\text{st}(w)\cap(\Gamma\setminus C)=C^{\perp}
, for every
w\in C
, which implies that
C
is collapsible.
∎

4.
Commuting subalgebras of graph product von Neumann algebras

4.1.
A structural result

The main goal of this section is to prove the following result concerning the structure of commuting subalgebras of graph product von Neumann algebras.

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
\Gamma
be a finite simple graph,
(M_{v},\tau_{v})_{v\in\Gamma}
a family of tracial von Neumann algebras, and
M_{\Gamma}=*_{v,\Gamma}M_{v}
the associated graph product von Neumann algebra. Let
P_{1},P_{2}\subset pM_{\Gamma}p
be commuting von Neumann subalgebras.

Then there exist four pairwise disjoint, possibly empty, full subgraphs
\Gamma_{i}\subset\Gamma
, for
0\leq i\leq 3
, and a nonzero projection
q\in\mathcal{Z}((P_{1}\vee P_{2})^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p)
such that the following conditions hold:

(a)

\Gamma_{0}
is a clique;

(b)

\Gamma_{i}
has an empty maximal clique factor, for every
1\leq i\leq 3
;

(c)

\Gamma_{i}\subset\Gamma_{j}^{\perp}
, for every
0\leq i<j\leq 3
;

(d)

P_{i}q\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\Gamma_{0}\cup\Gamma_{i}\cup\Gamma_{3}}
, for every
i\in\{1,2\}
;

(e)

(P_{1}\vee P_{2})q\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\Gamma_{0}\cup\Gamma_{1}\cup\Gamma_{2}\cup\Gamma_{3}}
;

(f)

(P_{1}\vee P_{2})q
is amenable relative to
M_{\Gamma_{0}\cup\Gamma_{1}\cup\Gamma_{2}}
inside
M_{\Gamma}
.

Moreover, if
P_{1}\vee P_{2}\prec^{s}_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda}
, for a full subgraph
\Lambda\subset\Gamma
, then we may take
\Gamma_{i}\subset\Lambda
, for every
0\leq i\leq 3
.

In order to prove Theorem
4.1
, we use
[
Va13
, Theorem A]
(generalizing
[
Io15
, Theorem 1.6]
) to control commuting subalgebras inside graph product von Neumann algebras. First, we note that
[
Va13
, Theorem A]
readily implies the following.

Theorem 4.2
.

Let
(M_{1},\tau_{1}),(M_{2},\tau_{2})
be tracial von Neumann algebras with a common von Neumann subalgebra
(B,\tau)
such that
{\tau_{1}}_{|B}={\tau_{2}}_{|B}=\tau

and denote
M=M_{1}*_{B}M_{2}
. Let
P_{1},P_{2}\subset pMp
be two commuting von Neumann subalgebras. Then at least one of the following conditions holds:

(1)

P_{1}\prec_{M}M_{1}
or
P_{1}\prec_{M}M_{2}
.

(2)

P_{2}\prec_{M}M_{1}
or
P_{2}\prec_{M}M_{2}
.

(3)

P_{1}\vee P_{2}
is amenable relative to
B
inside
M
.

Proof.

Assuming that (1) and (2) fail, we show that (3) holds. Since
P_{1}\nprec_{M}M_{1}
and
P_{1}\nprec_{M}M_{2}
by
[
BO08
, Corollary F.14]
we can find a diffuse abelian von Neumann subalgebra
A\subset P_{1}
such that
A\nprec_{M}M_{1}
and
A\nprec_{M}M_{2}
. Since
A
is amenable, by
[
Va13
, Theorem A]
we get that
\mathcal{N}_{pMp}(A)^{\prime\prime}
is amenable relative to
B
inside
M
. Since
P_{2}\subset\mathcal{N}_{pMp}(A)^{\prime\prime}
, we deduce that
P_{2}
is amenable relative to
B
inside
M
. By applying
[
Va13
, Theorem A]
again and using that
P_{2}\nprec_{M}M_{1}
and
P_{2}\nprec_{M}M_{2}
, we derive that
\mathcal{N}_{pMp}(P_{2})^{\prime\prime}
is amenable relative to
B
inside
M
. Since
P_{1}\vee P_{2}\subset\mathcal{N}_{pMp}(P_{2})^{\prime\prime}
, it follows that

P_{1}\vee P_{2}
is amenable relative to
B
inside
M
, so (3) holds, as claimed.
∎

To prove Theorem
4.1
we will also need the following result from
[
BCC24
]
.

Theorem 4.3

(​​
[
BCC24
, Theorem 5.3]
)
.

Let
\Gamma
be a finite simple graph,
(M_{v},\tau_{v})_{v\in\Gamma}
a family of tracial von Neumann algebras, and
M_{\Gamma}=*_{v,\Gamma}M_{v}
the associated graph product von Neumann algebra. Let
P\subset pM_{\Gamma}p
be a von Neumann subalgebra and
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\subset\Gamma
full subgraphs
such that
P
is amenable relative to
M_{\Gamma_{1}}
and
M_{\Gamma_{2}}
inside
M_{\Gamma}
.
Then
P
is amenable relative to
M_{\Gamma_{1}\cap\Gamma_{2}}
inside
M_{\Gamma}
.

Combining Theorems
4.2
and
4.3
allows to derive the following corollary.

Corollary 4.4
.

Let
\Gamma
be a finite simple graph,
(M_{v},\tau_{v})_{v\in\Gamma}
a family of tracial von Neumann algebras, and
M_{\Gamma}=*_{v,\Gamma}M_{v}
the associated graph product von Neumann algebra. Let
P_{1},P_{2}\subset pM_{\Gamma}p
be commuting von Neumann subalgebras, and
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\subset\Gamma
be full subgraphs.
Assume that for every
i\in\{1,2\}
, we have
P_{i}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\Gamma_{i}}
and
P_{i}\nprec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Gamma_{i}^{\prime}}
, for every full subgraph
\Gamma_{i}^{\prime}\subsetneq\Gamma_{i}
.
Let
\Lambda\subset\Gamma
be the full subgraph consisting of vertices
v\in\Gamma_{1}\cap\Gamma_{2}
such that
\Gamma_{1}\not\subset\emph{st}(v)
and
\Gamma_{2}\not\subset\emph{st}(v)
.

Then
P_{1}\vee P_{2}
is amenable relative to
M_{\Lambda^{\perp}}
inside
M_{\Gamma}
.

Proof.

Let
v\in\Lambda
and
i\in\{1,2\}
. Then
\Gamma_{i}\not\subset\Gamma\setminus\{v\}
and
\Gamma_{i}\not\subset\text{st}(v)
. Since by assumption,
M_{\Gamma_{i}}
is the hull of
P_{i}
, the moreover part of Proposition
3.7
implies that
P_{i}\nprec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Gamma\setminus\{v\}}
and
P_{i}\nprec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\text{st}(v)}
. Since
M_{\Gamma}=M_{\Gamma\setminus\{v\}}*_{M_{\mathrm{lk}(v)}}M_{\text{st}(v)}
, Theorem
4.2
implies that
P_{1}\vee P_{2}
is amenable relative to
M_{\text{lk}(v)}
inside
M_{\Gamma}
, for every
v\in\Lambda
. By applying Theorem
4.3
finitely many times we conclude that
P_{1}\vee P_{2}
is amenable relative to
M_{\cap_{v\in\Lambda}\text{lk}(v)}=M_{\Lambda^{\perp}}
inside
M_{\Gamma}
.
∎

We are now ready to prove Theorem
4.1
.

Proof of Theorem
4.1
.

Put
\mathcal{Z}=\mathcal{Z}((P_{1}\vee P_{2})^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p)
. Since
P_{1}\vee P_{2}\subset\mathcal{N}_{pM_{\Gamma}p}(P_{1})^{\prime\prime}\cap\mathcal{N}_{pM_{\Gamma}p}(P_{2})^{\prime\prime}
, we get that
\mathcal{N}_{pM_{\Gamma}p}(P_{i})^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p\subset\mathcal{Z}
, for every
i\in\{1,2\}
. Proposition
3.7
implies that we can find a nonzero projection
q\in\mathcal{Z}
, and, for every
i\in\{1,2\}
, a full subgraph
\Lambda_{i}\subset\Gamma
such that

(4.1)

P_{i}q\prec^{s}_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{i}}
and
P_{i}q\nprec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda^{\prime}_{i}}
, for every full subgraph
\Lambda_{i}^{\prime}\subset\Gamma
such that
\Lambda_{i}\not\subset\Lambda_{i}^{\prime}
.

By applying Proposition
3.9
(2) we get that

(4.2)

(P_{1}\vee P_{2})q\prec^{s}_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{i}\cup\Lambda_{i}^{\perp}}
, for every
i\in\{1,2\}
.

In particular
P_{1}q\prec^{s}_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{2}\cup\Lambda_{2}^{\perp}}
and
P_{2}q\prec^{s}_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{1}^{\perp}}
. By combining with (
4.1
) we get that

(4.3)

\Lambda_{1}\subset\Lambda_{2}\cup\Lambda_{2}^{\perp}
and
\Lambda_{2}\subset\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{1}^{\perp}
.

Let
\Gamma_{3}
be the set of
v\in\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda_{2}
such that
\Lambda_{1}\not\subset\text{st}(v)
and
\Lambda_{2}\not\subset\text{st}(v)
.
Define
\Gamma_{0}=(\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda_{2})\setminus\Gamma_{3}
,
\Gamma_{1}=\Lambda_{1}\setminus\Lambda_{2}
and
\Gamma_{2}=\Lambda_{1}^{\perp}
.
Then the graphs
\Gamma_{i}
,
0\leq i\leq 3
, are pairwise disjoint by definition.

If
v\in\Gamma_{0}
, then
\Lambda_{1}\subset\text{st}(v)
or
\Lambda_{2}\subset\text{st}(v)
, therefore
\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda_{2}\subset\text{st}(v)
. This shows that
\Gamma_{0}
is contained in the maximal clique factor of
\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda_{2}
. Thus,
\Gamma_{0}
is a clique, which proves (a), and
\Gamma_{0}\subset\Gamma_{3}^{\perp}
.

If
i\in\{0,1,3\}
, then
\Gamma_{i}\subset\Lambda_{1}
and since
\Gamma_{2}=\Lambda_{1}^{\perp}
we get that
\Gamma_{i}\subset\Gamma_{2}^{\perp}
.
Recall that
\Gamma_{1}=\Lambda_{1}\setminus\Lambda_{2}
, so by (
4.3
) we get
\Gamma_{1}\subset\Lambda_{2}^{\perp}
.
If
i\in\{0,3\}
, then since
\Gamma_{i}\subset\Lambda_{2}
, we deduce that
\Gamma_{i}\subset\Gamma_{1}^{\perp}
, which altogether proves (c).

Since
\Gamma_{0}\cup\Gamma_{3}=\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda_{2}
, we get that
\Gamma_{0}\cup\Gamma_{1}\cup\Gamma_{3}=\Lambda_{1}
and

\Gamma_{0}\cup\Gamma_{2}\cup\Gamma_{3}=(\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda_{2})\cup\Lambda_{1}^{\perp}
. The latter equality and (
4.3
) imply that
\Lambda_{2}\subset\Gamma_{0}\cup\Gamma_{2}\cup\Gamma_{3}
. By (
4.1
) we deduce that (d) holds.

Since we also have that
\Gamma_{0}\cup\Gamma_{1}\cup\Gamma_{2}\cup\Gamma_{3}=\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{1}^{\perp}
, (e) holds by (
4.2
).

Towards proving (f), we note first that (
4.1
) together with Corollary
4.4
gives that
(P_{1}\vee P_{2})q
is amenable relative to
M_{\Gamma_{3}^{\perp}}
inside
M_{\Gamma}
. By (e), Proposition
2.4
(4) implies that
(P_{1}\vee P_{2})q
is amenable relative to
M_{\Gamma_{0}\cup\Gamma_{1}\cup\Gamma_{2}\cup\Gamma_{3}}
inside
M_{\Gamma}
. Theorem
4.3
implies that
(P_{1}\vee P_{2})q
is amenable relative to
M_{\Gamma_{3}^{\perp}\cap(\Gamma_{0}\cup\Gamma_{1}\cup\Gamma_{2}\cup\Gamma_{3})}=M_{\Gamma_{0}\cup\Gamma_{1}\cup\Gamma_{2}}
inside
M_{\Gamma}
, which proves (f). Altogether, we proved (a) and (c)-(f).

Finally, to justify (b), let
C_{i}
be the maximal clique factor of
\Gamma_{i}
, for every
1\leq i\leq 3
. Then it is clear that conditions (a) and (c)-(f) still hold if we replace the quadruple
(\Gamma_{0},\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\Gamma_{3})
by
(\Gamma_{0}^{\prime},\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},\Gamma_{3}^{\prime})=(\Gamma_{0}\cup C_{1}\cup C_{2}\cup C_{3},\Gamma_{1}\setminus C_{1},\Gamma_{2}\setminus C_{2},\Gamma_{3}\setminus C_{3})
. Additionally, since
\Gamma_{i}^{\prime}
has an empty maximal clique factor, for every
1\leq i\leq 3
, (b) is also satisfied.

To prove the moreover assertion, assume that
P_{1}\vee P_{2}\prec^{s}_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda}
, for a full subgraph
