\Lambda\subset\Gamma
. By Proposition
2.4
(4) we get that
P_{1}\vee P_{2}
is amenable relative to
M_{\Lambda}
inside
M_{\Gamma}
. Using Corollary
3.5
and Theorem
4.3
it follows that conditions (a) and (c)-(f) hold after we replace
\Gamma_{i}
by
\Gamma_{i}\cap\Lambda
, for every
0\leq i\leq 3
.
Thus, we may find
\Gamma_{i}\subset\Lambda
, for
0\leq i\leq 3
, so that conditions (a) and (c)-(f) hold.
By repeating the previous paragraph we can also ensure that (b) is satisfied.
∎

4.2.
Corollaries of Theorem
4.1

As a consequence of Theorem
4.1
, we derive two useful results.
The reader is referred to Section
2.6
for the notion of a join subgraph.

Corollary 4.5
.

Let
\Gamma
be a finite simple graph,
(M_{v},\tau_{v})_{v\in\Gamma}
a family of tracial von Neumann algebras, and
M_{\Gamma}=*_{v,\Gamma}M_{v}
the associated graph product von Neumann algebra. Let
P_{1},P_{2}\subset p(M_{\Gamma}\otimes\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C}))p
be diffuse commuting von Neumann subalgebras, for some
n\geq 1
. Assume that
P_{1}\vee P_{2}
has no amenable direct summand and
P_{1}\vee P_{2}\nprec_{M_{\Gamma}\otimes\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})}M_{v}\otimes\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
, for every isolated vertex
v\in\Gamma
.

Then there exists a join full subgraph
\Lambda\subset\Gamma
such that

P_{1}\vee P_{2}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}\otimes\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})}M_{\Lambda}\otimes\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
.

Proof.

As in Remark
3.15
, we let
\tilde{\Gamma}=\Gamma\circ\{v_{0}\}
, and view
M_{\Gamma}\otimes\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
as a graph product
M_{\tilde{\Gamma}}
where the vertex algebra on
v_{0}
is
\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
.

Assume by contradiction that the conclusion fails. Theorem
4.1
provides pairwise disjoint full subgraphs
\tilde{\Gamma}_{i}\subset\tilde{\Gamma}
,
0\leq i\leq 3
, and a nonzero projection
q\in\mathcal{Z}((P_{1}\vee P_{2})^{\prime}\cap pM_{\tilde{\Gamma}}p))
such that conditions (a)-(f) hold. For every
i\in\{0,1,2,3\}
, we let
\Gamma_{i}:=\tilde{\Gamma}_{i}\cap\Gamma
. Since the conclusion fails, conditions (c) and (e) imply that at most one of the graphs
\Gamma_{i}
,
0\leq i\leq 3
, is non-empty
(in fact exactly one, using that
P_{1},P_{2}
are diffuse). If
\Gamma_{0}=\Gamma_{1}=\Gamma_{2}=\emptyset
, then (f) would imply that
(P_{1}\vee P_{2})q
is amenable, contradicting that
P_{1}\vee P_{2}
has no amenable direct summand. If
i\in\{1,2\}
and
\Gamma_{0}=\Gamma_{i}=\Gamma_{3}=\emptyset
, then (d) would imply that
P_{i}q\prec_{M_{\tilde{\Gamma}}}\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
, contradicting that
P_{i}
is diffuse. Altogether, we must have that
\Gamma_{1}=\Gamma_{2}=\Gamma_{3}=\emptyset
. By (e) we get that
(P_{1}\vee P_{2})q\prec_{M_{\tilde{\Gamma}}}^{s}M_{\Gamma_{0}}\otimes\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
. Since
\Gamma_{0}
is a clique by (a) and the conclusion is assumed to fail, we conclude that
\Gamma_{0}=\{v\}
, for some
v\in\Gamma
.
Hence,
P_{1}\vee P_{2}\prec_{M_{\tilde{\Gamma}}}M_{v}\otimes\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
.
By the hypothesis
v\in\Gamma
is not isolated, so there is
v^{\prime}\in\text{lk}(v)
. But then
P_{1}\vee P_{2}\prec_{M_{\tilde{\Gamma}}}M_{\{v,v^{\prime}\}}\otimes\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
, which also gives a contradiction as
\{v,v^{\prime}\}
is a join graph.
∎

Corollary 4.6
.

Let
\Gamma
be a finite simple graph,
(M_{v},\tau_{v})_{v\in\Gamma}
a family of diffuse tracial von Neumann algebras, and
M_{\Gamma}=*_{v,\Gamma}M_{v}
the associated graph product von Neumann algebra. Let
P_{1},P_{2}\subset pM_{\Gamma}p
be diffuse commuting von Neumann subalgebras. Put
P=P_{1}\vee P_{2}
. Let
\Lambda\subset\Gamma
be a full subgraph, and
q\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{\Lambda}^{\prime}\cap M_{\Gamma})
a nonzero projection.

Assume that
P\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\Lambda}q
and
M_{\Lambda}q\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}P
. Assume also that
Pp^{\prime}\nprec_{M_{\Gamma}}P(p-p^{\prime})
, for every nonzero projection
p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}(P^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p)
.
Let
p_{0}\in\mathcal{Z}(P^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p)
be a nonzero projection.

Then there exist a nonzero projection
p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}(P^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p)
with
p^{\prime}\leq p_{0}
, and disjoint full subgraphs
\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}\subset\Lambda
such that

•

\Lambda\setminus(\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{2})
is the maximal clique factor
C

of
\Lambda
,

•

\Lambda_{1}\subset\Lambda_{2}^{\perp}
, and

•

P_{i}p^{\prime}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{C\cup\Lambda_{i}}q
, for every
i\in\{1,2\}
.

Remark 4.7
.

The corollary remains true for subalgebras
P_{1},P_{2}\subset p(M_{\Gamma}\otimes\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C}))p
, for some
n\geq 1
. Again this can be derived using Remark
3.15
.

Proof.

Since
Pp_{0}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda}q
, the moreover assertion of Theorem
4.1
provides pairwise disjoint full subgraphs
\Lambda_{i}\subset\Lambda
(with
0\leq i\leq 3
), and a nonzero projection
p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}(P^{\prime}\cap pM_{\Gamma}p)
such that
p^{\prime}\leq p_{0}
and the following hold:

(a)

\Lambda_{0}
is a clique;

(b)

\Lambda_{i}
has an empty maximal clique factor, for every
1\leq i\leq 3
;

(c)

\Lambda_{i}\subset\Lambda_{j}^{\perp}
, for every
0\leq i<j\leq 3
;

(d)

P_{i}p^{\prime}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\Lambda_{0}\cup\Lambda_{i}\cup\Lambda_{3}}
, for every
i\in\{1,2\}
;

(e)

Pp^{\prime}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\Lambda_{0}\cup\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{2}\cup\Lambda_{3}}
;

(f)

Pp^{\prime}
is amenable relative to
M_{\Lambda_{0}\cup\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{2}}
inside
M_{\Gamma}
.

We first claim that
M_{\Lambda}q\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}Pp^{\prime}
. Otherwise, since
M_{\Lambda}q\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}P
, we get
M_{\Lambda}q\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}P(p-p^{\prime})
.
Since
Pp^{\prime}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda}q
, Proposition
2.4
(2) implies that
Pp^{\prime}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}P(p-p^{\prime})
, which is a contradiction.

Second, we claim that
\Lambda_{0}\cup\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{2}\cup\Lambda_{3}=\Lambda
. If
v\in\Lambda
, then since
M_{\Lambda}q\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}Pp^{\prime}
, we derive that
M_{v}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}Pp^{\prime}
. This fact, (e) and Proposition
2.4
(2) together imply that
M_{v}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\Lambda_{0}\cup\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{2}\cup\Lambda_{3}}
. By Lemma
2.16
(2), we get that
v\in\Lambda_{0}\cup\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{2}\cup\Lambda_{3}
, which proves our claim.

Third, we claim that
\Lambda_{0}
is the maximal clique factor
C
of
\Lambda
. The previous claim together with (b) ensures that
C\subset\Lambda_{0}
, and together with (c), it ensures that
\Lambda\subset\Lambda_{0}\cup\Lambda_{0}^{\perp}
, so
\Lambda_{0}
is a union of join factors of
\Lambda
. Since by (a)
\Lambda_{0}
is a clique, it follows that
\Lambda_{0}\subset C
, which proves our claim.

Fourth, we claim that
\Lambda_{3}=\emptyset
.
Assume by contradiction that
\Lambda_{3}\not=\emptyset
. Then (b) implies that
\Lambda_{3}
is not a clique.
Since
M_{\Lambda}q\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}Pp^{\prime}
, by Proposition
2.4
(3), we can find a nonzero projection
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{\Lambda}^{\prime}\cap M_{\Gamma})
such that
q^{\prime}\leq q
and
M_{\Lambda}q^{\prime}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}Pp^{\prime}
. Thus, we have
M_{\Lambda}q^{\prime}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}Pp^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{C}(1-p^{\prime})
, and hence that
M_{\Lambda}q^{\prime}
is amenable relative to
Pp^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{C}(1-p^{\prime})
inside
M_{\Gamma}
by Proposition
2.4
(4). By (f), we also get that
Pp^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{C}(1-p^{\prime})
is amenable relative to
M_{\Lambda_{0}\cup\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{2}}
inside
M_{\Gamma}
.
The last two facts and
[
OP10
, Proposition 2.4(3)]
together imply that
M_{\Lambda}q^{\prime}
is amenable relative to
M_{\Lambda_{0}\cup\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{2}}
inside
M_{\Gamma}
. In particular,
M_{\Lambda_{3}}q^{\prime}
is amenable relative to
M_{\Lambda_{0}\cup\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{2}}
inside
M_{\Gamma}
. Since
M_{\Lambda_{3}}q^{\prime}
is also amenable relative to
M_{\Lambda_{3}}
inside
M_{\Gamma}
and
\Lambda_{3}\cap(\Lambda_{0}\cup\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{2})=\emptyset
, Theorem
4.3

implies that
M_{\Lambda_{3}}q^{\prime}
is amenable.
By Lemma
2.16
(3), this contradicts the fact that
\Lambda_{3}
is not a clique.

Thus, we have that
\Lambda\setminus(\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{2})=\Lambda_{0}
, which is the clique factor of
\Lambda
, and
\Lambda_{1}\subset\Lambda_{2}^{\perp}
. If
i\in\{1,2\}
, then (d) gives that
P_{i}p^{\prime}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\Lambda_{0}\cup\Lambda_{i}}
. Since
P\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\Lambda}q
, we also have that
P_{i}p^{\prime}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\Lambda}q
. By combining the last two facts and using Corollary
3.11
we get that
P_{i}p^{\prime}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}^{s}M_{\Lambda_{0}\cup\Lambda_{i}}q
, which finishes the proof.
∎

5.
Strong primeness: Proof of Theorem
I

In this section, we use Corollary
4.6
to prove Theorem
I
.
It was shown in
[
BCC24
, Theorem B]
that if
\Gamma
is an irreducible graph with
|\Gamma|\geq 2
, then
M_{\Gamma}
is a prime II
1
factor, whenever all the vertex algebras
(M_{v})_{v\in\Gamma}
are II
1
factors. Towards proving Theorem
I
, we first use Corollary
4.6
to strengthen this fact by showing that
M_{\Gamma}
is strongly prime in the following sense defined by Isono.

Definition 5.1

(​​
[
Is14
, Section 5]
)
.

A II
1
factor
M
is called
strongly prime
if for any II
1
factors
N,P_{1},P_{2}
with
M\overline{\otimes}N=P_{1}\overline{\otimes}P_{2}
, there exist
u\in\mathcal{U}(M\overline{\otimes}N)
and
t>0
such that after identifying
P_{1}\overline{\otimes}P_{2}=P_{1}^{t}\overline{\otimes}P_{2}^{1/t}
, we have that
uMu^{*}\subset P_{1}^{t}
or
uMu^{*}\subset P_{2}^{1/t}
.

Theorem 5.2
.

Let
\Gamma
be an irreducible finite simple graph with
|\Gamma|\geq 2
,

(M_{v},\tau_{v})_{v\in\Gamma}
be a family of diffuse tracial von Neumann algebras, and
M_{\Gamma}=*_{v,\Gamma}M_{v}
the associated graph product von Neumann algebra.

Then
M_{\Gamma}
is a strongly prime II
1
factor.

Proof.

Since
\Gamma
is irreducible, it follows from Lemma
2.16
(1) that
M_{\Gamma}
is a II
1
factor.

Let
N,P_{1},P_{2}
be II
1
factors such that
M_{\Gamma}\overline{\otimes}N=P
, where
P=P_{1}\overline{\otimes}P_{2}
.
Let
v
be a vertex which does not belong to
\Gamma
and define
\widetilde{\Gamma}=\Gamma\circ\{v\}
. Put
M_{v}=N
and notice that
M_{\widetilde{\Gamma}}=M_{\Gamma}\overline{\otimes}N=P
. Since
\Gamma
is irreducible and
|\Gamma|\geq 2
, the maximal clique factor of
\widetilde{\Gamma}
is equal to
\{v\}
.

Note that since
M_{\Gamma}
and
N
are II
1
factors, we have
\mathcal{Z}(P)=\mathbb{C}1
. Therefore, by applying Corollary
4.6
(with
\Lambda=\tilde{\Gamma}
) we can find a decomposition of
\Gamma=\widetilde{\Gamma}\setminus\{v\}=\Gamma_{1}\cup\Gamma_{2}
such that
\Gamma_{1}\subset\Gamma_{2}^{\perp}
and
P_{i}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\{v\}\cup\Gamma_{i}}
, for every
i\in\{1,2\}
.
Since
\Gamma
is irreducible, we have
\Gamma_{i}=\emptyset
, for some
i\in\{1,2\}
. Let
j\in\{1,2\}\setminus\{i\}
. Since
P_{i}\prec_{P}M_{v}
, and since
M_{\Gamma}\subset M^{\prime}_{v}\cap P
and
P_{i}^{\prime}\cap P=P_{j}
, by Proposition
2.4
(1) we get that
M_{\Gamma}\prec_{P}P_{j}
. By Lemma
2.8
(1), we find
u\in\mathcal{U}(P)
and
t>0
such that after identifying
P=P_{i}^{t}\overline{\otimes}P_{j}^{1/t}
, we have
uM_{\Gamma}u^{*}\subset P_{j}^{1/t}
. This shows that
M_{\Gamma}
is strongly prime. ∎

As an immediate consequence of Theorem
5.2
we can now derive Theorem
I
.

Proof of Theorem
I
.

Since
\Gamma
has an empty maximal clique factor, we can decompose it as
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}\circ\cdots\circ\Gamma_{n}
, where
\Gamma_{i}
is an irreducible graph with

|\Gamma_{i}|\geq 2
, for every
1\leq i\leq n
.
Since
\Gamma_{i}
is an irreducible graph, Theorem
5.2
implies that
M_{\Gamma_{i}}
is a strongly prime II
1
factor, for every
1\leq i\leq n
.
Since
M_{\Gamma}=M_{\Gamma_{1}}\overline{\otimes}\cdots\overline{\otimes}M_{\Gamma_{n}}
, this fact gives the conclusion of Theorem
I
via standard arguments, see, e.g.,
[
Is20
, Section 4]
.

For instance, to justify (1), assume that
M_{\Gamma}=P_{1}\overline{\otimes}P_{2}
, where
P_{1},P_{2}
are II
1
factors. If
1\leq i\leq n
, then since
M_{\Gamma_{i}}
is strongly prime and
M_{\Gamma}=M_{\Gamma_{i}}\overline{\otimes}M_{\Gamma\setminus\Gamma_{i}}
,
[
Is20
, Lemma 4.1]
provides
j_{i}\in\{1,2\}
such that
M_{\Gamma_{i}}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}P_{j_{i}}
. For
j\in\{1,2\}
, put
I_{j}=\{1\leq i\leq n\mid j_{i}=j\}
. Then by using the last fact and applying
[
Is20
, Lemma 4.3]
repeatedly we get that
\overline{\otimes}_{i\in I_{j}}M_{\Gamma_{i}}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}P_{j}
, for every
j\in\{1,2\}
. Since
I_{1}\cup I_{2}=\{1,\dots,n\}
by taking relative commutants via Proposition
2.4
(1) we also get that
P_{j}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}\overline{\otimes}_{i\in I_{j}}M_{\Gamma_{i}}
, for every
j\in\{1,2\}
. Using Lemma
2.8
(2), assertion (1) follows readily.
∎

We end this section by recording a result concerning strongly prime II
1
factors which will be needed in later sections.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
M_{0},N_{0}
be II
1
factors and
M_{1},\dots,M_{m},N_{1},\dots,N_{n}
be strongly prime II
1
factors such that
P=\overline{\otimes}_{i=0}^{m}M_{i}=\overline{\otimes}_{j=0}^{n}N_{j}
. Suppose that
M_{i}\nprec_{P}N_{0}
, for every
1\leq i\leq m
, and
N_{j}\nprec_{P}M_{0}
, for every
1\leq j\leq n
. Then there exist a decomposition
P=N_{0}^{t}\overline{\otimes}(\overline{\otimes}_{j=1}^{n}N_{i})^{1/t}
, for some
t>0
, and a unitary
u\in P
such that
uM_{0}u^{*}=N_{0}^{t}
.

Proof.

Put
Q=\overline{\otimes}_{i=1}^{m}M_{i}
and
R=\overline{\otimes}_{j=1}^{n}N_{j}
.
For
1\leq i\leq m
, since
M_{i}\nprec_{P}N_{0}
and
M_{i}
is strongly prime, we deduce that
M_{i}\prec_{P}R
. Since this holds for every
1\leq i\leq m
,
[
Is20
, Lemma 4.3]
implies that
Q\prec_{P}R
. Similarly, we get that
R\prec_{P}Q
. Lemma
2.8
gives a decomposition
P=N_{0}^{t}\overline{\otimes}R^{1/t}
, for some
t>0
, and a unitary
u\in P
such that
uQu^{*}=R^{1/t}
. By taking relative commutants, we get that
uM_{0}u^{*}=N_{0}^{t}
.
∎

6.
Classification/Rigidity theorems: Strategy and tools

Notation 6.1
.

We start by introducing the following setting which we keep throughout the section:

(1)

\Gamma
and
\Lambda
are nonempty finite simple graphs.

(2)

(M_{v},\tau_{v})_{v\in\Gamma}
and
(N_{w},\tau_{w})_{w\in\Lambda}
are families of diffuse tracial von Neumann algebras.

(3)

M_{\Gamma}=*_{v,\Gamma}M_{v}
and
N_{\Lambda}=*_{w,\Lambda}N_{w}
are the associated graph product von Neumann algebras.

(4)

Assume that
M_{\Gamma}
and
N_{\Lambda}
are II
1
factors.

(5)

Let
\theta:M_{\Gamma}\rightarrow pPp
be a
*
-isomorphism, where
P=\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes N_{\Lambda}
for some
n\geq 1
, and
p\in P
is a projection. We identify
M_{\Gamma}=pPp
, via
\theta
, and
N_{\Lambda}=qPq
, where
q=e_{1,1}\otimes 1\in P
.

Remark 6.2
.

In view of Lemma
2.16
(1), condition (4) is automatically satisfied if neither
\Gamma
nor
\Lambda
is contained in the star of a single vertex. This is satisfied in particular if
\Gamma
and
\Lambda
are transvection-free, which is the case in all statements from the introduction.

We also introduce the following notation.

Notation 6.3
.

For a finite simple graph
\Omega
, we denote by
\mathscr{J}(\Omega)
the set of maximal join full subgraphs
\Sigma\subset\Omega
, and by
\mathscr{C}(\Omega)
the set of complete subgraphs
C\subset\Omega
.

6.1.
Locating von Neumann subalgebras of maximal join subgraphs

In preparation for the proofs of Theorems
A
to
C
, we establish that any
*
-isomorphism
\theta:M_{\Gamma}\rightarrow p(\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes N_{\Lambda})p
must identify (up to “piecewise”
\prec^{s}
) subalgebras corresponding to maximal join full subgraphs of
\Gamma
with subalgebras corresponding to maximal join full subgraphs of
\Lambda
.

Proposition 6.4
.

In the setting from Notation
6.1
, let
p_{\Sigma}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{\Sigma})
and
q_{\Delta}\in\mathcal{Z}(N_{\Delta})
be the maximal projections such that
M_{\Sigma}p_{\Sigma}
and
N_{\Delta}q_{\Delta}
have no amenable direct summands, for every
\Sigma\in\mathscr{J}(\Gamma)
and
\Delta\in\mathscr{J}(\Lambda)
.
Assume that
M_{\Sigma}p_{\Sigma}\nprec_{P}N_{w}
and
N_{\Delta}q_{\Delta}\nprec_{P}M_{v}
, for every
\Sigma\in\mathscr{J}(\Gamma)
and
\Delta\in\mathscr{J}(\Lambda)
with
p_{\Sigma}\not=0
and
q_{\Delta}\not=0
, and every isolated vertices
v\in\Gamma
and
w\in\Lambda
.

Then we can find projections
p_{\Sigma,\Delta}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{\Sigma}^{\prime}\cap pPp)
and
q_{\Delta,\Sigma}\in\mathcal{Z}(N_{\Delta}^{\prime}\cap qPq)
, for every
\Sigma\in\mathscr{J}(\Gamma)
and
\Delta\in\mathscr{J}(\Lambda)
, such that the following conditions hold:

(1)

\sum_{\Delta\in\mathscr{J}(\Lambda)}p_{\Sigma,\Delta}=p_{\Sigma}
, for every
\Sigma\in\mathscr{J}(\Gamma)
, and
\sum_{\Sigma\in\mathscr{J}(\Gamma)}q_{\Delta,\Sigma}=q_{\Delta}
, for every
\Delta\in\mathscr{J}(\Lambda)
;

(2)

M_{\Sigma}p_{\Sigma,\Delta}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\Delta}q_{\Delta,\Sigma}
and
N_{\Delta}q_{\Delta,\Sigma}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\Sigma}p_{\Sigma,\Delta}
, for every
\Sigma\in\mathscr{J}(\Gamma)
and
\Delta\in\mathscr{J}(\Lambda)
.

Remark 6.5
.

We make a few remarks on the statement.

(1)

The existence of the maximal projections
p_{\Sigma}
and
q_{\Delta}
is proven as in Remark
3.8
.

(2)

Condition (2) in particular implies that
p_{\Sigma,\Delta}=0
if and only if
q_{\Delta,\Sigma}=0
.

(3)

Under the extra assumption that no connected component of either
\Gamma
or
\Lambda
is a complete subgraph, the assumptions from the proposition are automatically satisfied with
p_{\Sigma}=p
and
q_{\Delta}=q
. Indeed in this case, a subgraph
\Sigma\in\mathscr{J}(\Gamma)
cannot be complete, so Lemma
2.16
(3) ensures that
M_{\Sigma}
has no amenable direct summand (and likewise for
\Lambda
).

(4)

In the particular case where all vertex algebras are II
1
factors, Lemma
2.16
(1) implies that
\mathcal{Z}(M_{\Sigma})=\mathbb{C}p
and
\mathcal{Z}(M^{\prime}_{\Sigma}\cap pPp)=\mathbb{C}p
. So for every
\Sigma\in\mathscr{J}(\Gamma)
, we have
p_{\Sigma}=p
, and there is a unique
\Delta
such that
p_{\Sigma,\Delta}
is non-zero (so
p_{\Sigma,\Delta}=p
). Likewise for every
\Delta\in\mathscr{J}(\Lambda)
, we have
q_{\Delta}=q
, and there is a unique
\Sigma
such that
q_{\Delta,\Sigma}
is non-zero (so
q_{\Delta,\Sigma}=q
).

Proof.

For
\Sigma\in\mathscr{J}(\Gamma)
and
\Delta\in\mathscr{J}(\Lambda)
, let
p_{\Sigma,\Delta}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{\Sigma}^{\prime}\cap pPp)p_{\Sigma}
and
q_{\Delta,\Sigma}\in\mathcal{Z}(N_{\Delta}^{\prime}\cap qPq)q_{\Delta}
be the maximal projections with
M_{\Sigma}p_{\Sigma,\Delta}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\Delta}
and
N_{\Delta}q_{\Delta,\Sigma}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\Sigma}
, respectively
(their existence is proved as in Remark
3.8
).
We claim that

(6.1)

\text{$\bigvee_{\Delta\in\mathscr{J}(\Lambda)}p_{\Sigma,\Delta}=p_{\Sigma}$, for every $\Sigma\in\mathscr{J}(\Gamma)$}.

Fix
\Sigma\in\mathscr{J}(\Gamma)
and let
p_{0}=p_{\Sigma}-\bigvee_{\Delta\in\mathscr{J}(\Lambda)}p_{\Sigma,\Delta}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{\Sigma}^{\prime}\cap pPp)p_{\Sigma}
. Assume by contradiction that
p_{0}\not=0
.
Write
\Sigma=\Sigma_{1}\circ\Sigma_{2}
, where
\Sigma_{1},\Sigma_{2}\subset\Sigma
are nonempty full subgraphs.
Since
M_{\Sigma}=M_{\Sigma_{1}}\overline{\otimes}M_{\Sigma_{2}}
,
M_{\Sigma}p_{0}

has no amenable direct summand and
M_{\Sigma}p_{0}\nprec_{P}N_{w}
, for every isolated vertex
w\in\Lambda
,
Corollary
4.5
implies that
M_{\Sigma}p_{0}\prec_{P}N_{\Delta}
for some join full subgraph
\Delta\subset\Lambda
. After possibly replacing
\Delta
with a larger subgraph, we may assume that
\Delta\in\mathscr{J}(\Lambda)
.
By Proposition
2.4
(3) we can find a nonzero projection
p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{\Sigma}^{\prime}\cap M_{\Gamma})p_{0}
such that
M_{\Sigma}p^{\prime}\prec^{s}_{P}N_{\Delta}
. Since
p^{\prime}\leq p_{0}\leq p_{\Sigma}-p_{\Sigma,\Delta}
, this contradicts the definition of
p_{\Sigma,\Delta}
and proves (
6.1
). Similarly, we get

(6.2)

\text{$\bigvee_{\Sigma\in\mathscr{J}(\Gamma)}q_{\Delta,\Sigma}=q_{\Delta}$, for every $\Delta\in\mathscr{J}(\Lambda)$}.

Next, let
\Sigma\in\mathscr{J}(\Gamma)
and
\Delta\in\mathscr{J}(\Lambda)
with
p_{\Sigma,\Delta}\not=0
. Assume that
M_{\Sigma}p_{\Sigma,\Delta}\not\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\Delta}q_{\Delta,\Sigma}
. Since
M_{\Sigma}p_{\Sigma,\Delta}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\Delta}
, it follows that

M_{\Sigma}p_{\Sigma,\Delta}\prec_{P}N_{\Delta}(q-q_{\Delta,\Sigma})
, where we recall from Notation
6.1
that
q
is the unit of
N_{\Lambda}
. Since
M_{\Sigma}p_{\Sigma,\Delta}
has no amenable direct summand, while
N_{\Delta}(q-q_{\Delta})
is amenable, we get that
M_{\Sigma}p_{\Sigma,\Delta}\nprec_{P}N_{\Delta}(q-q_{\Delta})
. Combining the last two facts gives that
M_{\Sigma}p_{\Sigma,\Delta}\prec_{P}N_{\Delta}(q_{\Delta}-q_{\Delta,\Sigma})
. Since
q_{\Delta}-q_{\Delta,\Sigma}\leq\bigvee_{\Sigma^{\prime}\in\mathscr{J}(\Gamma)\setminus\{\Sigma\}}q_{\Delta,\Sigma^{\prime}}
by (
6.2
), it follows that
M_{\Sigma}p_{\Sigma,\Delta}\prec_{P}N_{\Delta}q_{\Delta,\Sigma^{\prime}}
, for some
\Sigma^{\prime}\in\mathscr{J}(\Gamma)\setminus\{\Sigma\}
. By combining this fact with
N_{\Delta}q_{\Delta,\Sigma^{\prime}}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\Sigma^{\prime}}
and
Proposition
2.4
(2), we get that
M_{\Sigma}p_{\Sigma,\Delta}\prec_{P}M_{\Sigma^{\prime}}
. By Lemma
2.16
(2) we further get that
\Sigma\subset\Sigma^{\prime}
.
Since
\Sigma,\Sigma^{\prime}\in\mathscr{J}(\Gamma)
, this gives
\Sigma=\Sigma^{\prime}
, a contradiction.

This proves that
M_{\Sigma}p_{\Sigma,\Delta}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\Delta}q_{\Delta,\Sigma}
. Similarly,
N_{\Delta}q_{\Delta,\Sigma}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\Sigma}p_{\Sigma,\Delta}
, which proves (2).

Finally, by (
6.1
) and symmetry, in order to prove (1), it suffices to show that
p_{\Sigma,\Delta}p_{\Sigma,\Delta^{\prime}}=0
, for every
\Sigma\in\mathscr{J}(\Gamma)
and
\Delta,\Delta^{\prime}\in\mathscr{J}(\Lambda)
with
\Delta\not=\Delta^{\prime}
. Assume by contradiction that
p^{\prime\prime}=p_{\Sigma,\Delta}p_{\Sigma,\Delta^{\prime}}\not=0
.
Then
M_{\Sigma}p^{\prime\prime}\prec^{s}_{P}N_{\Delta}
and
M_{\Sigma}p^{\prime\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\Delta^{\prime}}
. By Corollary
3.5
we get that
M_{\Sigma}p^{\prime\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\Delta\cap\Delta^{\prime}}
.
On the other hand, Lemma
6.6
below gives that

N_{\Delta}q_{\Delta,\Sigma}\prec_{P}M_{\Sigma}p^{\prime\prime}
and
N_{\Delta^{\prime}}q_{\Delta^{\prime},\Sigma}\prec_{P}M_{\Sigma}p^{\prime\prime}
. By using Proposition
2.4
(2) we derive that
N_{\Delta}q_{\Delta,\Sigma}\prec_{P}N_{\Delta\cap\Delta^{\prime}}
and
N_{\Delta^{\prime}}q_{\Delta^{\prime},\Sigma}\prec_{P}N_{\Delta\cap\Delta^{\prime}}
, so in particular
N_{\Delta}\prec_{P}N_{\Delta\cap\Delta^{\prime}}
and
N_{\Delta^{\prime}}\prec_{P}N_{\Delta\cap\Delta^{\prime}}
. By Lemma
2.16
(2) we deduce that
\Delta\subset\Delta\cap\Delta^{\prime}
and
\Delta^{\prime}\subset\Delta\cap\Delta^{\prime}
, which contradicts that
\Delta\not=\Delta^{\prime}
and thus proves (1).
∎

Lemma 6.6
.

In the setting from Notation
6.1
, assume that
\Sigma\subset\Gamma,\Delta\subset\Lambda
are full subgraphs and
p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{\Sigma}^{\prime}\cap pPp),q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}(N_{\Delta}^{\prime}\cap qPq)
are nonzero projections such that
M_{\Sigma}p^{\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\Delta}q^{\prime}
and
N_{\Delta}q^{\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\Sigma}p^{\prime}
.

Then
M_{\Sigma}p^{\prime}\prec_{P}N_{\Delta}q^{\prime\prime}
, for every nonzero projection
q^{\prime\prime}\in(N_{\Delta}^{\prime}\cap qPq)q^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Assume by contradiction that there is a nonzero projection
q^{\prime\prime}\in(N_{\Delta}^{\prime}\cap qPq)q^{\prime}
such that
M_{\Sigma}p^{\prime}\nprec_{P}N_{\Delta}q^{\prime\prime}
. Let
q_{0}\in\mathcal{Z}(N_{\Delta}^{\prime}\cap qPq)q^{\prime}
be the central support of
q^{\prime\prime}
in
N_{\Delta}^{\prime}\cap qPq
.

We claim that
M_{\Sigma}p^{\prime}\nprec_{P}N_{\Delta}q_{0}
. Indeed, assume towards a contradiction that
M_{\Sigma}p^{\prime}\prec_{P}N_{\Delta}q_{0}
. By
[
DHI19
, Lemma 2.4(4)]
, we can find a non-zero projection
z\in\mathcal{Z}(N_{\Delta}^{\prime}\cap qPq)q_{0}
such that
M_{\Sigma}p^{\prime}\prec_{P}N_{\Delta}r
for every non-zero projection
r\in(N_{\Delta}^{\prime}\cap qPq)z
. Since
q_{0}=\bigvee_{u\in\mathcal{U}(N_{\Delta}^{\prime}\cap qPq)}uq^{\prime\prime}u^{*}
, we can find
u\in\mathcal{U}(N_{\Delta}^{\prime}\cap qPq)
such that
z(uq^{\prime\prime}u^{*})\neq 0
. This implies the existence of non-zero equivalent projections
z_{1},q_{1}\in N_{\Delta}^{\prime}\cap qPq
such that
z_{1}\leq z
and
q_{1}\leq uq^{\prime\prime}u^{*}
. By definition of
z
, we have
M_{\Sigma}p^{\prime}\prec_{P}N_{\Delta}z_{1}
, hence
M_{\Sigma}p^{\prime}\prec_{P}N_{\Delta}q_{1}
. Since
N_{\Delta}q_{1}\subset u(N_{\Delta}q^{\prime\prime})u^{*}
, it follows that
M_{\Sigma}p^{\prime}\prec_{P}N_{\Delta}q^{\prime\prime}
, a contradiction. This proves our claim.

Since
M_{\Sigma}p^{\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\Delta}q^{\prime}
, we get that
M_{\Sigma}p^{\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\Delta}(q^{\prime}-q_{0})
. Since
q_{0}\leq q^{\prime}
, we also have
N_{\Delta}q_{0}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\Sigma}p^{\prime}
. Using Proposition
2.4
(2), we derive that
N_{\Delta}q_{0}\prec_{P}N_{\Delta}(q^{\prime}-q_{0})
.
Notice also that
N_{\Delta}\not\prec_{P}N_{\Delta^{\prime}}
for any proper full subgraph
\Delta^{\prime}\subsetneq\Delta
, in view of Lemma
2.16
(2). Since
q^{\prime},q_{0}\in\mathcal{Z}(N_{\Delta}^{\prime}\cap qPq)
, this contradicts Corollary
3.13
and finishes the proof.
∎

6.2.
A combinatorial lemma.

In preparation for the next subsection, we now establish a combinatorial lemma.
Given two vertices
v,v^{\prime}\in\Gamma
, following
[
CV09
]
, we write
v\leq v^{\prime}
if
\mathrm{lk}(v)\subset\mathrm{st}(v^{\prime})
. This is a partial order on the vertex set of
\Gamma
. We let
\sim
be the associated equivalence relation (i.e.,
v\sim v^{\prime}
if
v\leq v^{\prime}
and
v^{\prime}\leq v
), and we denote by
[v]
the
\sim
-class of a vertex
v
. It turns out that every equivalence class of vertices is either a collapsible clique, or an edgeless collapsible subgraph
[
CV09
, Lemma 2.3]
. We let
\mathcal{E}(\Gamma)
be the graph whose vertices are
\sim
-equivalence classes of vertices of
\Gamma
, where two distinct classes
[v]
and
[w]
are joined by an edge if
v
and
w
are adjacent (this does not depend on the choice of representatives in the class). We denote by
\mathcal{P}(\Gamma)
(resp.
\mathcal{P}(\Lambda)
) the set of all nonempty subsets of vertices of
\Gamma
(resp.
\Lambda
).

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
\Gamma,\Lambda
be clique-reduced finite simple graphs. Let
\alpha:\Gamma\to\mathcal{P}(\Lambda)
and
\beta:\Lambda\to\mathcal{P}(\Gamma)
be maps such that

(1)

if two distinct vertices
v,v^{\prime}\in\Gamma
are adjacent, then
\alpha(v^{\prime})\subset\alpha(v)^{\perp}
;

(2)

if two distinct vertices
w,w^{\prime}\in\Lambda
are adjacent, then
\beta(w^{\prime})\subset\beta(w)^{\perp}
;

(3)

for any vertices
v\in\Gamma
and
w\in\Lambda
, we have
w\in\alpha(v)
if and only if
v\in\beta(w)
.

Then for every
v\in\Gamma
, the set
\alpha([v])
is a
\sim
-class of vertices of
\Lambda
, and for every
w\in\Lambda
, the set
\beta([w])
is a
\sim
-class of vertices of
\Gamma
. In addition the induced maps
\bar{\alpha}:\mathcal{E}(\Gamma)\to\mathcal{E}(\Lambda)
and
\bar{\beta}:\mathcal{E}(\Lambda)\to\mathcal{E}(\Gamma)
are graph isomorphisms, which are inverses of each other.

Proof.

Let
v\in\Gamma
. We first prove that
\alpha([v])
is contained in a single
\sim
-class. So assume towards a contradiction that
\alpha([v])
contains two inequivalent vertices
w_{1},w_{2}
. Then up to swapping
w_{1}
and
w_{2}
, we can find
w^{\prime}\in\Lambda
that is adjacent to
w_{1}
but not to
w_{2}
. It follows from (2) that
\beta(w^{\prime})\subset\beta(w_{1})^{\perp}
. Let
v_{1},v_{2}\in[v]
be such that
w_{1}\in\alpha(v_{1})
and
w_{2}\in\alpha(v_{2})
. By (3) we have
v_{1}\in\beta(w_{1})
, so
\beta(w^{\prime})\subset\mathrm{lk}(v_{1})
. Let
v^{\prime}\in\beta(w^{\prime})
. Then
v^{\prime}\in\mathrm{lk}(v_{1})
. Since
\Gamma
is clique-reduced, the equivalence class
[v]
corresponds to an edgeless subgraph, so
v^{\prime}\in\mathrm{lk}(v_{2})
. Then (1) implies that
\alpha(v^{\prime})\subset\alpha(v_{2})^{\perp}
, in particular
w^{\prime}\in\mathrm{lk}(w_{2})
, a contradiction.

By symmetry, for every
w\in\Lambda
, the set
\beta([w])
is contained in a single
\sim
-class.

We now prove that
\alpha([v])
is equal to a
\sim
-class. So let
w_{1}\in\alpha([v])
, and let
w_{2}\in\Lambda
with
w_{1}\sim w_{2}
. We aim to prove that
w_{2}\in\alpha([v])
. Let
v_{1}\in[v]
be such that
w_{1}\in\alpha(v_{1})
, then (3) implies that
v_{1}\in\beta([w_{1}])
. Since by the above
\beta([w_{1}])
is contained in a single
\sim
-class, it follows that
\beta(w_{2})\subset[v]
. Let
v_{2}\in\beta(w_{2})
, then by (3) we have
w_{2}\in\alpha(v_{2})
, so
w_{2}\in\alpha([v])
, as desired.

By symmetry, for every
w\in\Lambda
, the set
\beta([w])
is a single
\sim
-class. The induced maps
\bar{\alpha}:\mathcal{E}(\Gamma)\to\mathcal{E}(\Lambda)
and
\bar{\beta}:\mathcal{E}(\Lambda)\to\mathcal{E}(\Gamma)
preserve adjacency (by (1) and (2)) and are inverse to each other (by (3)), so the lemma follows.
∎

6.3.
A conjugacy criterion

We next prove the following conjugacy criterion which will guarantee that the conclusions of Theorems
A
,
B
and
C
hold. In order to cover more cases, we state a general version that does not require the graphs
\Gamma
and
\Lambda
to be transvection-free, see Remark
6.9
below. We insist that in the statement below, even if the vertices
v,w
belong to the untransvectable subgraphs
\Gamma^{u},\Lambda^{u}
, their stars
\mathrm{st}(v)
and
\mathrm{st}(w)
are always considered in the ambient graphs
\Gamma,\Lambda
.

Proposition 6.8
.

In the setting from Notation
6.1
, assume that for every
v\in\Gamma^{u}
and
w\in\Lambda^{u}
, there exist
projections
p_{v,w}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{\emph{st}(v)}^{\prime}\cap pPp)
and
q_{w,v}\in\mathcal{Z}(N_{\emph{st}(w)}^{\prime}\cap qPq)
, such that the following conditions hold:

(a)

\sum_{w\in\Lambda^{u}}p_{v,w}=p
, for every
v\in\Gamma^{u}
, and
\sum_{v\in\Gamma^{u}}q_{w,v}=q
, for every
w\in\Lambda^{u}
.

(b)

M_{v}p_{v,w}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{w}q_{w,v}
and
N_{w}q_{w,v}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{v}p_{v,w}
, for every
v\in\Gamma^{u}
and
w\in\Lambda^{u}
.

Then the following hold.

(1)

If
\Gamma^{u}
and
\Lambda^{u}
are clique-reduced, then there exists a graph isomorphism
\bar{\alpha}:\mathcal{E}(\Gamma^{u})\rightarrow\mathcal{E}(\Lambda^{u})
such that for every
v\in\Gamma^{u}
and
w\in\Lambda^{u}
, we have
M_{v}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\bar{\alpha}([v])}
and
N_{w}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\bar{\alpha}^{-1}([w])}
(where
[v]
denotes the
\sim
-equivalence class of
v
in
\Gamma^{u}
, and
[w]
denotes the
\sim
-equivalence class of
w
in
\Lambda^{u}
).

(2)

If all vertex algebras are II
1
factors, then there exists a graph isomorphism
\alpha:\Gamma^{u}\rightarrow\Lambda^{u}
such that for every
v\in\Gamma^{u}
and
w\in\Lambda^{u}
, we have
M_{v}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\alpha(v)}
and
N_{w}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\alpha^{-1}(w)}
.

Remark 6.9
.

The first statement applies in particular when
\Gamma
and
\Lambda
are transvection-free, with no additional assumptions on the vertex algebras other than they are diffuse tracial von Neumann algebras. In this case
\Gamma^{u}=\Gamma
and
\Lambda^{u}=\Lambda
are clique-reduced, and the conclusion is that there is a graph isomorphism
\alpha:\Gamma\to\Lambda
such that for every
v\in\Gamma
, we have
M_{v}\prec^{s}_{P}N_{\alpha(v)}
and
N_{\alpha(v)}\prec^{s}_{P}M_{v}
.

Before proving Proposition
6.8
, let us start with a lemma that will be useful in the proof.

Lemma 6.10
.

In the setting from Notation
6.1
, let
v\in\Gamma
be an untransvectable vertex. Then

\mathcal{Z}(M^{\prime}_{\mathrm{st}(v)}\cap pPp)=\mathcal{Z}(M^{\prime}_{v}\cap pPp)=\mathcal{Z}(M_{v}).

Proof.

Since
\text{st}(v)^{\perp}=\emptyset
, Lemma
2.16
(1) implies that

\mathcal{Z}(M_{\text{st}(v)}^{\prime}\cap pPp)=\mathcal{Z}(M_{v}^{\prime}\cap pPp)=\mathcal{Z}(M_{v})\overline{\otimes}\mathcal{Z}(M_{C}),

where
C
is the maximal clique factor of
\text{lk}(v)
. If
v^{\prime}\in C
, then
\text{st}(v)\subset\text{st}(v^{\prime})
which contradicts the fact that
v\in\Gamma
is untransvectable since
v^{\prime}\not=v
. Thus,
C=\emptyset
, which implies the lemma.
∎

Proof of Proposition
6.8
.

We first note that by combining (b) with Proposition
3.10
it follows that

(6.3)

M_{\text{st}(v)}p_{v,w}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\text{st}(w)}q_{w,v}
and
N_{\text{st}(w)}q_{w,v}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\text{st}(v)}p_{v,w}
, for every
v\in\Gamma^{u}
and
w\in\Lambda^{u}
.

We denote
E_{v}=\{w\in\Lambda^{u}\mid p_{v,w}\not=0\}
, for every
v\in\Gamma^{u}
, and
F_{w}=\{v\in\Gamma^{u}\mid q_{w,v}\not=0\}
, for every
w\in\Lambda^{u}
. Condition (b) guarantees that
w\in E_{v}
if and only if
v\in F_{w}
, for every
v\in\Gamma^{u}
and
w\in\Lambda^{u}
.

Next, we prove the following claim.

Claim 6.11
.

If
v,v^{\prime}\in\Gamma^{u}
are adjacent, then
E_{v^{\prime}}\subset E_{v}^{\perp}
.

Proof.

Let
w\in E_{v}
.
We first show that

(6.4)

M_{v^{\prime}}\prec^{s}_{P}N_{\mathrm{st}(w)}q_{w,v}.

To this end, note that

M_{\text{st}(v)}p_{v,w}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\text{st}(w)}q_{w,v}
, thus

(6.5)

M_{v^{\prime}}p_{v,w}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\text{st}(w)}q_{w,v}.

By Proposition
2.4
(3) we find a projection
z\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{v^{\prime}}^{\prime}\cap pPp)
with
p_{v,w}\leq z
and
M_{v^{\prime}}z\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\text{st}(w)}q_{w,v}
. By Lemma
6.10
, we have
\mathcal{Z}(M_{v^{\prime}}^{\prime}\cap pPp)=\mathcal{Z}(M_{v^{\prime}})
, so

z\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{v^{\prime}})
. Lemma
6.10
also gives
\mathcal{Z}(M_{\text{st}(v)}^{\prime}\cap pPp)=\mathcal{Z}(M_{v})
, so

p_{v,w}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{v})
. Moreover, if
x\in M_{v}
, then
\text{E}_{M_{v^{\prime}}}(x)=\tau(x)p
, where we consider the trace
\tau
on
M_{\Gamma}=pPp
normalized so that
\tau(p)=1
.
Using these facts and applying
\text{E}_{\mathcal{Z}(M_{v^{\prime}})}
to the inequality
p_{v,w}\leq z
gives

\tau(p_{v,w})p=\text{E}_{\mathcal{Z}(M_{v^{\prime}})}(p_{v,w})\leq\text{E}_{\mathcal{Z}(M_{v^{\prime}})}(z)=z.

Since
z\leq p
and
p_{v,w}\not=0
, this forces
z=p
. Therefore,
M_{v^{\prime}}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\text{st}(w)}q_{w,v}
, which proves (
6.4
).

We next prove that

(6.6)

E_{v^{\prime}}\subset\text{st}(w).

If
w^{\prime}\in E_{v^{\prime}}
, then
N_{w^{\prime}}\prec_{P}M_{v^{\prime}}
. Combining this fact with (
6.4
) and using Proposition
2.4
(2), we get
N_{w^{\prime}}\prec_{P}N_{\text{st}(w)}q_{w,v}
, and hence
N_{w^{\prime}}\prec_{P}N_{\mathrm{st}(w)}
. By Lemma
2.16
(2) we get that
w^{\prime}\in\text{st}(w)
. This altogether proves (
6.6
).

Finally, we show that

(6.7)

w\not\in E_{v^{\prime}}.

Assume by contradiction that
w\in E_{v^{\prime}}
. Then we have
p_{v^{\prime},w}\not=0
and
M_{v^{\prime}}p_{v^{\prime},w}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{w}
. Since
M_{v^{\prime}}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\text{st}(w)}q_{w,v}
by equation (
6.4
), we also have
M_{v^{\prime}}p_{v^{\prime},w}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\text{st}(w)}q_{w,v}
. By combining these facts, Corollary
3.11
implies that
M_{v^{\prime}}p_{v^{\prime},w}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{w}q_{w,v}
. Since
N_{w}q_{w,v}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{v}p_{v,w}
, applying Proposition
2.4
(2) gives
M_{v^{\prime}}p_{v^{\prime},w}\prec_{P}M_{v}p_{v,w}
. However, Lemma
2.16
(2) implies that
v^{\prime}=v
, which gives a contradiction. This proves (
6.7
).

By combining (
6.6
) and (
6.7
), we conclude that
E_{v^{\prime}}\subset\text{lk}(w)
. Since this holds for every
w\in E_{v}
, we deduce that
E_{v^{\prime}}\subset\cap_{w\in E_{v}}\text{lk}(w)=E_{v}^{\perp}
, which finishes the proof of the claim. ∎

By symmetry, repeating the proof of Claim
6.11
implies that

(6.8)

F_{w^{\prime}}\subset F_{w}^{\perp}
, for every
w,w^{\prime}\in\Lambda^{u}
with
w^{\prime}\in\text{lk}(w)
.

Let
\alpha:\Gamma^{u}\to\mathcal{P}(\Lambda^{u})
and
\beta:\Lambda^{u}\to\mathcal{P}(\Gamma^{u})
be given by
\alpha(v)=E_{v}
and
\beta(w)=F_{w}
.

To prove (1), we now assume that
\Gamma^{u}
and
\Lambda^{u}
are clique-reduced. It follows from the above that
\alpha
and
\beta
satisfy the assumptions from Lemma
6.7
(applied to the graphs
\Gamma^{u}
and
\Lambda^{u}
). It follows from that lemma that
\alpha
induces an isomorphism
\bar{\alpha}:\mathcal{E}(\Gamma^{u})\to\mathcal{E}(\Lambda^{u})
. Let now
v\in\Gamma^{u}
. Then
E_{v}\subset\bar{\alpha}([v])
, where
[v]
denotes the
\sim
-equivalence class of
v
in
\Gamma^{u}
. For every
w\in\bar{\alpha}([v])
, we have
M_{v}p_{v,w}\prec^{s}_{P}N_{w}
, so
M_{v}p_{v,w}\prec^{s}_{P}N_{\bar{\alpha}([v])}
. Since
\sum_{w\in\bar{\alpha}([v])}p_{v,w}=p
, it follows that
M_{v}\prec^{s}_{P}N_{\bar{\alpha}([v])}
. The same argument shows that for every
w\in\Lambda^{u}
, one has
N_{w}\prec^{s}_{P}M_{\bar{\alpha}^{-1}([w])}
, which completes the proof of (1).

To prove (2), we now assume that all vertex algebras are II
1
factors. Then the sets
E_{v}
and
F_{w}
are singletons (see Remark
6.5
(4)) and it immediately follows that
\alpha:\Gamma^{u}\rightarrow\Lambda^{u}
and
\beta:\Lambda^{u}\rightarrow\Gamma^{u}
are graph isomorphisms, which are inverse of each other. The conclusion follows.
∎

7.
Arbitrary vertex algebras: Proof of Theorem
A

We are now in position to prove the following result which generalizes Theorem
A
.
The basic consequences of the double intertwining obtained in the conclusion will be clarified in Section
10
.

Theorem 7.1
.

In the setting from Notation
6.1
, assume additionally that
\Gamma
and
\Lambda
are transvection-free, square-free and not reduced to a vertex.

Then the graphs
\Gamma
and
\Lambda
are isomorphic, and there is a graph isomorphism
\alpha:\Gamma\rightarrow\Lambda
such that
M_{v}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\alpha(v)}
and
N_{\alpha(v)}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{v}
, for every
v\in\Gamma
.

In preparation for the proof of Theorem
7.1
, we prove the following lemma.
We recall first from Notation
6.3
that for a graph
\Omega
, we denote by
\mathscr{J}(\Omega)
the set of maximal join subgraphs of
\Omega
.

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
\Omega
be a finite simple graph which is transvection-free, square-free and not reduced to a vertex. Then
\emph{st}(v)\in\mathscr{J}(\Omega)
, the maximal clique factor of
\emph{st}(v)
is
\{v\}
, and
\emph{lk}(v)
is an irreducible graph, for every
v\in\Omega
.
Moreover, every graph
\Sigma\in\mathscr{J}(\Omega)
is equal to
\emph{st}(v)
, for some
v\in\Omega
.

Proof.

Let
v\in\Omega
. Since
v
is untransvectable, it is not isolated. Thus,
\text{st}(v)\not=\{v\}
and
\text{st}(v)
is a join graph. If
v^{\prime}
belongs to the maximal clique factor of
\text{st}(v)
, then

\text{st}(v)\subset\text{st}(v^{\prime})
, which forces
v^{\prime}=v
. Thus, the maximal clique factor of
\text{st}(v)
is equal to
\{v\}
.

Let
\Sigma\in\mathscr{J}(\Omega)
. Decompose
\Sigma=C\circ\Sigma_{1}\circ\cdots\circ\Sigma_{n}
, where
C
is the maximal clique factor of
\Sigma
and
\Sigma_{1},\dots,\Sigma_{n}
are irreducible graphs with at least two vertices. If
n\geq 2
, then since
\Sigma_{i}
is not a clique, we can find vertices
v_{i},v_{i}^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{i}
which are not adjacent, for every
i\in\{1,2\}
. Then the vertices
v_{1},v_{2},v_{1}^{\prime},v_{2}^{\prime}
would form a square, which is a contradiction. This implies that
n\leq 1
. Hence,
C\not=\emptyset
, since
\Sigma
is a join graph.
If
w\in C
, then
\Sigma\subset\text{st}(w)
and the maximality of
\Sigma
implies that
\Sigma=\text{st}(w)
.

Finally, let
v\in\Omega
. Since
\text{st}(v)\subset\Omega
is a join full subgraph, we can find
\Sigma\in\mathscr{J}(\Omega)
containing
\text{st}(v)
. By the previous paragraph, we have that
\Sigma=\text{st}(w)
, for some
w\in\Omega
. Since
\text{st}(v)\subset\text{st}(w)
, we get that
w=v
, thus
\text{st}(v)=\Sigma
is a maximal join full subgraph.
Since
\{v\}
is the maximal clique factor of
\text{st}(v)
, the previous paragraph implies that
\text{lk}(v)
is an irreducible graph.
This finishes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
7.1
.

By Lemma
7.2
,
\mathscr{J}(\Gamma)=\{\text{st}(v)\mid v\in\Gamma\}
and
\mathscr{J}(\Lambda)=\{\text{st}(w)\mid w\in\Lambda\}
.
If
v\in\Gamma
and
w\in\Lambda
, then
\text{st}(v)
and
\text{st}(w)
are not cliques by Lemma
7.2
, thus
M_{\text{st}(v)}
and
N_{\text{st}(w)}
have no amenable direct summands by Lemma
2.16
(3). Moreover, since
\Gamma
and
\Lambda
are transvection-free and are not reduced to a vertex, they have no isolated vertices.

By applying Proposition
6.4

(see also Remark
6.5
(3)) we
find projections
p_{v,w}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{\text{st}(v)}^{\prime}\cap pPp)
and
q_{w,v}\in\mathcal{Z}(N_{\text{st}(w)}^{\prime}\cap qPq)
, for every
v\in\Gamma
and
w\in\Lambda
, such that the following conditions hold:

(1)

\sum_{w\in\Lambda}p_{v,w}=p
, for every
v\in\Gamma
, and
\sum_{v\in\Gamma}q_{w,v}=q
, for every
w\in\Lambda
;

(2)

M_{\text{st}(v)}p_{v,w}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\text{st}(w)}q_{w,v}
and
N_{\text{st}(w)}q_{w,v}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\text{st}(v)}p_{v,w}
, for every
v\in\Gamma
and
w\in\Lambda
.

Our next goal is to show the following claim.

Claim 7.3
.

M_{v}p_{v,w}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{w}q_{w,v}
and
N_{w}q_{w,v}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{v}p_{v,w}
, for every
v\in\Gamma
and
w\in\Lambda
.

Proof.

Let
v\in\Gamma,w\in\Lambda
with
p_{v,w}\not=0
. By symmetry, it suffices to argue that
M_{v}p_{v,w}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{w}q_{w,v}
. Let
p_{0}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{v}^{\prime}\cap pPp)p_{v,w}
be a nonzero projection. Since
\Gamma
is transvection-free, Lemma
6.10
implies that
\mathcal{Z}(M_{\text{st}(v)}^{\prime}\cap pPp)=\mathcal{Z}(M_{v})=\mathcal{Z}(M_{v}^{\prime}\cap pPp)
. Thus,
p_{0}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{\text{st}(v)}^{\prime}\cap pPp)p_{v,w}
.

Corollary
3.13
and Lemma
2.16
(2) give that
M_{\text{st}(v)}z\nprec_{P}M_{\text{st}(v)}(p-z)
, for every nonzero projection
z\in M_{\text{st}(v)\cup\text{st}(v)^{\perp}}^{\prime}\cap pPp=M_{\text{st}(v)}^{\prime}\cap pPp
. By Lemma
7.2
, the maximal clique factor of
\text{st}(w)
is equal to
\{w\}
.
By using (2) and applying Corollary
4.6
to the decomposition
M_{\text{st}(v)}p_{v,w}=M_{v}p_{v,w}\vee M_{\text{lk}(v)}p_{v,w}
we find a nonzero projection
p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{\text{st}(v)}^{\prime}\cap pPp)p_{0}
and a decomposition
\text{lk}(w)=\Lambda_{1}\circ\Lambda_{2}
such that

(7.1)

M_{v}p^{\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\{w\}\cup\Lambda_{1}}q_{w,v}
and
M_{\text{lk}(v)}p^{\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\{w\}\cup\Lambda_{2}}q_{w,v}
.

However, since
\text{lk}(w)
is an irreducible graph according to Lemma
7.2
,
\Lambda_{1}
or
\Lambda_{2}
is empty.
We claim that
\Lambda_{1}=\emptyset
. Assuming by contradiction that
\Lambda_{1}\not=\emptyset
, we get that

\Lambda_{2}=\emptyset
and therefore
M_{\text{lk}(v)}p^{\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{w}q_{w,v}
by (
7.1
). Let
v^{\prime}\in\text{lk}(v)
. Then
M_{v^{\prime}}p^{\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{w}q_{w,v}
. Proposition
3.10
gives
M_{\text{st}(v^{\prime})}p^{\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\text{st}(w)}q_{w,v}
. Since (2) gives
N_{\text{st}(w)}q_{w,v}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\text{st}(v)}p_{v,w}
, Proposition
2.4
(2) implies that
M_{\text{st}(v^{\prime})}p^{\prime}\prec_{P}M_{\text{st}(v)}p_{v,w}
. By Lemma
2.16
(2) we get
\text{st}(v^{\prime})\subset\text{st}(v)
. Since
v^{\prime}\not=v
, this contradicts the fact that
\Gamma
is transvection-free, which proves our claim that
\Lambda_{1}=\emptyset
.

Since
\Lambda_{1}=\emptyset
, by Equation (
7.1
) we get
M_{v}p^{\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{w}q_{w,v}
. In conclusion, for every nonzero projection
p_{0}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{v}^{\prime}\cap pPp)p_{v,w}
, we found a nonzero projection
p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{v}^{\prime}\cap pPp)p_{0}
such that
M_{v}p^{\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{w}q_{w,v}
.
This implies that
M_{v}p_{v,w}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{w}q_{w,v}
, which finishes the proof of the claim. ∎

By using Claim
7.3
and applying Proposition
6.8
(1) (with
\Gamma=\Gamma^{u}
and
\Lambda=\Lambda^{u}
), the conclusion of Theorem
7.1
follows.
∎

8.
Amenable vertex algebras: Proof of Theorem
B

In this section we prove Theorem
B
, dealing with the case where all vertex algebras are amenable. The consequences of this result to the
W^{*}
-classification of right-angled Artin groups, and to the classification of graph products of hyperfinite II
1
factors, will be recorded in Section
12
.

8.1.
Proof of Theorem
B

Let
\Gamma
be a finite simple graph. Recall that the untransvectable subgraph
\Gamma^{u}
of
\Gamma
is the full subgraph of
\Gamma
spanned by its untransvectable vertices. We also recall the definition of the graph
\mathcal{E}(\Gamma^{u})
from Section
6.2
. We finally recall that
\Gamma
is clique-reduced if it does not contain any collapsible complete subgraph on at least
2
vertices.

The goal of the present section is to prove the following result which generalizes Theorem
B
.

Theorem 8.1
.

In the setting from Notation
6.1
, assume additionally that the graphs
\Gamma
and
\Lambda
are clique-reduced, and that

(M_{v},\tau_{v})
and
(N_{w},\tau_{w})
are amenable, for every
v\in\Gamma
and
w\in\Lambda
.

(1)

If
\Gamma^{u},\Lambda^{u}
are clique-reduced, then the graphs
\mathcal{E}(\Gamma^{u})
and
\mathcal{E}(\Lambda^{u})
are isomorphic, and there exists a graph isomorphism
\bar{\alpha}:\mathcal{E}(\Gamma^{u})\rightarrow\mathcal{E}(\Lambda^{u})
such that
M_{v}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\bar{\alpha}([v])}
and
N_{w}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\bar{\alpha}^{-1}([w])}
, for every
v\in\Gamma^{u}
and
w\in\Lambda^{u}
.

(2)

If
M_{v}
and
N_{w}
are II
1
factors, for every
v\in\Gamma
and
w\in\Lambda
, then the graphs
\Gamma^{u}
and
\Lambda^{u}
are isomorphic, and there exists a graph isomorphism
\alpha:\Gamma^{u}\to\Lambda^{u}
such that
M_{v}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\alpha(v)}
and
N_{\alpha(v)}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{v}
, for every
v\in\Gamma^{u}
.

Remark 8.2
.

We make a few comments on the statement.

(1)

If
\Gamma
is transvection-free, then
\Gamma^{u}=\Gamma
and
\Gamma
is clique-reduced,
and
\mathcal{E}(\Gamma)=\Gamma
. Therefore, if
\Gamma
and
\Lambda
are both transvection-free, then the first part of Theorem
8.1
provides an isomorphism between
\Gamma
and
\Lambda
which satisfies the conclusion of Theorem
B
.

(2)

Our assumption that
\Gamma
and
\Lambda
are clique-reduced is mild: if
\Gamma
contains a collapsible clique
C
, then we can collapse this clique to a single vertex, and get a new graph product structure for
M_{\Gamma}
where the new vertex algebra is
\overline{\otimes}_{v\in C}M_{v}
, and therefore still amenable.

(3)

The following question remains open: in the setting of Notation
6.1
, if
\Gamma
and
\Lambda
contain no collapsible subgraph on at least two vertices that is either complete or edgeless, and if all vertex algebras are amenable, must
\Gamma
and
\Lambda
be isomorphic? Theorem
8.1
(1) shows, on the other hand, that if we further assume the untransvectable subgraphs
\Gamma^{u},\Lambda^{u}
to have no collapsible subgraph on at least two vertices that is either complete or edgeless, then we have an isomorphism between
\Gamma^{u}
and
\Lambda^{u}
.

The proof of Theorem
8.1
relies on a few auxiliary results.

Proposition 8.3
.

In the setting from Notation
6.1
, assume additionally that
(M_{v},\tau_{v})
and
(N_{w},\tau_{w})
are amenable, for every
v\in\Gamma
and
w\in\Lambda
.
Let
\Sigma\in\mathscr{J}(\Gamma)
and
\Delta\in\mathscr{J}(\Lambda)
. Let
p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{\Sigma}^{\prime}\cap pPp)
and
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}(N_{\Delta}^{\prime}\cap qPq)
be nonzero projections such that
M_{\Sigma}p^{\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\Delta}q^{\prime}
and
N_{\Delta}q^{\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\Sigma}p^{\prime}
. Let
\Sigma_{0}
and
\Delta_{0}
be the maximal clique factors of
\Sigma
and
\Delta
, respectively.
Assume that
\Sigma_{0}\not=\emptyset
.

Then
\Delta_{0}\not=\emptyset
and we have
M_{\Sigma_{0}}p^{\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\Delta_{0}}q^{\prime}
and
N_{\Delta_{0}}q^{\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\Sigma_{0}}p^{\prime}
.

Proof.

If
\Sigma
is a clique, then
\Sigma_{0}=\Sigma
and
M_{\Sigma}
is amenable. Since
N_{\Delta}q^{\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\Sigma}p^{\prime}
, we deduce that
N_{\Delta}q^{\prime}
is amenable. Hence,
\Delta
is a clique by Lemma
2.16
(3). Thus,
\Delta_{0}=\Delta\not=\emptyset
and the conclusion holds.

We may therefore assume that
\Sigma
is not a clique.
Let
\Sigma_{1}=\Sigma\setminus\Sigma_{0}\not=\emptyset
.
Let
p_{0}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{\Sigma}^{\prime}\cap pPp)p^{\prime}
be a nonzero projection.
Corollary
3.13
and Lemma
2.16
(2) give that
M_{\Sigma}z\nprec_{P}M_{\Sigma}(p-z)
, for every nonzero projection
z\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{\Sigma}^{\prime}\cap pPp)
. Using that
M_{\Sigma}p^{\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\Delta}q^{\prime}
and
N_{\Delta}q^{\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\Sigma}p^{\prime}
, by applying Corollary
4.6
to the decomposition
M_{\Sigma}p^{\prime}=M_{\Sigma_{0}}p^{\prime}\vee M_{\Sigma_{1}}p^{\prime}
we find a nonzero projection
p^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{\Sigma}^{\prime}\cap pPp)p_{0}
and a join decomposition
\Delta\setminus\Delta_{0}=\Delta_{1}\circ\Delta_{2}
such that

(8.1)

M_{\Sigma_{0}}p^{\prime\prime}\prec^{s}_{P}N_{\Delta_{0}\cup\Delta_{1}}q^{\prime}
and
M_{\Sigma_{1}}p^{\prime\prime}\prec^{s}_{P}N_{\Delta_{0}\cup\Delta_{2}}q^{\prime}
.

We claim that
\Delta_{1}=\emptyset
. Since the maximal clique factor of
\Sigma_{1}
is empty, Lemma
2.16
(1) gives
M_{\Sigma_{1}}^{\prime}\cap pPp=M_{\Sigma_{1}^{\perp}}
.
Since
M_{\Sigma_{1}}p^{\prime\prime}\prec^{s}_{P}N_{\Delta_{0}\cup\Delta_{2}}q^{\prime}
, we have

M_{\Sigma_{1}}\prec_{P}N_{\Delta_{0}\cup\Delta_{2}}q^{\prime}
. Since we also have
N_{\Delta_{1}}q^{\prime}\subset(N_{\Delta_{0}\cup\Delta_{2}}q^{\prime})^{\prime}\cap q^{\prime}Pq^{\prime}
, these facts and Proposition
2.4
(1) imply that
N_{\Delta_{1}}q^{\prime}\prec_{P}M_{\Sigma_{1}^{\perp}}
. By Proposition
2.4
(3) we find a nonzero projection
q^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}(N_{\Delta_{1}}^{\prime}\cap qPq)q^{\prime}
such that
N_{\Delta_{1}}q^{\prime\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\Sigma_{1}^{\perp}}
. Since
\Sigma\subset\Sigma_{1}\cup\Sigma_{1}^{\perp}
, by maximality we have
\Sigma=\Sigma_{1}\cup\Sigma_{1}^{\perp}
, and therefore
\Sigma_{1}^{\perp}=\Sigma_{0}
. So we have
N_{\Delta_{1}}q^{\prime\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\Sigma_{0}}
. Since
\Sigma_{0}
is a clique,
M_{\Sigma_{0}}
is amenable and so
N_{\Delta_{1}}q^{\prime\prime}
is amenable.
By Lemma
2.16
(3), this forces
\Delta_{1}
to be a clique. Since the maximal clique factor of
\Delta_{1}
is trivial, we conclude that indeed
\Delta_{1}=\emptyset
.

Further, by (
8.1
), we derive that
M_{\Sigma_{0}}p^{\prime\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\Delta_{0}}q^{\prime}
. In particular, since
\Sigma_{0}\not=\emptyset
, we get that
\Delta_{0}\not=\emptyset
. Moreover, we showed that for every nonzero projection
p_{0}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{\Sigma}^{\prime}\cap pPp)p^{\prime}
there is a nonzero projection
p^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{\Sigma}^{\prime}\cap pPp)p_{0}
such that
M_{\Sigma_{0}}p^{\prime\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\Delta_{0}}q^{\prime}
.

Finally, since
\Sigma\in\mathscr{J}(\Gamma)
and
\Sigma_{0}\subset\Sigma
is its maximal clique factor, we have
\Sigma^{\perp}=\emptyset
and
\Sigma_{0}^{\perp}=\Sigma\setminus\Sigma_{0}
. Lemma
2.16
(1) implies that
\mathcal{Z}(M_{\Sigma}^{\prime}\cap pMp)=\overline{\otimes}_{v\in\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{Z}(M_{v})=\mathcal{Z}(M_{\Sigma_{0}}^{\prime}\cap pPp)
.
The previous paragraph (combined with Remark
2.2
(5)) thus implies that
M_{\Sigma_{0}}p^{\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\Delta_{0}}q^{\prime}
. By symmetry, we also get
N_{\Delta_{0}}q^{\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\Sigma_{0}}p^{\prime}
, which finishes the proof.
∎

We say that a complete subgraph
C\subset\Gamma
is
untransvectable
if there is no vertex
w\notin C
such that
C^{\perp}\subset\mathrm{st}(w)
. We recall from Notation
6.3
that
\mathscr{C}(\Gamma)
denotes the set of all complete subgraphs of
\Gamma
. We let
\mathscr{C}^{u}(\Gamma)
be the set of all untransvectable complete subgraphs of
\Gamma
.

Lemma 8.4
.

Let
\Lambda
be a finite simple graph, and let
\Delta\in\mathscr{J}(\Lambda)
be a maximal join subgraph, and write
\Delta=C\circ\Delta_{1}\circ\dots\circ\Delta_{n}
, where
C\subset\Delta
is its maximal clique factor, and the subgraphs
\Delta_{i}
are irreducible with at least two vertices.

(1)

If
C\neq\emptyset
and
\Delta\not=C
(i.e.,
\Delta
is not a clique),
then
C\in\mathscr{C}^{u}(\Lambda)
.

(2)

If
C=\emptyset
, and if
D\subset\Delta_{1}
is a complete subgraph such that
D\in\mathscr{C}^{u}(\Delta_{1})
, then
D\in\mathscr{C}^{u}(\Lambda)
.

Proof.

For (1), assume towards a contradiction that
C^{\perp}\subset\mathrm{st}(w)
for some vertex
w\notin C
. Then
w
cannot belong to one of the subgraphs
\Delta_{i}
because
\Delta_{i}
is not contained in
\mathrm{st}(w)
. So
w\notin\Delta
. Since
\Delta_{1}\circ\dots\circ\Delta_{n}=\Delta\setminus C\not=\emptyset
, it follows that
(C\cup\{w\})\circ\Delta_{1}\circ\dots\circ\Delta_{n}
is a join subgraph, contradicting the maximality of
\Delta
.

For (2), assume towards a contradiction that
D^{\perp}\subset\mathrm{st}(w)
for some
w\notin D
. Since
D\in\mathscr{C}^{u}(\Delta_{1})
, we have
w\notin\Delta_{1}
. We also have
\Delta_{2}\cup\dots\cup\Delta_{m}\subset\mathrm{st}(w)
. As the subgraphs
\Delta_{i}
are irreducible, we cannot have
w\in\Delta_{i}
, for every
2\leq i\leq n
. It follows that
(\Delta_{1}\cup\{w\})\circ\Delta_{2}\circ\dots\circ\Delta_{m}
is a join subgraph, contradicting the maximality of
\Delta
.
∎

Proposition 8.5
.

In the setting from Notation
6.1
, assume additionally that
(M_{v},\tau_{v})
and
(N_{w},\tau_{w})
are amenable, for every
v\in\Gamma
and
w\in\Lambda
.
Let
v\in\Gamma^{u}
.

Then we can find projections
r_{C}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{v}^{\prime}\cap pPp),s_{C}\in\mathcal{Z}(N_{C}^{\prime}\cap qPq)
, for every
C\in\mathscr{C}^{u}(\Lambda)
, such that
M_{v}r_{C}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{C}s_{C}
and
N_{C}s_{C}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{v}r_{C}
, for every
C\in\mathscr{C}^{u}(\Lambda)
, and
\sum_{C\in\mathscr{C}^{u}(\Lambda)}r_{C}=p
.

Proof.

We will prove the conclusion by induction on
|\Gamma|
.
If
|\Gamma|=1
, then
\Gamma=\{v\}
. Since
pPp=M_{v}
is amenable we deduce that
P
and thus
N_{\Lambda}
has an amenable direct summand. Lemma
2.16
(3) gives that
\Lambda
is a clique. Since by Notation
6.1
,
M_{\Gamma}=M_{v}
and
N_{\Lambda}
are II
1
factors, the statement holds trivially by letting
r_{\Lambda}=p
and
r_{\Lambda^{\prime}}=0
, for every full subgraph
\Lambda^{\prime}\subsetneq\Lambda
.
To prove the inductive step, suppose that the statement holds whenever
|\Gamma|\leq d
, for a fixed
d\geq 1
.
Assume that
|\Gamma|=d+1\geq 2
.

Since
v
is untransvectable,
\text{lk}(v)\not=\emptyset
, for otherwise
\text{lk}(v)\subset\text{st}(v^{\prime})
, for every
v^{\prime}\in\Gamma\setminus\{v\}
. Thus,
\text{st}(v)\subset\Gamma
is a join subgraph.
Let
\Sigma\subset\Gamma
be a maximal join full subgraph which contains
\text{st}(v)
.
If
v^{\prime}\in\text{lk}(v)
, then
\text{lk}(v)\not\subset\text{st}(v^{\prime})
, and thus
\text{st}(v)
is not a clique.
Hence,
\Sigma
is not a clique and so
M_{\Sigma}
has no amenable direct summand by Lemma
2.16
(3).
In particular,
M_{\Sigma}\nprec_{P}N_{w}
, for every
w\in\Lambda
.

Moreover, if
\Sigma^{\prime}\subset\Gamma
is a full subgraph such that
M_{\Sigma^{\prime}}
is not amenable, then
\Sigma^{\prime}
is not a clique, hence
M_{\Sigma^{\prime}}
has no amenable direct summand and thus
M_{\Sigma^{\prime}}\nprec_{P}N_{w}
for every
w\in\Lambda
. Similarly, if
\Delta^{\prime}\subset\Lambda
is a full subgraph such that
N_{\Delta^{\prime}}
is not amenable, then
\Delta^{\prime}
is not a clique, hence
N_{\Delta^{\prime}}
has no amenable direct summand and thus
N_{\Delta^{\prime}}\nprec_{P}M_{v^{\prime}}
for every
v^{\prime}\in\Gamma
.

By applying Proposition
6.4
we find projections
r_{\Delta}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{\Sigma}^{\prime}\cap pPp)
,
s_{\Delta}\in\mathcal{Z}(N_{\Delta}^{\prime}\cap qPq)
such that
M_{\Sigma}r_{\Delta}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\Delta}s_{\Delta}
and
N_{\Delta}s_{\Delta}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\Sigma}r_{\Delta}
, for every
\Delta\in\mathscr{J}(\Lambda)
, and
\sum_{\Delta\in\mathscr{J}(\Lambda)}r_{\Delta}=p
.
Specifically, in the context of Proposition
6.4
, we note that
p_{\Sigma}=p
and denote
r_{\Delta}:=p_{\Sigma,\Delta}
and
s_{\Delta}:=q_{\Delta,\Sigma}
. Note that
r_{\Delta}=0
if and only if
s_{\Delta}=0
.

Let
\Sigma_{0}
be the maximal clique factor of
\Sigma
. We continue by treating separately two cases.

Case 1.

\Sigma_{0}\not=\emptyset
.

Let
\Delta\in\mathscr{J}(\Lambda)
such that
r_{\Delta}\not=0
.
Since
M_{\Sigma}
has no amenable direct summand and
M_{\Sigma}r_{\Delta}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\Delta}s_{\Delta}
, we get that
N_{\Delta}
is not amenable, thus
\Delta
is not a clique.
Let
C_{\Delta}\in\mathscr{C}(\Lambda)
be the maximal clique factor of
\Delta
. Then
C_{\Delta}\in\mathscr{C}^{u}(\Lambda)
by Lemma
8.4
(1).
Since
M_{\Sigma}r_{\Delta}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\Delta}s_{\Delta}
and
N_{\Delta}s_{\Delta}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\Sigma}r_{\Delta}
, applying Proposition
8.3
gives that
C_{\Delta}\not=\emptyset
,

M_{\Sigma_{0}}r_{\Delta}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{C_{\Delta}}s_{\Delta}
and
N_{C_{\Delta}}s_{\Delta}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\Sigma_{0}}r_{\Delta}
.
As
\Sigma\subset\Gamma
is a maximal join full subgraph, we have
\Sigma^{\perp}=\emptyset
and
\Sigma_{0}^{\perp}=\Sigma\setminus\Sigma_{0}
. By Lemma
2.16
(1) we get

\mathcal{Z}(M_{\Sigma}^{\prime}\cap pPp)=\overline{\otimes}_{v^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{Z}(M_{v^{\prime}})=\mathcal{Z}(M_{\Sigma_{0}}^{\prime}\cap pPp).

Similarly,
\mathcal{Z}(N_{\Delta}^{\prime}\cap qPq)=\mathcal{Z}(N_{C_{\Delta}}^{\prime}\cap qPq)
. Hence,

r_{\Delta}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{\Sigma_{0}}^{\prime}\cap pPp)
and
s_{\Delta}\in\mathcal{Z}(N_{C_{\Delta}}^{\prime}\cap qPq)
.

Finally, for every
v^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{0}
, we have
\text{st}(v)\subset\Sigma\subset\text{st}(v^{\prime})
. Since
v
is untransvectable, we get that
v^{\prime}=v
, and thus
\Sigma_{0}=\{v\}
.
Note that if
\Delta,\Delta^{\prime}\in\mathscr{J}(\Lambda)
satisfy
\Delta\not=\Delta^{\prime}
, then
C_{\Delta}\not=C_{\Delta^{\prime}}
. Indeed, if
C_{\Delta}=C_{\Delta^{\prime}}
, then
\Delta\cup\Delta^{\prime}=C_{\Delta}\circ((\Delta\cup\Delta^{\prime})\setminus C_{\Delta})
would be a join subgraph of
\Lambda
which strictly contains
\Delta
, contradicting that
\Delta\in\mathscr{J}(\Lambda)
.

If
C\in\mathscr{C}^{u}(\Lambda)
, let
r_{C}=r_{\Delta}
, if
C=C_{\Delta}
, for some
\Delta\in\mathscr{J}(\Lambda)
(such a
\Delta
is unique by the previous paragraph), and let
r_{C}=0
, if
C
is not equal to any
C_{\Delta}
.
Since
\sum_{\Delta\in\mathscr{J}(\Lambda)}r_{\Delta}=p
, we get that
\sum_{C\in\mathscr{C}^{u}(\Lambda)}r_{C}=p
,
and the above analysis ensures that the conclusion of the proposition holds.

Case 2.

\Sigma_{0}=\emptyset
.

Let
\Delta\in\mathscr{J}(\Lambda)
with
r_{\Delta}\not=0
. Since
\Sigma_{0}=\emptyset
,
M_{\Sigma}r_{\Delta}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\Delta}s_{\Delta}
and
N_{\Delta}s_{\Delta}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\Sigma}r_{\Delta}
, Proposition
8.3
implies that
\Delta
has a trivial maximal clique factor. Since
\Sigma\subset\Gamma
and
\Delta\subset\Lambda
are maximal join full subgraphs,
\Sigma^{\perp}=\emptyset
and
\Delta^{\perp}=\emptyset
. Lemma
2.16
(1) implies that
M_{\Sigma}^{\prime}\cap pPp=\mathbb{C}p
and
N_{\Delta}^{\prime}\cap qPq=\mathbb{C}q
.
In particular,
M_{\Sigma}
and
N_{\Delta}
are II
1
factors.
Moreover, we derive that
r_{\Delta}=p
and
s_{\Delta}=q
. Hence,
M_{\Sigma}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\Delta}
and
N_{\Delta}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\Sigma}
. By combining Proposition
2.4
(2)
and Lemma
2.16
(2), this gives that
M_{\Sigma}\nprec_{P}N_{\Delta^{\prime}}
and
N_{\Delta}\nprec_{P}M_{\Sigma^{\prime}}
, for any full subgraphs
\Sigma^{\prime}\subsetneq\Sigma
and
\Delta^{\prime}\subsetneq\Delta
.

Let
P_{0}\subset P
be a subfactor such that
p\in P_{0}
and
pP_{0}p=M_{\Sigma}
. Then we can identify
P_{0}=M_{\Sigma}^{t}
, where
t=(\text{Tr}\otimes\tau)(p)^{-1}
and
\text{Tr}:\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
is the non-normalized trace.
By Theorem
3.14
we get unitaries
a,b\in P
such that
aP_{0}a^{*}\subset\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes N_{\Delta}
and
b(\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes N_{\Delta})b^{*}\subset P_{0}
. Thus,
(ab)(\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes N_{\Delta})(ab)^{*}\subset\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes N_{\Delta}
, which by Proposition
3.9
implies that
ab\in\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes N_{\Delta}
. This gives that
(ab)(\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes N_{\Delta})(ab)^{*}=\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes N_{\Delta}
, which implies that
aP_{0}a^{*}=\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes N_{\Delta}
.

Since
\Sigma
and
\Delta
are join graphs with trivial maximal clique factors, we can decompose them as
\Sigma=\Sigma_{1}\circ\cdots\circ\Sigma_{k}
and
\Delta=\Delta_{1}\circ\cdots\circ\Delta_{m}
, where
\Sigma_{1},\dots,\Sigma_{k},\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m}
are irreducible graphs, not reduced to a vertex, for some
k,m\geq 2
.
Thus,
M_{\Sigma}=\overline{\otimes}_{i=1}^{k}M_{\Sigma_{i}}
and
N_{\Delta}=\overline{\otimes}_{j=1}^{m}N_{\Delta_{j}}
.

Since
P_{0}=M_{\Sigma}^{t}
and
aP_{0}a^{*}=\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes N_{\Delta}
, applying Theorem
I
gives that
k=m
and there exist a unitary
\xi\in\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes N_{\Delta}
and decompositions
P_{0}=\overline{\otimes}_{i=1}^{k}M_{\Sigma_{i}}^{s_{i}}
and
\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes N_{\Delta}=\overline{\otimes}_{j=1}^{k}N_{\Delta_{j}}^{t_{j}}
, for some
s_{1},\dots,s_{k},t_{1},\dots,t_{k}>0
with
s_{1}\cdots s_{k}=t
and
t_{1}\cdots t_{k}=n
, such that modulo a permutation of
\{1,\dots,k\}
we have

(8.2)

(\xi a)M_{\Sigma_{i}}^{s_{i}}(\xi a)^{*}=N_{\Delta_{i}}^{t_{i}}
, for every
1\leq i\leq k
.

Next, assume without loss of generality that
v\in\Sigma_{1}
.
We claim that
v
is untransvectable in
\Sigma_{1}
. Indeed, assume that
\text{lk}(v)\cap\Sigma_{1}\subset\text{st}(v^{\prime})\cap\Sigma_{1}
, for some
v^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{1}
.
Since
\text{st}(v)\subset\Sigma
, we have
\text{lk}(v)=(\text{lk}(v)\cap\Sigma_{1})\cup(\Sigma\setminus\Sigma_{1})
. Since we also have
(\text{st}(v^{\prime})\cap\Sigma_{1})\cup(\Sigma\setminus\Sigma_{1})\subset\mathrm{st}(v^{\prime})
, it follows that
\text{lk}(v)\subset\text{st}(v^{\prime})
. Since
v
is untransvectable in
\Gamma
, we get
v^{\prime}=v
, which proves our claim.

Since
\Sigma_{1}\subsetneq\Sigma
, we have
\Sigma_{1}\subsetneq\Gamma
and thus
|\Sigma_{1}|\leq d
. Let
\kappa=t_{1}/s_{1}>0
. By the induction assumption, the conclusion of the proposition holds for the
*
-isomorphism
M_{\Sigma_{1}}\cong N_{\Delta_{1}}^{\kappa}
induced by the equality
(\xi a)M_{\Sigma_{1}}^{s_{1}}(\xi a)^{*}=N_{\Delta_{1}}^{t_{1}}
given by (
8.2
).
This implies that we can find projections
r_{C}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{v}^{\prime}\cap M_{\Sigma_{1}}),s_{C}\in\mathcal{Z}(N_{C}^{\prime}\cap N_{\Delta_{1}})
such that
M_{v}r_{C}\prec^{s}_{P}N_{C}s_{C}
and
N_{C}s_{C}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{v}r_{C}
, for every
C\in\mathscr{C}^{u}(\Delta_{1})
, and
\sum_{C\in\mathscr{C}^{u}(\Delta_{1})}r_{C}=p
. Notice also that whenever
C\in\mathscr{C}^{u}(\Delta_{1})
, we also have
C\in\mathscr{C}^{u}(\Lambda)
by Lemma
8.4
(2).

Finally, since
v
is untransvectable in
\Gamma
, we get that
\text{lk}(v)
has an empty maximal clique factor. Lemma
2.16
(1) implies that
\mathcal{Z}(M_{v}^{\prime}\cap M_{\Gamma})=\mathcal{Z}(M_{v})
. Since
v
is untransvectable in
\Sigma_{1}
, we also have that
\mathcal{Z}(M_{v}^{\prime}\cap M_{\Sigma_{1}})=\mathcal{Z}(M_{v})
. This allows us to derive that
r_{C}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{v}^{\prime}\cap M_{\Gamma})
, for every
C\in\mathscr{C}^{u}(\Delta_{1})
. In the same way, since the clique
C
is untransvectable in
\Lambda
, the subgraph
C^{\perp}
has an empty maximal clique factor, so
\mathcal{Z}(N_{C}^{\prime}\cap N_{\Lambda})=\bar{\otimes}_{w\in C}\mathcal{Z}(N_{w})
. Likewise, since
C
is untransvectable in
\Delta_{1}
we have
\mathcal{Z}(N_{C}^{\prime}\cap N_{\Delta_{1}})=\overline{\otimes}_{w\in C}\mathcal{Z}(N_{w})
. So
s_{C}\in\mathcal{Z}(N_{C}^{\prime}\cap N_{\Lambda})
, for every
C\in\mathscr{C}^{u}(\Delta_{1})
, which completes our proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
8.1
.

Let
v\in\Gamma
be an untransvectable vertex. By applying Proposition
8.5
we find projections
r_{v,C}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{v}^{\prime}\cap pPp),s_{C,v}\in\mathcal{Z}(N_{C}^{\prime}\cap qPq)
such that
M_{v}r_{v,C}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{C}s_{C,v}
and
N_{C}s_{C,v}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{v}r_{v,C}
, for every
C\in\mathscr{C}^{u}(\Lambda)
, and
\sum_{C\in\mathscr{C}^{u}(\Lambda)}r_{v,C}=p
.

If
C\in\mathscr{C}^{u}(\Lambda)
is such that
r_{v,C}\not=0
, then
C
is collapsible by Proposition
3.17
. Since
\Lambda
is clique-reduced,
C
must consist of a single vertex. Since
C
is untransvectable, this vertex is untransvectable. Thus, we have projections
r_{v,w}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{v}^{\prime}\cap pPp),s_{w,v}\in\mathcal{Z}(N_{w}^{\prime}\cap qPq)
such that
M_{v}r_{v,w}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{w}s_{w,v}
and
N_{w}s_{w,v}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{v}r_{v,w}
, for every
w\in\Lambda^{u}
, and
\sum_{w\in\Lambda^{u}}r_{v,w}=p
.

Assume that we are in case (1), that is,
\Gamma^{u}
and
\Lambda^{u}
are clique-reduced. Let
v\in\Gamma^{u}
. In order to apply Proposition
6.8
and derive the conclusion, it will be enough to argue that
\sum_{v\in\Gamma^{u}}s_{w,v}=q
, for every
w\in\Lambda^{u}
.
Towards this goal, we first note that by symmetry, for every
w\in\Lambda^{u}
, we find projections
r_{v,w}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}(M_{v}^{\prime}\cap pPp),s_{w,v}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}(N_{w}^{\prime}\cap qPq)
such that
M_{v}r_{v,w}^{\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{w}s_{w,v}^{\prime}
and
N_{w}s_{w,v}^{\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{v}r_{v,w}^{\prime}
, for every
v\in\Gamma^{u}
, and
\sum_{v\in\Gamma^{u}}s_{w,v}^{\prime}=q
.

We claim that
r_{v,w}^{\prime}\leq r_{v,w}
, for every
v\in\Gamma^{u}
and
w\in\Lambda^{u}
. Let
v\in\Gamma^{u}
and
w\in\Lambda^{u}
. Since
r_{v,w}^{\prime}\leq p
and
p=\sum_{w\in\Lambda^{u}}r_{v,w}
, in order to justify the claim it suffices to show that
r_{v,w}^{\prime}r_{v,w^{\prime}}=0
, for every
w^{\prime}\in\Lambda^{u}\setminus\{w\}
. Assume by contradiction that
r:=r_{v,w}^{\prime}r_{v,w^{\prime}}\not=0
, for some
w^{\prime}\in\Lambda^{u}\setminus\{w\}
. Since
r\leq r_{v,w}^{\prime}
, Lemma
6.6
implies that
N_{w}s_{w,v}^{\prime}\prec_{P}M_{v}r
. Since
r\leq r_{v,w^{\prime}}
, we also have
M_{v}r\prec_{P}^{s}N_{w^{\prime}}s_{w^{\prime},v}
, and
s_{w^{\prime},v}\neq 0
because
r_{v,w^{\prime}}\neq 0
. Combining these facts and Proposition
2.4
(2) gives
N_{w}s_{w,v}^{\prime}\prec_{P}N_{w^{\prime}}s_{w^{\prime},v}
. By Lemma
2.16
(2), this implies that
\{w\}\subset\{w^{\prime}\}
, which contradicts the fact that
w^{\prime}\not=w
, and proves the claim.

Similarly, we deduce that
s_{w,v}\leq s_{w,v}^{\prime}
, for every
v\in\Gamma^{u}
and
w\in\Lambda^{u}
.

Let
v\in\Gamma^{u}
and
w\in\Lambda^{u}
.
Since
N_{w}s_{w,v}^{\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{v}r_{v,w}^{\prime}
,
r_{v,w}^{\prime}\leq r_{v,w}
and thus
M_{v}r_{v,w}^{\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{v}r_{v,w}
, and
M_{v}r_{v,w}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{w}s_{w,v}
, by applying Proposition
2.4
(2) we deduce that
N_{w}s_{w,v}^{\prime}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{w}s_{w,v}
. By Corollary
3.13
we derive that
s_{w,v}^{\prime}\leq s_{w,v}
. Since
s_{w,v}\leq s_{w,v}^{\prime}
, we conclude that
s_{w,v}=s_{w,v}^{\prime}
.
Therefore,
\sum_{v\in\Gamma^{u}}s_{w,v}=\sum_{v\in\Gamma^{u}}s_{w,v}^{\prime}=q
, for every
w\in\Lambda^{u}
,
and
Proposition
6.8
gives the conclusion.

Now, assume that we are in case (2), that is,
M_{v}
and
N_{w}
are II
1
factors, for every
v\in\Gamma
and
w\in\Lambda
. Then Lemma
2.16
(1) implies that
\mathcal{Z}(M_{v}^{\prime}\cap pPp)=\mathbb{C}p
and
\mathcal{Z}(N_{w}^{\prime}\cap qPq)=\mathbb{C}q
, and therefore
r_{v,w}\in\{0,p\}
and
s_{w,v}\in\{0,q\}
, for every
v\in\Gamma^{u}
and
w\in\Lambda^{u}
. So for every
v\in\Gamma^{u}
there exists a (unique) vertex
\alpha(v)\in\Lambda^{u}
such that
M_{v}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{\alpha(v)}
and
N_{\alpha(v)}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{v}
. By symmetry, for every
w\in\Lambda^{u}
there exists a unique vertex
\beta(w)\in\Gamma^{u}
such that
N_{w}\prec_{P}^{s}M_{\beta(w)}
and
M_{\beta(w)}\prec_{P}^{s}N_{w}
. It then follows from Lemma
2.16
(2) that for every
v\in\Gamma^{u}
, we have
\beta\circ\alpha(v)=v
, and that for every
w\in\Lambda^{u}
, we have
\beta\circ\alpha(w)=w
. By Proposition
6.8
(2), the conclusion now follows in case (2).
∎

9.
II
1
factor vertex algebras: Proof of Theorem
C

In this section, we prove the following theorem, which is the main part of Theorem
C
(the moreover part of Theorem
C
will be a consequence of Lemma
10.1
(1) below).

Theorem 9.1
.

Let
\Gamma,\Lambda
be finite simple graphs, and assume that every connected component of
\Gamma
and
\Lambda
is strongly reduced, transvection-free,
and not reduced to one vertex. Let
(M_{v})_{v\in\Gamma}
and
(N_{w})_{w\in\Lambda}
be families of II
1
factors, and let
M_{\Gamma}=\ast_{v,\Gamma}M_{v}
and
N_{\Lambda}=\ast_{w,\Lambda}N_{w}
. Let
t>0
.

If
\theta:M_{\Gamma}\to N_{\Lambda}^{t}
is any
*
-isomorphism,
then
\Gamma
and
\Lambda
are isomorphic and there exists a graph isomorphism
\alpha:\Gamma\to\Lambda
such that
\theta(M_{v})\prec_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}N_{\alpha(v)}^{t}
and
N_{\alpha(v)}^{t}\prec_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}^{s}\theta(M_{v})
, for every
v\in\Gamma
.

Here and after, given II
1
factors
R\subset Q
and
t>0
, we consider the usual inclusion
R^{t}\subset Q^{t}
.

The proof of Theorem
9.1
relies on the following technical result.

Proposition 9.2
.

Let
\Gamma,\Lambda
be two finite simple graphs, and
(M_{v})_{v\in\Gamma}
,
(N_{w})_{w\in\Lambda}
be families of II
1
factors. Let
M_{\Gamma}=*_{v,\Gamma}M_{v}
and
N_{\Lambda}=*_{w,\Lambda}N_{w}
be the associated graph product II
1
factors, and
\theta:M_{\Gamma}\rightarrow N_{\Lambda}^{t}
a
*
-isomorphism, for some
t>0
. Let
v\in\Gamma
be a vertex.
Assume that the following three conditions hold:

(1)

v
is untransvectable.

(2)

There is no full subgraph
\Delta\subset\Lambda
such that
|\Delta|\geq 2
,
N_{\Delta}^{t}\prec_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}\theta(M_{v})
and
\theta(M_{v})\prec_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}N_{\Delta\cup\Delta^{\perp}}^{t}
.

(3)

There are no vertex
w\in\Lambda
and full subgraph
\Sigma\subset\Gamma
such that
|\Sigma|\geq 2
,
\theta(M_{\Sigma})\prec_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}N_{w}^{t}
and
N_{w}^{t}\prec_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}\theta(M_{\Sigma\cup\Sigma^{\perp}})
.

Then there exists a vertex
w\in\Lambda
such that
\theta(M_{v})\prec_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}N_{w}^{t}
.

Before proving Proposition
9.2
, we first show that it implies Theorem
9.1
.

Proof of Theorem
9.1
.

We note first that by Lemma
2.16
(1) we have
\mathcal{Z}(M_{\Sigma}^{\prime}\cap M_{\Gamma})=\mathbb{C}1
and
\mathcal{Z}(N_{\Delta}^{\prime}\cap N_{\Lambda})=\mathbb{C}1
, for any nonempty full subgraphs
\Sigma\subset\Gamma
and
\Delta\subset\Lambda
.

Claim 9.3
.

There are no vertex
v\in\Gamma
and full subgraph
\Delta\subset\Lambda
such that
|\Delta|\geq 2
,
N_{\Delta}^{t}\prec_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}\theta(M_{v})
and
\theta(M_{v})\prec_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}N_{\Delta\cup\Delta^{\perp}}^{t}
.

Proof.

Assume by contradiction that there exist
v\in\Gamma
and a full subgraph
\Delta\subset\Lambda
such that
|\Delta|\geq 2
,
N_{\Delta}^{t}\prec_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}\theta(M_{v})
and
\theta(M_{v})\prec_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}N_{\Delta\cup\Delta^{\perp}}^{t}
.
Since
\mathcal{Z}(\theta(M_{v})^{\prime}\cap N_{\Lambda}^{t})=\mathbb{C}1
and
\mathcal{Z}((N_{\Delta}^{t})^{\prime}\cap N_{\Lambda}^{t})=\mathbb{C}1
, Proposition
2.4
(3) gives
N_{\Delta}^{t}\prec_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}^{s}\theta(M_{v})
and
\theta(M_{v})\prec^{s}_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}N_{\Delta\cup\Delta^{\perp}}^{t}
. By Proposition
3.17
we deduce that
\Delta\subset\Lambda
is a collapsible subgraph and
\theta(M_{\text{st}(v)})\prec^{s}_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}N_{\Delta\cup\Delta^{\perp}}^{t}
.

Since
\Delta
is collapsible and the connected components of
\Lambda
are strongly reduced, Lemma
2.13
ensures that
\Delta
is a union of connected components of
\Lambda
.
In particular,
\Delta^{\perp}=\emptyset
. Then
N_{\Delta}^{t}\prec_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}^{s}\theta(M_{v})
and
\theta(M_{\text{st}(v)})\prec^{s}_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}N_{\Delta}^{t}
. By Proposition
2.4
(2) we get that
\theta(M_{\text{st}(v)})\prec_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}\theta(M_{v})
, thus
M_{\text{st}(v)}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{v}
. However, by Lemma
2.16
(2) this implies that
\text{st}(v)\subset\{v\}
. Hence,
\text{lk}(v)=\emptyset
, so
v
is isolated, contradicting our assumption that no connected component of
\Gamma
is reduced to one vertex.
∎

By symmetry, the analogue of Claim
9.3
holds if we swap the roles of
\Gamma
and
\Lambda
. Thus, there are no vertex
w\in\Lambda
and full subgraph
\Sigma\subset\Gamma
such that
|\Sigma|\geq 2
,
\theta(M_{\Sigma})\prec_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}N_{w}^{t}
and
N_{w}^{t}\prec_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}\theta(M_{\Sigma\cup\Sigma^{\perp}})
.

Since by assumption all vertices of
\Gamma
and
\Lambda
are untransvectable, we can apply Proposition
9.2
and get maps
\alpha:\Gamma\rightarrow\Lambda
and
\beta:\Lambda\rightarrow\Gamma
such that
\theta(M_{v})\prec_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}N_{\alpha(v)}^{t}
and
N_{w}^{t}\prec_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}\theta(M_{\beta(w)})
, for every
v\in\Gamma
and
w\in\Lambda
. Since
\mathcal{Z}(\theta(M_{v})^{\prime}\cap N_{\Delta}^{t})=\mathcal{Z}((N_{w}^{t})^{\prime}\cap N_{\Lambda}^{t})=\mathbb{C}1
, Proposition
2.4
(3) further gives that

(9.1)

\theta(M_{v})\prec_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}^{s}N_{\alpha(v)}^{t}
and
N_{w}^{t}\prec_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}^{s}\theta(M_{\beta(w)})
, for every
v\in\Gamma
and
w\in\Lambda
.

Combining (
9.1
) with Proposition
2.4
(2) gives that
\theta(M_{v})\prec_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}\theta(M_{\beta(\alpha(v))})
, hence
M_{v}\prec_{M_{\Gamma}}M_{\beta(\alpha(v))}
, for every
v\in\Gamma
. By Lemma
2.16
(2) we deduce that
\beta(\alpha(v))=v
, for every
v\in\Gamma
. Similarly, we deduce that
\alpha(\beta(w))=w
, for every
w\in\Lambda
. Therefore,
\alpha:\Gamma\rightarrow\Lambda
is a bijection and
\beta=\alpha^{-1}
.

The fact that
\alpha
is a graph isomorphism is now a direct consequence of Proposition
6.8
(2), but let us also provide a short direct argument. Let
v\in\Gamma
and
v^{\prime}\in\text{lk}(v)
. Since
\theta(M_{v})\prec_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}^{s}N_{\alpha(v)}^{t}
, Proposition
3.9
(2) implies that
\theta(M_{\text{st}(v)})\prec_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}^{s}N_{\text{st}(\alpha(v))}^{t}
. In particular,
\theta(M_{v^{\prime}})\prec_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}^{s}N_{\text{st}(\alpha(v))}^{t}
. Since
N_{\alpha(v^{\prime})}^{t}\prec_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}^{s}\theta(M_{v^{\prime}})
, Proposition
2.4
(2) gives that
N_{\alpha(v^{\prime})}^{t}\prec_{N_{\Lambda}^{t}}^{s}N_{\text{st}(\alpha(v))}^{t}
. By Lemma
2.16
(2) we deduce that
\alpha(v^{\prime})\in\text{st}(\alpha(v))
. Since
\alpha(v^{\prime})\not=\alpha(v)
, we get that
\alpha(v^{\prime})\in\text{lk}(\alpha(v))
. Similarly, if
w\in\Lambda
and
w^{\prime}\in\text{lk}(w)
, then
\alpha^{-1}(w^{\prime})\in\text{lk}(\alpha^{-1}(w)))
. This shows that
\alpha
is a graph isomorphism, which finishes the proof.
∎

Proof of Proposition
9.2
.

Denote
L=N_{\Lambda}^{t}
and identify
M_{\Gamma}=L
, via
\theta
. By Lemma
2.16
(1) we have
\mathcal{Z}(M_{\Sigma}^{\prime}\cap M_{\Gamma})=\mathbb{C}1
and
\mathcal{Z}(N_{\Delta}^{\prime}\cap N_{\Lambda})=\mathbb{C}1
, for any nonempty full subgraphs
\Sigma\subset\Gamma
and
\Delta\subset\Lambda
. This implies that if
M_{\Sigma}\prec_{L}N_{\Delta}^{t}
, then
M_{\Sigma}\prec_{L}^{s}N_{\Delta}^{t}
, and if
N_{\Delta}^{t}\prec_{L}M_{\Sigma}
, then
N_{\Delta}^{t}\prec_{L}^{s}M_{\Sigma}
.

We will prove the conclusion by induction on
|\Gamma|
. If
|\Gamma|=1
, then
\Gamma=\{v\}
and
L=M_{v}
. Condition (2) of the hypothesis implies that
|\Lambda|=1
, and the conclusion follows trivially.
To prove the inductive step, suppose that the conclusion holds if
|\Gamma|\leq d
, for a fixed integer
d\geq 1
.
We will prove that the conclusion holds if
|\Gamma|=d+1\geq 2
.

We claim that since
v
is untransvectable,
\text{st}(v)\subset\Gamma
is a join subgraph that is not a clique.
Indeed,
\text{lk}(v)\not=\emptyset
, since otherwise
\text{lk}(v)\subset\text{st}(v^{\prime})
, for every
v^{\prime}\in\Gamma\setminus\{v\}
. Thus,
\text{st}(v)=\{v\}\circ\text{lk}(v)
is a join graph.
Moreover,
\text{st}(v)
is not a clique because otherwise
\text{lk}(v)\subset\text{st}(v)\subset\text{st}(v^{\prime})
, for every
v^{\prime}\in\text{lk}(v)
.

Let
\Sigma\subset\Gamma
be a maximal join full subgraph containing
\text{st}(v)
, i.e., a join full subgraph which contains
\text{st}(v)
and is not a proper subgraph of any join full subgraph of
\Gamma
. Then
\Sigma
is not a clique, and thus
M_{\Sigma}
is nonamenable by Lemma
2.16
(3).
If
M_{\Sigma}\prec_{P}N_{w}^{t}
, for some vertex
w\in\Lambda
, then
M_{v}\prec_{P}N_{w}^{t}
and the conclusion holds.
We may therefore assume that
M_{\Sigma}\nprec_{P}N_{w}^{t}
, for every vertex
w\in\Lambda
.

By Corollary
4.5
, we can find a join full subgraph
\Delta\subset\Lambda
such that
M_{\Sigma}\prec_{L}N_{\Delta}^{t}
and thus
M_{\Sigma}\prec_{L}^{s}N_{\Delta}^{t}
. Moreover, we may assume that
\Delta\subset\Lambda
is a maximal join full subgraph.
We next claim that
N_{\Delta}^{t}\nprec_{L}M_{v^{\prime}}
, for every
v^{\prime}\in\Gamma
. If
N_{\Delta}^{t}\prec_{L}M_{v^{\prime}}
, for some
v^{\prime}\in\Gamma
, then
N_{\Delta}^{t}\prec_{L}^{s}M_{v^{\prime}}
. Since
M_{\Sigma}\prec_{L}N_{\Delta}^{t}
, Proposition
2.4
(2) implies that
M_{\Sigma}\prec_{L}M_{v^{\prime}}
. By Lemma
2.16
(2) this entails that
\Sigma\subset\{v^{\prime}\}
, which gives a contradiction and proves the claim.

Since
M_{\Sigma}\prec_{L}^{s}N_{\Delta}^{t}
and
M_{\Sigma}
is nonamenable,
N_{\Delta}
is nonamenable.
Since
N_{\Delta}
is nonamenable, the claim from the previous paragraph and Corollary
4.5
together yield a join full subgraph
\Sigma^{\prime}\subset\Gamma
such that
N_{\Delta}^{t}\prec_{L}M_{\Sigma^{\prime}}
. By reasoning as above, we get that
N_{\Delta}^{t}\prec_{L}^{s}M_{\Sigma^{\prime}}
and
\Sigma\subset\Sigma^{\prime}
. The maximality of
\Sigma
implies that
\Sigma=\Sigma^{\prime}
.
In conclusion, we found a maximal join full subgraph
\Delta\subset\Lambda
with

(9.2)

M_{\Sigma}\prec_{L}^{s}N_{\Delta}^{t}
and
N_{\Delta}^{t}\prec_{L}^{s}M_{\Sigma}
.

We continue by showing that
M_{\Sigma}\nprec_{L}N_{\Delta^{\prime}}^{t}
, for every full subgraph
\Delta^{\prime}\subsetneq\Delta
. Indeed, if we have
M_{\Sigma}\prec_{L}N_{\Delta^{\prime}}^{t}
, for a full subgraph
\Delta^{\prime}\subsetneq\Delta
, then
M_{\Sigma}\prec_{L}^{s}N_{\Delta^{\prime}}^{t}
. Since
N_{\Delta}^{t}\prec_{L}M_{\Sigma}
, Proposition
2.4
(2)implies that
N_{\Delta}^{t}\prec_{L}N_{\Delta^{\prime}}^{t}
, which by Lemma
2.16
(2) gives that
\Delta\subset\Delta^{\prime}
, a contradiction. Similarly, we get
N_{\Delta}^{t}\nprec_{L}M_{\Sigma^{\prime}}
, for every full subgraph
\Sigma^{\prime}\subsetneq\Sigma
.

Since
\Sigma^{\perp}=\Delta^{\perp}=\emptyset
, by combining (
9.2
), the last paragraph and Theorem
3.14
we find unitaries
u,v\in L
such that
uM_{\Sigma}u^{*}\subset N_{\Delta}^{t}
and
vN_{\Delta}^{t}v^{*}\subset M_{\Sigma}
. Thus,
(vu)M_{\Sigma}(vu)^{*}\subset vN_{\Delta}^{t}v^{*}\subset M_{\Sigma}
. By Proposition
3.9
(1) we get
vu\in M_{\Sigma}
and hence
vuM_{\Sigma}(vu)^{*}=M_{\Sigma}
. Therefore,
vN_{\Delta}^{t}v^{*}=M_{\Sigma}
. Hence, if we denote
Q=N_{\Delta}^{t}
, then, after unitary conjugacy, we may assume that
M_{\Sigma}=Q
.

Decompose
\Sigma=C\circ\Sigma_{1}\circ\cdots\circ\Sigma_{m}
, where
C
is the maximal clique factor of
\Sigma
and
\Sigma_{i}
is an irreducible graph with
|\Sigma_{i}|\geq 2
, for every
1\leq i\leq m
.
Similarly, decompose
\Delta=D\circ\Delta_{1}\circ\cdots\circ\Delta_{n}
, where
D
is the maximal clique factor of
\Delta
and
\Delta_{j}
is an irreducible graph with
|\Delta_{j}|\geq 2
, for every
1\leq j\leq n
. By Theorem
5.2
we have that
M_{\Sigma_{i}}
and
N_{\Delta_{j}}
are strongly prime II
1
factors, for every
1\leq i\leq m
and
1\leq j\leq n
.

We claim that

(9.3)

M_{\Sigma_{i}}\nprec_{Q}N_{D}^{t}
,     for every
1\leq i\leq m
.

This is clear if
D=\emptyset
, so we will assume otherwise. Assume by contradiction that
M_{\Sigma_{i}}\prec_{Q}N_{D}^{t}
, for some
1\leq i\leq m
. Since
Q=M_{\Sigma_{i}}\overline{\otimes}M_{\Sigma\setminus\Sigma_{i}}=N_{D}^{t}\overline{\otimes}N_{\Delta\setminus D}
,
[
OP04
, Proposition 12]
(see Lemma
2.8
(1)) gives a decomposition
Q=N_{D}^{s}\overline{\otimes}N_{\Delta\setminus D}^{t/s}
, for some
s>0
, such that after conjugacy with a unitary from
Q
we have
M_{\Sigma_{i}}\subset N_{D}^{s}
. Moreover, if we put
R=M_{\Sigma_{i}}^{\prime}\cap N_{D}^{s}
, then
N_{D}^{s}=M_{\Sigma_{i}}\overline{\otimes}R
by
[
Is20
, Lemma 3.3]
. Since
